 With boundless stores of grace.
IV
By thee my pray’rs acceptance gain,
 

 Although with sin defiled;
 

 Satan accuses me in vain,
 

 And I am own’d a child.
V
Jesus! my Shepherd, Husband, Friend,
 

 My Prophet, Priest, and King;
 

 My Lord, my life, my way, my end,
 

 Accept the praise I bring.
VI
Weak is the effort of my heart,
 

 And cold my warmest thought;
 

 But when I see thee as thou art
 

 I’ll praise thee as I ought.
VII
Till then I would thy love proclaim
 

 With every fleeting breath;
 

 And may the music of thy name
 

 Refresh my soul in death.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
雅歌 1:3。
一
耶稣之名在信徒耳中
听起来何等甘甜！
它抚平他的忧伤，医治他的伤口，
并驱走他的恐惧。
二
它使受伤的灵完整，
平静搅扰的胸怀；
它是饥饿灵魂的吗哪，
又是疲乏者的安息。
三
亲爱之名！我建造其上的磐石，
我的盾牌与藏身之处，
我永不失败的宝库，
装满无边恩典的库存。
四
借着你，我的祷告蒙悦纳，
虽然被罪玷污；
撒但控告我归于徒然，
我被承认为儿子。
五
耶稣！我的牧者、丈夫、朋友，
我的先知、祭司、君王；
我的主、我的生命、我的道路、我的终点，
求你悦纳我带来的赞美。
六
我的心何等软弱，
我最热的思想何等冷淡；
但当我见你本相，
我必照所当的赞美你。
七
直到那时，我愿以每口
流逝的气息传扬你的爱；
愿你名的音乐
在死亡中复苏我的灵魂。

## 58 O Lord, I Will Praise Thee / 58 主啊，我要赞美你

===Original===
By Cowper
Isaiah 12.
I
I will praise thee every day,
 

 Now thine anger’s turn’d away!
 

 Comfortable thoughts arise
 

 From the bleeding sacrifice.
II
Here, in the fair gospel field,
 

 Wells of free salvation yield
 

 Streams of life, a plenteous store,
 

 And my soul shall thirst no more.
III
Jesus is become at length
 

 My salvation and my strength;
 

 And his praises shall prolong,
 

 While I live, my pleasant song.
IV
Praise ye then his glorious name,
 

 Publish his exalted fame!
 

 Still his worth your praise exceeds,
 

 Excellent are all his deeds.
V
Raise again the joyful sound,
 

 Let the nations roll it round!
 

 Zion, shout, for this is he,
 

 God the Saviour dwells in thee.
===Chinese===
库珀 作
以赛亚书 12。
一
我要天天赞美你，
如今你的怒气已转消！
安慰的思想升起，
来自那流血的祭牲。
二
在这里，在福音的美田中，
自由救恩的井
涌出生命的溪流、丰富的储存，
我的灵魂必不再渴。
三
耶稣终于成了
我的救恩与我的力量；
他的赞美必延长，
只要我活着，作我喜乐的歌。
四
那么要赞美他荣耀的名，
传扬他被高举的名声！
他的宝贵仍超过你们的赞美，
他的一切作为都卓越。
五
再次扬起欢乐的声音，
愿列国把它传遍！
锡安哪，要欢呼，因为这位就是他，
神、救主住在你里面。

## 59 The Refuge, River, and Rock of the Church / 59 教会的避难所、河流与磐石

===Original===
By Newton
Isaiah 32:2.
I
He who on earth as man was known,
 

 And bore our sins and pains,
 

 Now, seated on the eternal throne,
 

 The God of glory reigns.
II
His hands the wheels of nature guide
 

 With an unerring skill;
 

 And countless worlds extended wide
 

 Obey his sovereign will.
III
While harps unnumber’d sound his praise
 

 In yonder world above,
 

 His saints on earth admire his ways,
 

 And glory in his love.
IV
His righteousness to faith reveal’d,
 

 Wrought out for guilty worms,
 

 Affords a hiding-place and shield
 

 From enemies and storms.
V
This land, through which his pilgrims go,
 

 Is desolate and dry;
 

 But streams of grace from him o’erflow,
 

 Their thirst to satisfy.
VI
When troubles, like a burning sun,
 

 Beat heavy on their head,
 

 To this almighty Rock they run,
 

 And find a pleasing shade.
VII
How glorious he! how happy they
 

 In such a glorious Friend!
 

 Whose love secures them all the way,
 

 And crowns them at the end.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
以赛亚书 32:2。
一
那在地上为人所知、
背负我们罪与疼痛的，
如今坐在永恒的宝座上，
以荣耀之神的身份掌权。
二
他的手以无误的技巧
引导自然的轮子；
无数广阔的诸世界
顺服他主权的旨意。
三
当无数琴在彼世
颂赞他时，
他在地上的圣徒钦慕他的道路，
并以他的爱为荣。
四
他的义向信心启示，
为有罪的虫成就，
供备藏身之处与盾牌，
脱离仇敌与风暴。
五
他的客旅所经过的这地，
荒凉而干旱；
但恩典的溪流从他满溢，
来满足他们的渴。
六
当患难如烈日
重重打在他们头上，
他们奔向这大能的磐石，
寻得可喜悦的荫凉。
七
他何等荣耀！他们何等有福，
有这样一位荣耀的朋友！
他的爱全程保守他们安全，
并在终点为他们加冕。

## 60 Zion; or the City of God.7 / 60 锡安；或神的城.7

===Original===
By Newton
Isaiah 33:20﻿–﻿21.
I
Glorious things of thee are spoken,
 

 Zion, city of our God!
 

 He, whose word cannot be broken,
 

 Form’d thee for his own abode:
 

 On the Rock of ages founded,
 

 What can shake thy sure repose?
 

 With salvation’s walls surrounded,
 

 Thou may’st smile at all thy foes.
II
See! the streams of living waters
 

 Springing from eternal love,
 

 Well supply thy sons and daughters,
 

 And all fear of want remove:
 

 Who can faint while such a river
 

 Ever flows their thirst t’ assuage?
 

 Grace, which, like the Lord, the giver,
 

 Never fails from age to age.
III
Round each habitation hov’ring,
 

 See the cloud and fire appear!
 

 For a glory and a cov’ring,
 

 Showing that the Lord is near:
 

 Thus deriving from their banner
 

 Light by night, and shade by day;
 

 Safe they feed upon the manna
 

 Which he gives them when they pray.
IV
Bless’d inhabitants of Zion,
 

 Wash’d in the Redeemer’s blood!
 

 Jesus, whom their souls rely on,
 

 Makes them kings and priests to God;
 

 ’Tis his love his people raises
 

 Over self to reign as kings,
 

 And as priests his solemn praises
 

 Each for a thank-off’ring brings.
V
Saviour, if of Zion’s city
 

 I through grace a member am,
 

 Let the world deride or pity﻿—
 

 I will glory in thy name:
 

 Fading is the worldling’s pleasure﻿—
 

 All his boasted pomp and show;
 

 Solid joys and lasting treasure
 

 None but Zion’s children know.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
以赛亚书 33:20–21。
一
有荣耀的事乃指着你而说，
锡安，我们神的城！
他，其话不能废弃的那位，
造你作他自己的居所：
建立在万古磐石上，
什么能摇动你确实的安息？
有救恩的墙环绕，
你可向一切仇敌微笑。
二
看哪！活水的溪流
从永恒之爱涌出，
充分供应你的儿女，
除去一切缺乏的恐惧：
这样的人谁能昏厥，
有这河流永远解他们的渴？
恩典，像主那赐予者一样，
从一代到一代永不失败。
三
环绕每个居所盘旋，
看云与火显现！
作荣耀并作遮盖，
显明主在近旁：
这样他们从自己的旌旗
得夜间的光、白日的荫；
他们安然吃吗哪，
就是他们祷告时他赐下的。
四
锡安有福的居民，
蒙救赎主的血洗净！
耶稣，他们灵魂所倚靠的，
使他们作君王和祭司归于神；
是他的爱把他的子民举起，
胜过己意作王掌权，
而作祭司，他那庄严的赞美
各人作为感恩祭献上。
五
救主啊，我若靠恩典
是锡安城的一个成员，
任凭世界嘲笑或怜悯——
我必以你的名为荣：
世界之子的宴乐正在凋残——
他一切所夸的浮华与排场；
惟有锡安的儿女知道
确实的喜乐与持久的宝藏。

## 61 Look Unto Me, and Be Ye Saved / 61 当仰望我，就必得救

===Original===
By Newton
Isaiah 45:22.
I
As the serpent raised by Moses
 

 Heal’d the burning serpent’s bite,
 

 Jesus thus himself discloses
 

 To the wounded sinner’s sight:
 

 Hear his gracious invitation,
 

 “I have life and peace to give﻿—
 

 I have wrought out full salvation;
 

 Sinner, look to me and live.
II
“Pore upon your sins no longer,
 

 Well I know their mighty guilt;
 

 But my love than death is stronger,
 

 I my blood have freely spilt:
 

 Though your heart has long been harden’d,
 

 Look on me﻿—it soft shall grow;
 

 Past transgressions shall be pardon’d,
 

 And I’ll wash you white as snow.
III
“I have seen what you were doing,
 

 Though you little thought of me;
 

 You were madly bent on ruin,
 

 But I said﻿—It shall not be:
 

 You had been for ever wretched,
 

 Had I not espoused your part;
 

 Now behold my arms outstretched
 

 To receive you to my heart.
IV
“Well may shame and joy and wonder
 

 All your inward passions move;
 

 I could crush thee with my thunder,
 

 But I speak to thee in love:
 

 See! your sins are all forgiven﻿—
 

 I have paid the countless sum!
 

 Now my death has open’d heaven,
 

 Thither you shall shortly come.”
V
Dearest Saviour, we adore thee
 

 For thy precious life and death;
 

 Melt each stubborn heart before thee,
 

 Give us all the eye of faith:
 

 From the law’s condemning sentence,
 

 To thy mercy we appeal;
 

 Thou alone canst give repentance,
 

 Thou alone our souls canst heal.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
以赛亚书 45:22。
一
正如摩西举起的铜蛇
医治了火蛇的咬伤，
耶稣就这样把自己
启示给受伤罪人的眼：
听他恩慈的邀请，
「我有生命与平安可赐——
我已作成完全的救恩；
罪人哪，仰望我而活。
二
「不要再久久凝视你的罪，
我深知它们重大的罪咎；
但我的爱比死更强，
我已白白流了我的血：
虽然你的心久已刚硬，
仰望我——它必变软；
已往的过犯必蒙赦免，
我必把你洗得白如雪。
三
「我已看见你所作的，
虽然你很少想到我；
你疯狂地奔向毁灭，
但我说——必不这样：
你本必永远悲惨，
若我不曾站在你一边；
看哪，我张开双臂
要接你到我心中。
四
「羞愧、喜乐与惊奇
尽可激动你里面的一切情感；
我本可用我的雷霆压碎你，
但我以爱对你说话：
看！你的罪全都蒙赦免——
我已付清那无数的赎价！
如今我的死已开了天，
你不久必到那里。」
五
最亲爱的救主，我们敬拜你，
为你宝贵的生与死；
求你熔化每个向你刚硬的心，
赐我们众人信心的眼：
从律法定罪的判语，
我们向你怜悯申诉；
惟独你能赐下悔改，
惟独你能医治我们的灵魂。

## 62 The Good Physician / 62 良医

===Original===
By Newton
I
How lost was my condition
 

 Till Jesus made me whole!
 

 There is but one Physician
 

 Can cure a sin-sick soul!
 

 Next door to death he found me,
 

 And snatch’d me from the grave
 

 To tell to all around me
 

 His wondrous power to save.
II
The worst of all diseases
 

 Is light compared with sin;
 

 On every part it seizes,
 

 But rages most within:
 

 ’Tis palsy, dropsy, fever,
 

 And madness﻿—all combined;
 

 And none but a believer
 

 The least relief can find.
III
From men great skill professing
 

 I thought a cure to gain;
 

 But this proved more distressing,
 

 And added to my pain:
 

 Some said that nothing ail’d me,
 

 Some gave me up for lost;
 

 Thus every refuge fail’d me,
 

 And all my hopes were cross’d.
IV
At length this great Physician,
 

 How matchless is his grace!
 

 Accepted my petition,
 

 And undertook my case;
 

 First gave me sight to view him,
 

 For sin my sight had seal’d,
 

 Then bid me look unto him﻿—
 

 I look’d, and I was heal’d.
V
A dying, risen Jesus,
 

 Seen by the eye of faith,
 

 At once from anguish frees us,
 

 And saves the soul from death:
 

 Come then to this Physician,
 

 His help he’ll freely give,
 

 He makes no hard condition,
 

 ’Tis only﻿—look and live.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
我的境况何等失丧，
直到耶稣使我完全！
只有一位医生
能医治患罪病的灵魂！
他在死亡的隔壁寻见我，
把我从坟墓中抢救，
好向四围的人述说
他拯救的大能。
二
一切疾病中最坏的，
与罪相比也算轻；
它侵袭每一部分，
但在里面肆虐最甚：
它是瘫痪、水肿、热病
与疯狂——全都并发；
除了信徒，
无人能寻得丝毫缓解。
三
我曾想从自称医术高明的人
得着医治；
但这证明更叫人忧苦，
加增我的疼痛：
有人说没有病，
有人断我无救；
这样每个避难所都使我失望，
我一切的盼望受挫。
四
最后这位伟大的医生，
他的恩典何等无比！
接受了我的恳求，
承接了我的病案；
先赐我视力看见他，
因罪已封了我的眼目，
再吩咐我仰望他——
我仰望了，就得痊愈。
五
一位受死又复活的耶稣，
被信心的眼看见，
立时使我们脱离苦楚，
救灵魂脱离死亡：
那就来就这位医生，
他必白白赐下帮助，
他不定艰难的条件，
只是——仰望而活。

## 63 To the Afflicted, Tossed with Tempests, and Not Comforted / 63 致受风暴颠簸、未得安慰的受苦者

===Original===
By Newton
Isaiah 44:5﻿–﻿11.
I
Pensive, doubting, fearful heart,
 

 Hear what Christ the Saviour says;
 

 Every word should joy impart,
 

 Change thy mourning into praise:
 

 Yes, he speaks, and speaks to thee,
 

 May he help thee to believe!
 

 Then thou presently wilt see,
 

 Thou hast little cause to grieve.
II
“Fear thou not, nor be ashamed,
 

 All thy sorrows soon shall end:
 

 I who heaven and earth have framed
 

 Am thy Husband and thy Friend:
 

 I the High and Holy One,
 

 Isr’el’s God by all adored,
 

 As thy Saviour will be known,
 

 Thy Redeemer and thy Lord.
III
For a moment I withdrew,
 

 And thy heart was fill’d with pain;
 

 But my mercies I’ll renew﻿—
 

 Thou shalt soon rejoice again:
 

 Though I seem to hide my face,
 

 Yery soon my wrath shall cease;
 

 ’Tis but for a moment’s space,
 

 Ending in eternal peace.
IV
When my peaceful bow appears,
 

 Painted on the wat’ry cloud;
 

 ’Tis to dissipate thy fears,
 

 Lest the earth should be o’erflow’d:
 

 ’Tis an emblem too of grace,
 

 Of my cov’nant love a sign;
 

 Though the mountains leave their place,
 

 Thou shalt be for ever mine.
V
Though afflicted, tempest-toss’d.
 

 Comfortless awhile thou art,
 

 Do not think thou canst be lost,
 

 Thou art graven on my heart:
 

 All thy wastes I will repair,
 

 Thou shalt be rebuilt anew;
 

 And in thee it shall appear
 

 What a God of love can do.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
以赛亚书 44:5–11。
一
沉思、疑惑、恐惧的心哪，
要听救主基督所说；
每句话都当传递喜乐，
把你的哀恸变为赞美：
是的，他说话，且是对你说，
愿他帮助你相信！
那你立时必看见，
你几乎没有理由忧伤。
二
「你不要惧怕，也不要羞愧，
你一切的忧伤不久必结束；
我这设立天地的一位
是你的丈夫和朋友：
我，那至高至圣者、
被众人尊崇的以色列的神，
必被称为你的救主，
你的救赎主和你的主。
三
我片刻退隐，
你的心便充满疼痛；
但我必更新我的怜悯——
你不久必再喜乐：
虽然我似掩藏我的面，
我的怒气不久必止息；
那不过是一刻之久，
终结于永远的平安。
四
当我平安的虹显现，
绘在含水气的云上；
那是为驱散你的恐惧，
免得大地被泛滥：
那也是恩典的表记，
是我盟约之爱的记号；
纵使大山离开原位，
你必永远属我。
五
虽然受苦、被风暴颠簸，
你一时未得安慰，
不要以为你会失丧，
你刻在我心上：
你一切的荒凉我必修补，
你必被重新建造；
在你里面必显明
爱的神能作什么。

## 64 The Contrite Heart / 64 痛悔的心

===Original===
By Cowper
Isaiah 57:15.
I
The Lord will happiness divine
 

 On contrite hearts bestow:
 

 Then tell me, gracious God, is mine
 

 A contrite heart or no?
II
I hear, but seem to hear in vain,
 

 Insensible as steel;
 

 If aught is felt, ’tis only pain
 

 To find I cannot feel.
III
I sometimes think myself inclined
 

 To love thee, if I could;
 

 But often feel another mind,
 

 Averse to all that’s good.
IV
My best desires are faint and few,
 

 I fain would strive for more;
 

 But when I cry, “My strength renew,”
 

 Seem weaker than before.
V
Thy saints are comforted, I know,
 

 And love thy house of prayer;
 

 I therefore go where others go,
 

 But find no comfort there.
VI
O make this heart rejoice or ache,
 

 Decide this doubt for me;
 

 And if it be not broken break,
 

 And heal it if it be.
===Chinese===
库珀 作
以赛亚书 57:15。
一
主必把神圣的福气
赐给痛悔的心：
那么告诉我，恩慈的神，我的心
是不是痛悔的心？
二
我听见，却似徒然听见，
无感如钢铁；
若有什么感受，只是痛苦，
因发现自己不能感受。
三
我有时以为自己倾向于
爱你，若我能够；
但常感受另一个心思，
厌恶一切良善。
四
我最好的愿望微弱而稀少，
我切愿力争更多；
但当我呼喊「更新我的力量」，
似比从前更软弱。
五
你的圣徒得着安慰，我知道，
并爱你的祷告之殿；
我因此去别人去的地方，
却在那里寻不见安慰。
六
哦，使这心喜乐或疼痛，
为我解消这疑虑；
它若未破碎，求你破碎，
它若破碎，求你医治。

## 65 The Future Peace and Glory of the Church / 65 教会将来的平安与荣耀

===Original===
By Cowper
Isaiah 60:15﻿–﻿20.
I
Hear what God the Lord hath spoken:
 

 “O my people, faint and few,
 

 Comfortless, afflicted, broken,
 

 Fair abodes I build for you;
 

 Thorns of heart-felt tribulation
 

 Shall no more perplex your ways;
 

 You shall name your walls Salvation,
 

 And your gates shall all be Praise.
II
There, like streams that feed the garden,
 

 Pleasures without end shall flow;
 

 For the Lord, your faith rewarding,
 

 All his bounty shall bestow:
 

 Still in undisturb’d possession
 

 Peace and righteousness shall reign;
 

 Never shall you feel oppression﻿—
 

 Hear the voice of war again.
III
Ye no more your suns descending,
 

 Waning moons no more shall see;
 

 But, your griefs for ever ending,
 

 Find eternal noon in me:
 

 God shall rise, and shining o’er you,
 

 Change to day the gloom of night;
 

 He the Lord, shall be your glory,
 

 God your everlasting light.”
===Chinese===
库珀 作
以赛亚书 60:15–20。
一
要听主神所说的话：
「哦我的人民，衰微而稀少，
无慰、受苦、破碎，
我为你们建造美好的居所；
心中忧患的荆棘
必不再搅扰你们的路；
你们必称你们的墙为救恩，
你们的门尽都为赞美。
二
在那里，像滋润园子的溪流，
喜乐必无尽头地涌流；
因主赏赐你们的信心，
必赐下他一切的丰富：
仍在不受搅扰的拥有中，
平安与公义必掌权；
你们必不再感受压迫——
再听见战争的声音。
三
你们必不再见你们的日落，
不再见亏缺的月亮；
你们的忧伤永远终结，
在我里面寻得永恒的正午：
神必兴起，照耀你们，
把夜的幽暗变为白昼；
他，主，必作你们的荣耀，
神作你们永远的光。」

## 66 The Trust of the Wicked and the Righteous Compared / 66 恶人与义人之信靠的比较

===Original===
By Newton
Jeremiah 17:5﻿–﻿8.
I
As parched in the barren sands,
 

 Beneath a burning sky,
 

 The worthless bramble with’ring stands,
 

 And only grows to die;
II
Such is the sinner’s awful case,
 

 Who makes the world his trust,
 

 And dares his confidence to place
 

 In vanity and dust.
III
A secret curse destroys his root,
 

 And dries his moisture up;
 

 He lives awhile, but bears no fruit,
 

 Then dies without a hope.
IV
But happy he whose hopes depend
 

 Upon the Lord alone;
 

 The soul that trusts in such a Friend
 

 Can ne’er be overthrown.
V
Though gourds should wither, cisterns break.
 

 And creature-comforts die,
 

 No change his solid hope can shake,
 

 Or stop his sure supply.
VI
So thrives and blooms the tree whose roots
 

 By constant streams are fed;
 

 Array’d in green, and rich in fruits,
 

 It rears its branching head.
VII
It thrives though rain should be denied,
 

 And drought around prevail;
 

 ’Tis planted by a river side
 

 Whose waters cannot fail.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
耶利米书 17:5–8。
一
如在贫瘠的沙土中焦干，
在燃烧的天空下，
无用的荆棘枯萎站立，
生长只为死去；
二
罪人可怕的境况正是这样，
他以世界为信靠，
竟敢把他的信心
安置于虚空与尘土。
三
一个隐秘的咒诅毁坏他的根，
吸干他的水分；
他活一时，却不结果子，
然后无望而死。
四
但那盼望惟独
倚靠主的人有福了；
信靠这样一位朋友的灵魂
永不能被倾覆。
五
虽然瓜藤枯萎、水池破裂，
受造之物的安慰死去，
没有变迁能摇动他确实的盼望，
或截断他确实的供应。
六
那树根蒙恒常溪流
滋养的，便繁茂开花；
披上绿装，果实丰盛，
昂起它分枝的头。
七
虽然雨水被拒、
四围干旱盛行，它仍繁茂；
它栽在河流旁，
其水永不失败。

## 67 Jehovah Our Righteousness / 67 耶和华我们的义

===Original===
By Cowper
Jeremiah 23:6.
I
My God, how perfect are thy ways!
 

 But mine polluted are;
 

 Sin twines itself about my praise,
 

 And slides into my prayer.
II
When I would speak what thou hast done
 

 To save me from my sin,
 

 I cannot make thy mercies known
 

 But self-applause creeps in.
III
Divine desire, that holy flame,
 

 Thy grace creates in me;
 

 Alas! impatience is its name
 

 When it returns to thee.
IV
This heart, a fountain of vile thoughts,
 

 How does it overflow!
 

 While self upon the surface floats,
 

 Still bubbling from below.
V
Let others in the gaudy dress
 

 Of fancied merits shine,
 

 The Lord shall be my righteousness,
 

 The Lord for ever mine.
===Chinese===
库珀 作
耶利米书 23:6。
一
我的神，你的道路何等完全！
但我的已被污染；
罪缠绕我的赞美，
并滑入我的祷告。
二
当我要述说你所作的，
拯救我脱离我的罪，
我不能述说你的怜悯
而不混入自夸。
三
神圣的愿望，那圣洁的火焰，
是你的恩典在我里面造的；
唉！当它归向你时，
它的名字却是不耐。
四
这颗心，污秽思想的泉源，
何等地满溢！
当自我浮在表面，
仍从下面冒泡。
五
任凭别人穿着幻想功德的
华丽衣裳发光，
主必作我的义，
主永远是我的。

## 68 Ephraim Repenting / 68 以法莲的悔改

===Original===
By Cowper
Jeremiah 31:18﻿–﻿20.
I
My God, till I received thy stroke,
 

 How like a beast was I!
 

 So unaccustom’d to the yoke,
 

 So backward to comply.
II
With grief my just reproach I bear,
 

 Shame fills me at the thought;
 

 How frequent my rebellions were!
 

 What wickedness I wrought!
III
3 Thy merciful restraint I scorn’d,
 

 And left the pleasant road;
 

 Yet turn me, and I shall be turn’d:
 

 Thou art the Lord my God.
IV
Is Ephraim banish’d from my thoughts,
 

 Or vile in my esteem?
 

 No, saith the Lord, with all his faults
 

 I still remember him.
V
Is he a dear and pleasant child?
 

 Yes, dear and pleasant still;
 

 Though sin his foolish heart beguiled.
 

 And he withstood my will.
VI
My sharp rebuke has laid him low,
 

 He seeks my face again;
 

 My pity kindles at his woe,
 

 He shall not seek in vain.
===Chinese===
库珀 作
耶利米书 31:18–20。
一
我的神，直到我受你的击打，
我何等像一头兽！
那样不习惯负轭，
那样退后不顺从。
二
我怀着忧伤承受当受的责备，
一想到就满心羞愧；
我的悖逆何等频繁！
我行了何等的邪恶！
三
3 我藐视了你怜悯的约束，
离开了那可悦的路；
但求你使我回转，我必回转：
你是主我的神。
四
以法莲从我思想中被驱逐了吗，
或在我眼中看为卑贱？
不，主说，纵有这一切过犯，
我仍记念他。
五
他是一个亲爱的、可悦的孩子吗？
是的，仍然亲爱可悦；
虽然罪迷惑他愚昧的心，
他违背了我的旨意。
六
我严厉的责备使他低微，
他再寻求我的面；
我的怜悯因他的祸患燃起，
他寻求必不徒然。

## 69 The Lord Is My Portion / 69 主是我的份

===Original===
By Newton
Lamentations 3:24.
I
From pole to pole let others roam,
 

 And search in vain for bliss;
 

 My soul is satisfied at home,
 

 The Lord my portion is.
II
Jesus, who on his glorious throne,
 

 Rules heaven and earth and sea,
 

 Is pleased to claim me for his own,
 

 And give himself to me.
III
His person fixes all my love,
 

 His blood removes my fear;
 

 And while he pleads for me above,
 

 His arm preserves me here.
IV
His word of promise is my food,
 

 His Spirit is my guide;
 

 Thus daily is my strength renewed,
 

 And all my wants supplied.8
V
For him I count as gain each loss,
 

 Disgrace for him renown;
 

 Well may I glory in his cross,
 

 While he prepares my crown!
VI
Let worldlings then indulge their boast,
 

 How much they gain or spend;
 

 Their joys must soon give up the ghost,
 

 But mine shall know no end.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
耶利米哀歌 3:24。
一
任凭别人从这极到那极游历，
徒然寻求福气；
我的灵魂在家中满足，
主是我的份。
二
耶稣，在他荣耀的宝座上，
治理天地与海，
喜悦认我为他自己的，
并把自己赐给我。
三
他的位格固定我所有的爱，
他的血除去我的恐惧；
当他在上面为我代求，
他的膀臂在此保守我。
四
他应许的话是我的食物，
他的圣灵是我的引导；
这样我的力量日日更新，
我一切的需用得着供应。8
五
为他，我把每次损失算作收益，
为他，羞辱算作尊荣；
我尽可以他的十字架夸耀，
当他预备我的冠冕！
六
任凭世界之子沉溺于自夸，
夸他们赚得或花费多少；
他们的喜乐不久必断气，
我的却必无尽头。

## 70 Humbled and Silenced by Mercy / 70 因怜悯而谦卑静默

===Original===
By Newton
Ezekiel 16:63.
I
Once perishing in blood I lay;
 

 Creatures no help could give;
 

 But Jesus pass’d me in the way,
 

 He saw, and bade me live.
II
Though Satan still his rule maintain’d,
 

 And all his arts employ’d;
 

 That mighty word his rage restrain’d,
 

 I could not be destroy’d.
III
At length the time of love arrived,
 

 When I my Lord should know;
 

 Then Satan, of his power deprived,
 

 Was forced to let me go.
IV
O can I e’er that day forget,
 

 When Jesus kindly spoke!
 

 “Poor soul, my blood has paid thy debt,
 

 And now I break thy yoke.
V
“Henceforth I take thee for my own,
 

 And give myself to thee;
 

 Forsake the idols thou hast known,
 

 And yield thy heart to me.”
VI
Ah, worthless heart! it promised fair,
 

 And said it would be thine;
 

 I little thought it e’er would dare
 

 Again with idols join.
VII
Lord, dost thou such backslidings heal,
 

 And pardon all that’s past?
 

 Sure, if I am not made of steel,
 

 Thou hast prevail’d at last.
VIII
My tongue, which rashly spoke before,
 

 This mercy will restrain;
 

 Surely I now shall boast no more,
 

 Nor censure, nor complain.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
以西结书 16:63。
一
我曾一旦灭亡在血中；
受造之物不能施助；
但耶稣在路上经过我，
他看见，吩咐我活。
二
虽然撒但仍维持他的统治，
用尽他一切的伎俩；
那大能的言语约束他的狂怒，
我不能被毁灭。
三
终于爱的时刻来到，
当我该认识我主之时；
撒但于是被夺去权势，
被迫放我走。
四
哦，我岂能忘记那日，
当耶稣恩慈地说话！
「可怜的灵魂，我的血已付你的债，
如今我折断你的轭。
五
「从今我认你为我自己的，
把我自己赐给你；
离弃你所认识的偶像，
把你的心交给我。」
六
啊，无价值的心！它许诺得美好，
说它必属你；
我一点想不到它竟敢
再与偶像联合。
七
主啊，你医治这样的后退，
并赦免一切已往吗？
确实，我若不是钢作的，
你终必得胜。
八
我那从前鲁莽说话的舌，
这怜悯必约束它；
如今我必不再自夸，
不指责，也不抱怨。

## 71 The Covenant / 71 约

===Original===
By Cowper
Ezekiel 36:25﻿–﻿28.
I
The Lord proclaims his grace abroad!
 

 “Behold, I change your hearts of stone;
 

 Each shall renounce his idol-god,
 

 And serve, henceforth, the Lord alone.
II
“My grace, a flowing stream, proceeds
 

 To wash your filthiness away;
 

 Ye shall abhor your former deeds,
 

 And learn my statutes to obey.
III
“My truth the great design ensures,
 

 I give myself away to you;
 

 You shall be mine, I will be yours,
 

 Your God unalterably true.
IV
“Yet not unsought or unimplored
 

 The plenteous grace shall I confer;
 

 No﻿—your whole hearts shall seek the Lord,
 

 I’ll put a praying spirit there.
V
“From the first breath of life divine,
 

 Down to the last expiring hour,
 

 The gracious work shall all be mine,
 

 Begun and ended in my power.”
===Chinese===
库珀 作
以西结书 36:25–28。
一
主在外宣告他的恩典！
「看哪，我改换你们的石心；
各人必离弃他的偶像神，
从今以后单事奉主。」
二
「我的恩典如流水涌出，
要洗去你们的污秽；
你们必厌恶从前的行为，
并学习遵行我的律例。
三
「我的信实确保这伟大的设计，
我把自己赐给你们；
你们必属我，我必属你们，
作你们的神，永远真实不改。
四
「然而这丰富的恩典
并非不被寻求、不被恳求就赐下；
不——你们的全心必寻求主，
我必把祷告的灵放在其中。
五
「从神圣生命的初次呼吸，
到最后断气之时，
这恩典的工作必全属我，
在我的大能中开始并结束。」

## 72 Jehovah-Shammah / 72 耶和华沙玛——主在那里

===Original===
By Cowper
Ezekiel 48:35.
I
As birds their infant brood protect,
 

 And spread their wings to shelter them;
 

 Thus saith the Lord to his elect,
 

 “So will I guard Jerusalem.”
II
And what then is Jerusalem﻿—
 

 This darling object of his care?
 

 Where is its worth in God’s esteem?
 

 Who built it? who inhabits there?
III
Jehovah founded it in blood.
 

 The blood of his incarnate Son;
 

 There dwell the saints, once foes to God﻿—
 

 The sinners whom he calls his own.
IV
There, though besieged on every side,
 

 Yet much beloved and guarded well,
 

 From age to age they have defied
 

 The utmost force of earth and hell.
V
Let earth repent and hell despair,
 

 This city has a sure defence;
 

 Her name is call’d “The Lord is there,”
 

 And who has power to drive him thence?
===Chinese===
库珀 作
以西结书 48:35。
一
如鸟保护它们的幼雏，
展翅遮蔽它们；
主对他的选民也这样说：
「我必这样保护耶路撒冷。」
二
那么耶路撒冷是什么——
他看顾的这一珍爱对象？
它在神眼中的价值在哪里？
谁建造了它？谁居住其中？
三
耶和华以血建立它，
他道成肉身之子的血；
那里住着圣徒，曾与神为敌——
那些他称为自己子民的罪人。
四
那里虽被四围围攻，
却深蒙爱、蒙好好守护，
从一代到一代他们已抵挡
地和地狱的极尽全力。
五
任凭地悔改、地狱绝望，
这城有确实的防御；
它的名被称为「主在那里」，
谁能有能力把他驱逐？

## 73 The Power and Triumph of Faith / 73 信心的大能与得胜

===Original===
By Newton
Daniel 3:6.
I
Supported by the word,
 

 Though in himself a worm,
 

 The servant of the Lord
 

 Can wondrous acts perform:
 

 Without dismay he boldly treads
 

 Where’er the path of duty leads.
II
The haughty king in vain,
 

 With fury on his brow,
 

 Believers would constrain
 

 To golden gods to bow:
 

 The furnace could not make them fear,
 

 Because they knew the Lord was near.
III
As vain was the decree
 

 Which charged them not to pray:
 

 Daniel still bow’d his knee,
 

 And worshipp’d thrice a day.
 

 Trusting in God, he fear’d not men,
 

 Though threaten’d with the lions’ den.
IV
Secure they might refuse
 

 Compliance with such laws;
 

 For what had they to lose
 

 When God espoused their cause?
 

 He made the hungry lions crouch;
 

 Nor durst the fire his children touch.
V
The Lord is still the same,
 

 A mighty shield and tower,
 

 And they who trust his name
 

 Are guarded by his power;
 

 He can the rage of lions tame,
 

 And bear them harmless through the flame.
VI
Yet we too often shrink
 

 When trials are in view,
 

 Expecting we must sink,
 

 And never can get through:
 

 But could we once believe indeed,
 

 From all these fears we should be freed.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
但以理书 3:6。
一
蒙话语扶持，
虽然本身是条虫，
主的仆人
能行出奇事：
他毫不惊惶，大胆踏过
职责所引的任何路径。
二
狂傲的君王徒然
眉间带着狂怒，
要强令信徒
向金神下拜：
火炉不能使他们惧怕，
因为他们知道主在近旁。
三
同样徒然的是那禁令，
禁止他们祷告：
但以理仍屈膝，
一日三次敬拜。
信靠神，他不怕人，
虽然被威胁扔进狮子坑。
四
他们可以安然拒绝
遵从这样的法律；
因为当神站在
他们一边，他们有何可失？
他使饥饿的狮子蹲伏；
火也不敢碰他的儿女。
五
主仍是一样，
大能的盾牌与坚台，
凡信靠他名的人
都蒙他的大能护卫；
他能驯服狮子的狂怒，
并携他们无害穿过火焰。
六
然而我们太常退缩，
当试炼在望，
以为自己必沉沦，
永不能渡过：
但我们若真能一度相信，
就必从这一切恐惧中得释。

## 74 Belshazzar / 74 伯沙撒

===Original===
By Newton
Daniel 5:5﻿–﻿6.
I
Poor sinners! little do they think
 

 With whom they have to do!
 

 But stand securely on the brink
 

 Of everlasting woe.
II
Belshazzar thus, profanely bold,
 

 The Lord of hosts defied;
 

 But vengeance soon his boasts control’d,
 

 And humbled all his pride.
III
He saw a hand upon the wall
 

 (And trembled on his throne),
 

 Which wrote his sudden dreadful fall
 

 In characters unknown.
IV
Why should he tremble at the view
 

 Of what he could not read?
 

 Foreboding conscience quickly knew
 

 His ruin was decreed.
V
See him o’erwhelm’d with deep distress;
 

 His eyes with anguish roll;
 

 His looks and loosen’d joints express
 

 The terrors of his soul.
VI
His pomp and music, guests and wine,
 

 No more delight afford:
 

 O sinner, ere this case be thine,
 

 Begin to seek the Lord.
VII
The law like this hand-writing stands,
 

 And speaks the wrath of God;
 

 But Jesus answers its demands,
 

 And cancels it with blood.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
但以理书 5:5–6。
一
可怜的罪人们！他们何等少想到
他们所面对的是谁！
却安稳地站在
永远祸患的边缘。
二
伯沙撒就这样亵渎地放胆，
违抗万军之主；
但报应速速辖制他的夸口，
降卑他一切的骄傲。
三
他看见墙上有手
（在宝座上战兢），
以未知的文字
写下他突临的可怖倾覆。
四
他为何因所见
不能读的字而战兢？
预感的良心速速知道
他的毁灭已定。
五
看他被深忧压倒；
他的眼因痛苦翻滚；
他的面色与松脱的关节
表达他灵魂的恐怖。
六
他的浮华与音乐、宾客与酒，
不再供给他欢乐：
哦罪人，趁这境况还未临到你，
要开始寻求主。
七
律法像这手写的一样站立，
述说神的忿怒；
但耶稣答应它的要求，
用血把它涂销。

## 75 The Gourd / 75 蓖麻

===Original===
By Newton
Jonah 4:6﻿–﻿8.
I
As once for Jonah, so the Lord,
 

 To sooth and cheer my mournful hours,
 

 Prepared for me a pleasing gourd,
 

 Cool was its shade, and sweet its flowers.
II
To prize this gift was surely right;
 

 But through the folly of my heart,
 

 It hid the Giver from my sight,
 

 And soon my joy was changed to smart.
III
While I admired its beauteous form,
 

 Its pleasant shade and grateful fruit,
 

 The Lord, displeased, sent forth a worm,
 

 Unseen to prey upon the root.
IV
I trembled when I saw it fade,
 

 But guilt restrain’d the murm’ring word;
 

 My folly I confess’d, and pray’d﻿—
 

 Forgive my sin, and spare my gourd.
V
His wondrous love can ne’er be told﻿—
 

 He heard me and relieved my pain;
 

 His word the threat’ning worm control’d,
 

 And bid my gourd revive again.
VI
Now, Lord, my gourd is mine no more,
 

 ’Tis thine, who only couldst it raise;
 

 The idol of my heart before
 

 Henceforth shall flourish to thy praise.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
约拿书 4:6–8。
一
正如曾为约拿所作的，主
为安抚鼓舞我哀恸的时辰，
为我预备了一棵可悦的蓖麻，
它的荫凉爽，它的花甘甜。
二
珍视这恩赐本是对的；
但借我心中的愚妄，
它把赐予者从我眼前遮蔽，
不久我的喜乐变为刺痛。
三
当我欣赏它美丽的形式、
它可悦的荫和甘美的果，
主不悦，差来一条虫，
隐秘地侵害它的根。
四
我见它凋残便战兢，
但罪咎制止了怨言；
我承认我的愚妄，并祷告——
赦免我的罪，留下我的蓖麻。
五
他奇妙的爱永不能述尽——
他垂听我，减轻我的疼痛；
他的话约束那威吓的虫，
并吩咐我的蓖麻再次复苏。
六
如今，主啊，我的蓖麻不再属我，
它属你，惟你能使它生长；
它从前是我心中的偶像，
从今愿它茂盛来赞美你。

## 76 Prayer for the Lord’s Promised Presence / 76 求主应许的同在

===Original===
By Newton
Zechariah 2:10.
I
Son of God, thy people shield!
 

 Must we still thine absence mourn?
 

 Let thy promise be fulfill’d,
 

 Thou hast said, “I will return!”
II
Gracious leader, now appear,
 

 Shine upon us with thy light!
 

 Like the spring, when thou art near,
 

 Days and suns are doubly bright.
III
As a mother counts the days
 

 Till her absent son she see,
 

 Longs and watches, weeps and prays,
 

 So our spirits long for thee.
IV
Come and let us feel thee nigh,
 

 Then thy sheep shall feed in peace;
 

 Plenty bless us from on high,
 

 Evil from amongst us cease.
V
With thy love and voice and aid,
 

 Thou canst every care assuage;
 

 Then we shall not be afraid,
 

 Though the world and Satan rage.
VI
Thus each day for thee we’ll spend,
 

 While our callings we pursue;
 

 And the thoughts of such a friend
 

 Shall each night our joy renew.
VII
Let thy light be ne’er withdrawn,
 

 Golden days afford us long!
 

 Thus we pray at early dawn,
 

 This shall be our evening song.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
撒迦利亚书 2:10。
一
神的儿子，求你护卫你的子民！
我们岂仍要哀恸你的不在？
愿你的应许得成就，
你曾说过：「我必回来！」
二
恩慈的引领者，如今显现，
用你的光照耀我们！
如春天，当你在近旁，
日子与太阳加倍明亮。
三
如母亲数算日子，
直到见她不在的儿子，
渴望、守望、哭泣、祷告，
我们的灵也这样切望你。
四
来吧，让我们感受你在近旁，
那时你的羊必平安吃草；
丰盛从上赐福我们，
恶事从我们中间止息。
五
有你的爱、你的声音、你的扶助，
你能减轻一切挂虑；
那时我们便不惧怕，
虽然世界与撒但狂怒。
六
这样我们每日为你而度，
同时尽我们的呼召；
对这样一位朋友的思念
必每夜更新我们的喜乐。
七
愿你的光永不收回，
赐我们长久的黄金日子！
这样我们在清晨祷告，
这必成为我们黄昏的诗歌。

## 77 A Brand Plucked Out of the Fire / 77 从火中抽出来的一根柴

===Original===
By Newton
Zechariah 3:1﻿–﻿5.
I
With Satan, my accuser, near,
 

 My spirit trembled when I saw
 

 The Lord in majesty appear,
 

 And heard the language of his law.
II
In vain I wish’d and strove to hide
 

 The tatter’d filthy rags I wore:
 

 While my fierce foe insulting cried,
 

 “See what you trusted in before!”
III
Struck dumb, and left without a plea,
 

 I heard my gracious Saviour say,
 

 “Know, Satan, I this sinner free﻿—
 

 I died to take his sins away.
IV
“This is a brand which I in love
 

 To save from wrath and sin design;
 

 In vain thy accusations prove;
 

 I answer all, and claim him mine.”
V
At this rebuke the tempter fled;
 

 Then He removed my filthy dress;
 

 “Poor sinner, take this robe,” He said,
 

 “It is thy Saviour’s righteousness.
VI
“And see a crown of life prepared!
 

 That I might thus thy head adorn;
 

 I thought no shame of suff’ring hard,
 

 But wore for thee a crown of thorn.”
VII
O, how I heard these gracious words!
 

 They broke and heal’d my heart at once;
 

 Constrained me to become the Lord’s,
 

 And all my idol-gods renounce.
VIII
Now, Satan, thou hast lost thy aim;
 

 Against this brand thy threats are vain;
 

 Jesus has pluck’d it from the flame,
 

 And who shall put it in again?
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
撒迦利亚书 3:1–5。
一
当我的控告者撒但在近旁，
我的灵战兢，当我看见
主在威严中显现，
并听见他律法的言语。
二
我徒然想要遮掩
我所穿褴褛污秽的衣服：
我凶猛的仇敌辱骂地喊：
「看你从前所信靠的！」
三
哑口无言，再无申辩，
我听见我恩慈的救主说：
「撒但，要知道，我释放这罪人——
我死了，要除去他的罪。
四
「这是一根柴，我在爱中
设计要拯救它脱离忿怒与罪；
你的控告尽属徒然；
我回答一切，并认他为我的。」
五
这责备一出，试探者逃遁；
然后他脱去我污秽的衣；
「可怜的罪人，接受这袍，」他说，
「这是你救主的义。
六
「再看这预备好的生命冠冕！
使我这样装扮你的头；
我不以受苦为耻，
为你戴过荆棘冠冕。」
七
哦，我何等需要听见这些恩言！
它们立时破碎又医治了我的心；
约束我归于主，
并弃绝我一切的偶像神。
八
如今撒但，你已失去目标；
对这根柴你的威胁徒然；
耶稣已把它从火中抽出，
谁能再把它放回去？

## 78 On One Stone Shall Be Seven Eyes / 78 一块石头上有七眼

===Original===
By Newton
Zechariah 3:9.
I
Jesus Christ, the Lord’s Anointed,
 

 Who his blood for sinners spilt,
 

 Is the Stone by God appointed,
 

 And the church is on him built:
 

 He delivers all who trust him from their guilt.
II
Many eyes at once are fix’d
 

 On a person so divine;
 

 Love, with awful justice mix’d,
 

 In his great redemption shine:
 

 Mighty Jesus! give me leave to call thee mine.
III
By the Father’s eye approved,
 

 Lo, a voice is heard from heaven,
 

 “Sinners, this is my Beloved,
 

 For your ransom freely given:
 

 All offences for his sake shall be forgiven,”
IV
Angels with their eyes pursued him
 

 When he left his glorious throne;
 

 With astonishment they view’d him
 

 Put the form of servant on;
 

 Angels worshipp’d Him who was on earth unknown.
V
Satan and his host, amazed,
 

 Saw this Stone in Zion laid;
 

 Jesus, though to death abased,
 

 Bruised the subtle Serpent’s head
 

 When, to save us, on the cross his blood he shed.
VI
When a guilty sinner sees him,
 

 While he looks his soul is heal’d;
 

 Soon this sight from anguish frees him,
 

 And imparts a pardon seal’d:
 

 May this Saviour be to all our hearts reveal’d!
VII
With desire and admiration,
 

 All his blood-bought flock behold
 

 Him who wrought out their salvation,
 

 And enclosed them in his fold:
 

 Yet their warmest love and praises are too cold.
VIII
By the eye of carnal reason
 

 Many view him with disdain;
 

 How will they abide the season
 

 When he comes with all his train?
 

 To escape him then they’ll wish, but wish in vain.
IX
How their hearts will melt and tremble
 

 When they hear his awful voice
 

 But his saints he’ll then assemble,
 

 As his portion and his choice,
 

 And receive them to his everlasting joys.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
撒迦利亚书 3:9。
一
耶稣基督，主的受膏者，
他为罪人流了血，
是神所指定的石头，
教会建造在他上面：
他释放一切信靠他的人脱离罪咎。
二
许多眼目一时定睛
在这样一位神圣者身上；
爱与可畏的公义相混，
在他伟大的救赎中照耀：
大能的耶稣！准我称你为我自己的。
三
蒙父的眼认可，
看哪，有声音从天上来，
「罪人们，这是我的爱子，
为你们的赎价白白赐下：
为他一切的过犯都必蒙赦免，」
四
众天使以眼目跟随他，
当他离开他荣耀的宝座；
他们惊异地观看他
穿上仆人的形像；
众天使敬拜这位在地上不为人知的。
五
撒但和他的军兵惊愕，
看见这石头安放在锡安；
耶稣虽被降卑至死，
却压碎了狡猾蛇的头，
当他为救我们在十字架上流血之时。
六
当一个有罪的罪人看见他，
他一仰望，灵魂就得医治；
这景象立时使他脱离苦楚，
并赐下盖印的赦免：
愿这救主向我们众心启示！
七
带着渴慕与钦佩，
他宝血所买的羊群都仰望
那作成他们救恩、
并把他们圈入羊圈的一位：
然而他们最热的爱与赞美仍太冷淡。
八
凭属肉体的理性之眼，
许多人轻蔑地看他；
他们怎能忍受那季节，
当他带着他一切随从而来？
那时他们必想逃避，但徒然想望。
九
他们的心将何等熔化战兢，
当他们听见他可怕的声音；
但他必聚集他的圣徒，
作为他的份和他的拣选，
并接他们进入他永远的喜乐。

## 79 Praise for the Fountain Opened / 79 赞美敞开的泉源

===Original===
By Cowper
Zechariah 13:1.
I
There is a fountain fill’d with blood
 

 Drawn from Emmanuel’s veins;
 

 And sinners plunged beneath that flood
 

 Lose all their guilty stains.
II
The dying thief rejoiced to see
 

 That fountain in his day;
 

 And there have I, as vile as he,
 

 Wash’d all my sins away.
III
Dear dying Lamb, thy precious blood
 

 Shall never lose its power
 

 Till all the ransom’d church of God
 

 Be saved, to sin no more.
IV
E’er since, by faith, I saw the stream
 

 Thy flowing wounds supply,
 

 Redeeming love has been my theme,
 

 And shall be till I die.
V
Then in a nobler, sweeter song,
 

 I’ll sing thy power to save﻿—
 

 When this poor lisping stamm’ring tongue
 

 Lies silent in the grave.
VI
Lord, I believe thou hast prepared
 

 (Unworthy though I be,)
 

 For me a blood-bought free reward,
 

 A golden harp for me!
VII
’Tis strung, and tuned for endless years,
 

 And form’d by power divine;
 

 To sound in God the Father’s ears
 

 No other name but thine.
===Chinese===
库珀 作
撒迦利亚书 13:1。
一
有一泉源充满宝血，
发自以马内利的脉；
罪人被浸在那洪流之下，
失去一切罪咎的污点。
二
那垂死的强盗欢喜看见
他日子里的那泉源；
而我，像他一样卑劣，
也曾在那里洗去我一切的罪。
三
亲爱垂死的羔羊，你的宝血
永不失去它的能力，
直到神蒙赎的教会
全数得救，不再犯罪。
四
自从我凭信心看见
你流血伤口所供的溪流，
救赎的爱一直是我主题，
直到我死也必如此。
五
那时在更高贵、更甜的歌中，
我要歌颂你拯救的大能——
当这可怜结巴的舌
静卧在坟墓中。
六
主啊，我相信你已预备
（虽然我不配，）
为我一个用血买来、白白舍下的赏赐，
为我的一张金琴！
七
它已张好，调准无尽年岁，
由神圣大能造作；
要在神父的耳中
只奏你的名，别无他名。

## 80 They Shall Be Mine, Saith the Lord / 80 万军之耶和华说：他们必属我

===Original===
By Newton
Malachi 3:16﻿–﻿18.
I
When sinners utter boasting words,
 

 And glory in their shame,
 

 The Lord, well pleased, an ear affords,
 

 To those who fear his name.
II
They often met to seek his face;
 

 And what they do or say
 

 Is rioted in his book of grace
 

 Against another day.
III
For they, by faith, a day descry,
 

 And joyfully expect,
 

 When he, descending from the sky,
 

 His jewels will collect.
IV
Unnoticed now, because unknown,
 

 A poor and suff’ring few;
 

 He comes to claim them for his own,
 

 And bring them forth to view.
V
With transport then their Saviour’s care
 

 And favour they shall prove;
 

 As tender parents guard and spare
 

 The children of their love.
VI
Assembled worlds will then discern
 

 The saints alone are blest,
 

 When wrath shall like an oven burn,
 

 And vengeance strike the rest.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
玛拉基书 3:16–18。
一
当罪人说出夸口的言语，
以他们的羞辱为荣，
主却喜悦侧耳，
垂听那些敬畏他名的人。
二
他们常聚集寻求他的面；
他们所行所说的
被记在他的恩典册上，
为另一日子存留。
三
因他们凭信心望见一日，
并喜乐地期待，
当他从天降临，
收聚他的珍宝。
四
如今不被注意，因为不被认识，
一群贫穷受苦的少数；
他来认他们为他自己的，
并把他们显明于人前。
五
那时他们必欢腾地证明
他们救主的看顾与恩宠；
如温柔的父母护卫宽容
他们所爱的儿女。
六
聚集的诸世界那时必辨明，
惟独圣徒是有福的，
当忿怒如炉燃烧，
报应击打其余的人。

## 81 The Beggar / 81 乞丐

===Original===
By Newton
Matthew 7:7﻿–﻿8.
I
Encouraged by thy word
 

 Of promise to the poor,
 

 Behold a beggar, Lord,
 

 Waits at thy mercy’s door!
 

 No hand, no heart, O Lord, but thine,
 

 Can help or pity wants like mine.
II
The beggar’s usual plea,
 

 Relief from men to gain,
 

 If offer’d unto thee,
 

 I know thou wouldst disdain;
 

 And pleas which move thy gracious ear
 

 Are such as men would scorn to hear.
III
I have no right to say,
 

 That though I now am poor,
 

 Yet once there was a day
 

 When I possessed more:
 

 Thou know’st that from my very birth
 

 I’ve been the poorest wretch on earth.
IV
Nor can I dare profess,
 

 As beggars often do,
 

 Though great is my distress,
 

 My faults have been but few:
 

 If thou shouldst leave my soul to starve,
 

 It would be what I well deserve.
V
’Twere folly to pretend
 

 I never begg’d before;
 

 Or if thou now befriend,
 

 I’ll trouble thee no more;
 

 Thou often hast relieved my pain,
 

 And often I must come again.
VI
Though crumbs are much too good
 

 For such a dog as I,
 

 No less than children’s food
 

 My soul can satisfy.
 

 O do not frown and bid me go﻿—
 

 I must have all thou canst bestow!
VII
Nor can I willing be
 

 Thy bounty to conceal
 

 From others who, like me,
 

 Their wants and hunger feel:
 

 I’ll tell them of thy mercy’s store,
 

 And try to send a thousand more.
VIII
Thy thoughts, thou Only Wise,
 

 Our thoughts and ways transcend,
 

 Far as the arched skies
 

 Above the earth extend:
 

 Such pleas as mine men would not bear,
 

 But God receives a beggar’s pray’r.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
马太福音 7:7–8。
一
蒙你话语的鼓励，
那给穷人的应许，
看哪，一个乞丐，主啊，
等候在你怜悯的门前！
除了你的手、你的心，主啊，
无手无心能帮助怜恤像我这样的缺乏。
二
乞丐常用的理由，
为从人得着救济，
若献上给你，
我知道你必不屑；
而感动你恩耳的恳求，
是人所不屑一听的。
三
我无权说，
虽然我如今贫穷，
却曾有一日
我拥有更多：
你知道我从出生以来，
就是地上最可怜的人。
四
我也不敢宣称，
像乞丐们常作的那样，
虽然我的困苦重大，
我的过失却少：
你若任我灵魂挨饿，
那正是我所应得的。
五
假装我从前
从未乞讨过，是愚妄；
或说你如今施恩，
我便不再搅扰你；
你屡次减轻我的疼痛，
我也必须屡次再来。
六
虽然碎渣也太好，
不配给我这样一条狗，
但不亚于儿女的食物
才能满足我的灵魂。
哦，不要皱眉赶我走——
我必须得着你所能赐的一切！
七
我也不能愿意
把你丰富的恩赐
向那些像我一样
感受缺乏饥饿的人隐藏：
我要述说你怜悯的库藏，
并尽力差遣千人来。
八
你的意念，惟有智慧的你，
超过我们的意念与道路，
如穹苍诸天
高过大地那样远：
这样的恳求人必不容纳，
但神垂听乞丐的祷告。

## 82 The Leper / 82 麻风病人

===Original===
By Newton
Matthew 8:2﻿–﻿3.
I
Oft as the leper’s case I read,
 

 My own described I feel:
 

 Sin is a leprosy indeed
 

 Which none but Christ can heal.
II
Awhile I would have pass’d for well,
 

 And strove my spots to hide,
 

 Till it broke out incurable,
 

 Too plain to be denied.
III
Then from the saints I sought to flee,
 

 And dreaded to be seen;
 

 I thought they all would point at me.
 

 And cry, “Unclean, unclean!”
IV
What anguish did my soul endure
 

 Till hope and patience ceased!
 

 The more I strove myself to cure,
 

 The more the plague increased.
V
While thus I lay distress’d, I saw
 

 The Saviour passing by;
 

 To him, though fill’d with shame and awe,
 

 I raised my mournful cry.
VI
Lord, thou canst heal me if thou wilt,
 

 For thou canst all things do;
 

 O cleanse my leprous soul from guilt,
 

 My filthy heart renew.
VII
He heard, and with a gracious look
 

 Pronounced the healing word;
 

 “I will﻿—be clean,” and while he spoke
 

 I felt my health restored.
VIII
Come, lepers, seize the present hour,
 

 The Saviour’s grace to prove;
 

 He can relieve, for he is power﻿—
 

 He will, for he is love.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
马太福音 8:2–3。
一
每当我读到麻风病人的案例，
便感到描述的是我自己：
罪确是一种麻风病，
除基督外无人能医治。
二
一度我想要装作无恙，
竭力遮掩我的斑点，
直到它溃发成不可救药，
明显得无法否认。
三
于是我想逃离圣徒，
害怕被人看见；
我想他们都会指着我
喊：「不洁净，不洁净！」
四
我的灵魂忍受何等苦楚，
直到盼望与忍耐止息！
我越努力医治自己，
瘟疫越是加增。
五
当我这样困卧之时，我看见
救主经过；
我虽满心羞愧敬畏，
仍向他发出哀恸的呼求。
六
主啊，你若肯，必能医治我，
因你能作万事；
哦，求你洁净我麻风灵魂的罪咎，
更新我污秽的心。
七
他垂听了，以恩慈的面色
说出医治的言语；
「我肯——你洁净了吧，」他一说话，
我便感到健康恢复。
八
来吧，麻风病人们，要抓住现今的时辰，
以证明救主的恩典；
他能解救，因为他是能力——
他肯，因为他是爱。

## 83 A Sick Soul / 83 患病的灵魂

===Original===
By Newton
Matthew 9:12.
I
Physician of my sin-sick soul,
 

 To thee I bring my case;
 

 My raging malady control,
 

 And heal me by thy grace.
II
Pity the anguish I endure,
 

 See how I mourn and pine;
 

 For never can I hope a cure
 

 From any hand but thine.
III
I would disclose my whole complaint,
 

 But where shall I begin?
 

 No words of mine can fully paint
 

 That worst distemper, sin.
IV
It lies not in a single part,
 

 But through my frame is spread;
 

 A burning fever in my heart,
 

 A palsy in my head.
V
It makes me deaf and dumb and blind,
 

 And impotent and lame;
 

 It overclouds and fills my mind
 

 With folly, fear, and shame.
VI
A thousand evil thoughts intrude
 

 Tumultuous in my breast;
 

 Which indispose me for my food,
 

 And rob me of my rest.
VII
Lord, I am sick, regard my cry,
 

 And set my spirit free:
 

 Say, canst thou let a sinner die,
 

 Who longs to live to thee?
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
马太福音 9:12。
一
我患罪病灵魂的医生，
我把病案带到你面前；
求你控制我狂暴的疾病，
借你的恩典医治我。
二
求你怜恤我忍受的苦楚，
看我何等哀恸憔悴；
因我永不能盼望医治
从你以外的任何手。
三
我愿陈述我全部的病状，
但从何处说起？
我的言语不能完全描绘
那最重的疾病——罪。
四
它不单在一处，
而是漫过我的全身；
我心中一场燃烧的热病，
我头中一次瘫痪。
五
它使我聋、哑、瞎，
无能又瘸腿；
它遮蔽并充满我的心思，
以愚昧、恐惧和羞耻。
六
一千个邪念喧扰地
闯入我胸；
它们使我食不下咽，
并夺去我的安息。
七
主啊，我病了，求你垂听我的呼求，
释放我的灵：
说，你岂能让一个罪人死，
他渴望为你而活？

## 84 Satan Returning / 84 撒但回来

===Original===
By Newton
Matthew 12:43﻿–﻿45.
I
When Jesus claims the sinner’s heart,
 

 Where Satan ruled before,
 

 The evil spirit must depart,
 

 And dares return no more.
II
But when he goes without constraint,
 

 And wanders from his home,
 

 Although withdrawn, ’tis but a feint﻿—
 

 He means again to come.
III
Some outward change perhaps is seen
 

 If Satan quit the place;
 

 But though the house seem swept and clean.
 

 ’Tis destitute of grace.
IV
Except the Saviour dwell and reign
 

 Within the sinner’s mind,
 

 Satan, when he returns again,
 

 Will easy entrance find.
V
With rage and malice sevenfold
 

 He then resumes his sway;
 

 No more by checks to be control’d,
 

 No more to go away.
VI
The sinner’s former state was bad,
 

 But worse the latter far;
 

 He lives possessed, blind, and mad,
 

 And dies in dark despair.
VII
Lord, save me from this dreadful end!
 

 And from this heart of mine
 

 O drive and keep away the fiend
 

 Who fears no voice but thine.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
马太福音 12:43–45。
一
当耶稣认领罪人的心，
那里从前由撒但统治，
污灵必须离去，
再不敢回来。
二
但当他不受约束地出去，
从他的住所流离，
虽然退隐，那不过是诡计——
他存意再来。
三
撒但若离开那地，
或许外显的改变可见；
虽然房屋似已打扫干净，
却缺乏恩典。
四
除非救主住在掌权
在罪人的心思之内，
撒但当他再回来时，
必轻易寻见入口。
五
带着七倍的狂怒与恶毒，
他那时重掌霸权；
不再受拦阻辖制，
不再离去。
六
罪人先前的境况是坏，
但后者更坏得远；
他活着被附着、瞎、癫狂，
死在黑暗的绝望中。
七
主啊，救我脱离这可怕的结局！
并从我这颗心中
驱除并拦阻那恶魔，
他只惧怕你的声音。

## 85 The Sower / 85 撒种的

===Original===
By Cowper
Matthew 13:3.
I
Ye sons of earth, prepare the plough,
 

 Break up your fallow ground!
 

 The sower is gone forth to sow,
 

 And scatter blessings round.
II
The seed that finds a stony soil
 

 Shoots forth a hasty blade;
 

 But ill repays the sower’s toil﻿—
 

 Soon wither’d, scorch’d, and dead.
III
The thorny ground is sure to baulk
 

 All hopes of harvest there;
 

 We find a tall and sickly stalk,
 

 But not the fruitful ear.
IV
The beaten path and highway-side
 

 Receive the trust in vain;
 

 The watchful birds the spoil divide,
 

 And pick up all the grain.
V
But where the Lord of grace and power
 

 Has bless’d the happy field,
 

 How plenteous is the golden store
 

 The deep-wrought furrows yield!
VI
Father of mercies, we have need
 

 Of thy preparing grace;
 

 Let the same hand that gives the seed
 

 Provide a fruitful place.
===Chinese===
库珀 作
马太福音 13:3。
一
地之子们哪，要备好犁，
开垦你们的荒地！
撒种的已出去撒种，
四散播撒赐福。
二
落在石头土壤的种子
急速抽出嫩芽；
却亏负撒种者的辛劳——
不久枯萎、焦灼、死亡。
三
荆棘地必使那里
丰收的一切盼望落空；
我们寻见高而病弱的秆，
却寻不见饱满的穗。
四
被踏平的小径和路旁
徒然接受这信託；
警醒的飞鸟分取赃物，
拾去一切的谷粒。
五
但在恩典与大能的主
已赐福的良田，
深耕的犁沟何等丰富地
出产金黄的储存！
六
怜悯之父，我们需要
你预备的恩典；
愿赐种子的同一只手，
供备一块结果的沃土。

## 86 The Wheat and Tares / 86 麦子与稗子

===Original===
By Newton
Matthew 13:37﻿–﻿42.
I
Though in the outward church below
 

 The wheat and tares together grow,
 

 Jesus ere long will weed the crop,
 

 And pluck the tares in anger up.
II
Will it relieve their horrors there
 

 To recollect their stations here﻿—
 

 How much they heard, how much they knew,
 

 How long amongst the wheat they grew?
III
Oh! this will aggravate their case;
 

 They perish’d under means of grace;
 

 To them the word of life and faith
 

 Became an instrument of death.
IV
We seem alike when thus we meet,
 

 Strangers might think we all are wheat;
 

 But to the Lord’s all-searching eyes
 

 Each heart appears without disguise.
V
The tares are spared for various ends;
 

 Some for the sake of praying friends,
 

 Others the Lord, against their will,
 

 Employs his counsels to fulfill.
VI
But though they grow so tall and strong,
 

 His plan will not require them long;
 

 In harvest, when he saves his own,
 

 The tares shall into hell be thrown.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
马太福音 13:37–42。
一
虽然在外面的教会里，
麦子与稗子一同生长，
耶稣不久必清拣庄稼，
在怒中拔起稗子。
二
回想他们在地上的身份
能否减轻那里的恐怖——
他们听了多少、知道多少，
在麦子中生长了多久？
三
哦！这将加重他们的案情；
他们在蒙恩之道下灭亡；
对他们，生命与信心之道
成了死亡的器具。
四
我们这样相聚时看似相同，
生人可能以为我们都是麦子；
但在主全然鉴察的眼中，
每颗心显露无伪。
五
稗子被宽容有各样的目的；
有些为着祷告的朋友，
其余的，主违背他们的意愿，
使用他们来成就他的谋略。
六
虽然他们长得又高又壮，
他的计划不久就不再需要他们；
在收割时，当他拯救自己的，
稗子必被扔进地狱。

## 87 Peter Walking Upon the Water / 87 彼得行走海面

===Original===
By Newton
Matthew 14:28﻿–﻿31.
I
A word from Jesus calms the sea,
 

 The stormy wind controls,
 

 And gives repose and liberty
 

 To tempest-tossed souls.
II
To Peter on the waves he came,
 

 And gave him instant peace;
 

 Thus he to me reveal’d his name,
 

 And bade my sorrows cease.
III
Then fill’d with wonder, joy, and love.
 

 Peter’s request was mine;
 

 Lord, call me down, I long to prove
 

 That I am wholly thine.
IV
Unmoved at all I have to meet
 

 On life’s tempestuous sea,
 

 Hard shall be easy, bitter sweet,
 

 So I may follow thee.
V
He heard and smiled, and bade me try;
 

 I eagerly obey’d;
 

 But when from him I turn’d my eye,
 

 How was my soul dismay’d!
VI
The storm increased on every side,
 

 I felt my spirit shrink!
 

 And soon, with Peter, loud I cried,
 

 “Lord, save me, or I sink.”
VII
Kindly he caught me by the hand,
 

 And said, “Why dost thou fear
 

 Since thou art come at my command.
 

 And I am always near?
VIII
“Upon my promise rest thy hope,
 

 And keep my love in view;
 

 I stand engaged to hold thee up,
 

 And guide thee safely through.”
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
马太福音 14:28–31。
一
耶稣的一句话平静大海，
控制狂风，
并赐安息与自由
给被风暴颠簸的灵魂。
二
他来到海浪上的彼得那里，
即时赐他平安；
这样他向我启示他的名，
吩咐我的忧伤止息。
三
那时充满惊奇、喜乐与爱，
彼得的请求成了我的：
主啊，召我下来，我渴望证明
我完全属你。
四
不为任何我将遭遇的所动，
在人生风暴的海上，
难的必变易，苦的必变甜，
只要我能跟从你。
五
他垂听并微笑，吩咐我尝试；
我热切地顺从；
但当我转眼不看他，
我的灵魂何等惊惶！
六
风暴在四围加增，
我感到我的灵退缩！
不久我便与彼得同声高呼：
「主啊，救我，不然我灭亡。」
七
他仁慈地用手拉住我，
说：「你为何惧怕，
既然你是奉我的命令前来，
而我总在近旁？
八
「要把你的盼望安息在我的应许上，
把我的爱放在眼前；
我已承担要持住你，
并引导你安然经过。」

## 88 The Woman of Canaan / 88 迦南妇人

===Original===
By Newton
Matthew 15:22﻿–﻿28.
I
Prayer an answer will obtain,
 

 Though the Lord awhile delay;
 

 None shall seek his face in vain,
 

 None be empty sent away.
II
When the woman came from Tyre,
 

 And for help to Jesus sought,
 

 Though he granted her desire,
 

 Yet at first he answer’d not.
III
Could she guess at his intent
 

 When he to his foll’wers said,
 

 “I to Israel’s sheep am sent;
 

 Dogs must not have children’s bread’?
IV
She was not of Israel’s seed,
 

 But of Canaan’s wretched race;
 

 Thought herself a dog indeed;
 

 Was not this a hopeless case?
V
Yet although from Canaan sprung,
 

 Though a dog herself she styled,
 

 She had Israel’s faith and tongue,
 

 And was own’d for Abram’s child.
VI
From His word she draws a plea:
 

 “Though unworthy children’s bread,
 

 ’Tis enough for one like me
 

 If with crumbs I may be fed.”
VII
Jesus then his heart reveal’d:
 

 “Woman, canst thou thus believe?
 

 I to thy petition yield,
 

 All that thou canst wish receive.”
VIII
’Tis a pattern set for us,
 

 How we ought to wait and pray;
 

 None who plead and wrestle thus
 

 Shall be empty sent away.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
马太福音 15:22–28。
一
祷告必得着回应，
虽然主耽延一时；
无人会徒然寻求他的面，
无人空手被遣回。
二
当那妇人从推罗来，
向耶稣寻求帮助，
他虽应允她的所愿，
但起初却不回答。
三
她岂能猜到他的用意，
当他对门徒说：
「我奉差到以色列的羊那里；
狗不可吃儿女的饼」？
四
她不是以色列的后裔，
而是迦南悲惨的种族；
自认确是一条狗；
这岂不是一个无望的病案？
五
然而她虽出于迦南，
虽自称为一条狗，
她却有以色列的信心与口舌，
并被认作亚伯拉罕的儿女。
六
从他的话她引出恳求：
「虽不配儿女的饼，
但对我这样的人已足够，
若能用碎渣得喂养。」
七
耶稣于是显露他的心：
「妇人，你竟能这样信吗？
我应允你的祈求，
领受你一切所愿的。」
八
这是为我们设立的榜样，
我们当怎样等候祷告；
凡这样恳求摔跤的，
无人空手被遣回。

## 89 What Think Ye of Christ? / 89 你们对基督的看法如何？

===Original===
By Newton
Matthew 22:42.
I
What think you of Christ is the test
 

 To try both your state and your scheme;
 

 You cannot be right in the rest
 

 Unless you think rightly of him.
 

 As Jesus appears in your view,
 

 As he is beloved or not,
 

 So God is disposed to you,
 

 And mercy or wrath are your lot.
II
Some take him a creature to be,
 

 A man, or an angel at most:
 

 Sure these have not feelings like me,
 

 Nor know themselves wretched and lost.
 

 So guilty, so helpless am I,
 

 I durst not confide in his blood,
 

 Nor on his protection rely,
 

 Unless I were sure he is God.
III
Some call him a Saviour in word,
 

 But mix their own works with his plan;
 

 And hope he his help will afford,
 

 When they have done all that they can:
 

 If doings prove rather too light,
 

 (A little, they own, they may fail,)
 

 They purpose to make up full weight,
 

 By casting his name in the scale.
IV
Some style him the pearl of great price,
 

 And say he’s the fountain of joys;
 

 Yet feed upon folly and vice,
 

 And cleave to the world and its toys;
 

 Like Judas, the Saviour they kiss,
 

 And while they salute him, betray;
 

 Ah! what will profession like this
 

 Avail in his terrible day?
V
If ask’d what of Jesus I think﻿—
 

 Though still my best thoughts are but poor,
 

 I say he’s my meat and my drink,
 

 My life and my strength and my store;
 

 My Shepherd, my Husband, my Friend,
 

 My Saviour from sin and from thrall,
 

 My hope from beginning to end,
 

 My portion, my Lord, and my all.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
马太福音 22:42。
一
你们对基督的看法是测试，
试验你们的光景与体系；
你们不能在其余的事上正确，
除非你们对他想法正确。
耶稣在你眼中怎样显现，
他是否被爱，
神对你的意念也怎样，
怜悯或忿怒是你的分。
二
有人把他当作受造者，
一个人，或至多一位天使：
这样的人必没有像我这样的感受，
也不知自己悲惨失丧。
我如此有罪、如此无助，
我不敢信靠他的血，
也不敢倚赖他的保护，
除非我确知他是神。
三
有人口头称他救主，
却把自己的行为混入他的计划；
并盼望他会供帮助，
当他们已尽其所能：
若行为显得太轻，
（他们承认可能略有亏缺，）
他们打算补足满秤，
把他的名投进秤盘。
四
有人称他为重价的珠子，
说他是喜乐的泉源；
却以愚妄和罪恶为粮，
并恋慕世界和它的玩具；
像犹大，他们亲吻救主，
一面行礼一面出卖；
啊！这样的表白
在他可怕之日有何益？
五
若问我怎样看耶稣——
虽然我最好的想法仍贫乏，
我说他是我的粮与我的杯，
我的生命、我的力量、我的储存；
我的牧者、我的丈夫、我的朋友，
救我脱离罪与奴役的救主，
我从始至终的盼望，
我的份、我的主、我的一切。

## 90 The Foolish Virgins.9 / 90 愚拙的童女.9

===Original===
By Newton
Matthew 25:1.
I
When descending from the sky
 

 The Bridegroom shall appear,
 

 And the solemn midnight cry
 

 Shall call professors near,
 

 How the sound our hearts will damp﻿—
 

 How will shame o’erspread each face
 

 If we only have a lamp
 

 Without the oil of grace!
II
Foolish virgins then will wake
 

 And seek for a supply;
 

 But in vain the pains they take
 

 To borrow or to buy:
 

 Then with those they now despise
 

 Earnestly they’ll wish to share;
 

 But the best among the wise
 

 Will have no oil to spare.
III
Wise are they, and truly blest,
 

 Who then shall ready be!
 

 But despair will seize the rest,
 

 And dreadful misery:
 

 Once, they’ll cry, we scorn’d to doubt,
 

 Though in lies our trust we put;
 

 Now our lamp of hope is out﻿—
 

 The door of mercy shut.
IV
If they then presume to plead,
 

 “Lord, open to us now;
 

 We on earth have heard and pray’d,
 

 And with thy saints did bow:”
 

 He will answer from his throne
 

 “Though you with my people mix’d,
 

 Yet to me you ne’er were known;
 

 Depart, your doom is fix’d.”
V
Oh that none who worship here
 

 May hear that word, depart!
 

 Lord, impress a godly fear
 

 On each professor’s heart:
 

 Help us, Lord, to search the camp,
 

 Let us not ourselves beguile,
 

 Trusting to a dying lamp,
 

 Without a stock of oil.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
马太福音 25:1。
一
当新郎从天而降
显现之时，
那严肃的午夜呼声
召宣信者前来，
这声音将何等使我们的心消沉——
羞愧将何等铺满每张脸，
若我们只有一盏灯
却没有恩典的油！
二
愚拙的童女那时必醒来
寻求供应；
但她们为借贷或购买
所受的劳苦尽属徒然：
那时她们必恳切盼望
与她们现今所轻视的同享；
但智者中最好的
也没有多余的油可分。
三
有智慧的是那些，
那时已预备好的人，真是有福！
但绝望必抓住其余的人，
还有可怕的悲惨：
「曾几何时，」她们必喊，「我们耻于怀疑，
虽然我们把信託放在虚谎里；
如今我们盼望的灯已熄——
怜悯的门已关。」
四
他们若那时擅敢恳求：
「主啊，现在给我们开门；
我们在地上曾听见、曾祷告，
并曾与你的圣徒同拜：」
他必从宝座回答：
「虽然你们与我的子民相混，
你们却从未被我认识；
离去吧，你们的定命已定。」
五
哦，愿在此敬拜的人中，
无一人听见那话：「离去！」
主啊，愿将敬虔的惧怕
印在每个宣信者的心上：
主啊，帮助我们察验营盘，
愿我们不要自欺，
信靠一盏将残的灯，
而没有油的储存。

## 91 Peter Sinning and Repenting / 91 彼得的犯罪与悔改

===Original===
By Newton
Matthew 26:73.
I
When Peter boasted, soon he fell,
 

 Yet was by grace restored;
 

 His case should be regarded well
 

 By all who fear the Lord.
II
A voice it has, and helping hand,
 

 Backsliders to recall;
 

 And cautions those who think they stand,
 

 Lest suddenly they fall.
III
He said, “Whatever others do,
 

 With Jesus I’ll abide;”
 

 Yet soon amidst a murd’rous crew
 

 His suff’ring Lord denied.
IV
He who had been so bold before
 

 Now trembled like a leaf﻿—
 

 Not only lied, but cursed and swore,
 

 To gain the more belief.
V
While he blasphemed he heard the cock,
 

 And Jesus look’d in love;
 

 At once, as if by lightning struck,
 

 His tongue forebore to move.
VI
Deliver’d thus from Satan’s snare,
 

 He starts, as from a sleep;
 

 His Saviour’s look he could not bear,
 

 But hasted forth to weep.
VII
But sure the faithful cock had crow’d
 

 A hundred times in vain
 

 Had not the Lord that look bestow’d
 

 The meaning to explain.
VIII
As I, like Peter, vows have made,
 

 Yet acted Peter’s part;
 

 So conscience, like the cock, upbraids
 

 My base ungrateful heart.
IX
Lord Jesus, hear a sinner’s cry,
 

 My broken peace renew;
 

 And grant one pitying look, that I
 

 May weep with Peter too.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
马太福音 26:73。
一
当彼得自夸，他不久就跌倒，
却蒙恩典复原；
他的案例当被好好留意，
被一切敬畏主的人。
二
它有声音，也有帮助之手，
挽回后退的人；
并警诫那些自以为站立的人，
免得他们突然跌倒。
三
他曾说：「无论别人怎样，
我必与耶稣同在；」
不久却在凶杀的人群中
否认他受苦的主。
四
那先前如此大胆的
如今如叶颤抖——
不但说谎，还起咒发誓，
为得着更多的信。
五
当他亵渎时他听见鸡叫，
耶稣以爱观看；
立时，如被雷击，
他的舌不敢再动。
六
这样脱离了撒但的网罗，
他惊起，如从睡中；
他不能承受救主的一望，
便急忙出去痛哭。
七
但那忠信的鸡纵然鸣了
一百次也是徒然，
若非主赐下那一望，
解释其中的含义。
八
我既像彼得立过誓愿，
却行了彼得所行的；
良心也像那鸡，
责备我卑劣忘恩的心。
九
主耶稣，垂听罪人的呼求，
更新我破碎的平安；
并赐一个怜悯的一望，使我
也能与彼得同哭。

## 92 The Legion Dispossessed / 92 群鬼被逐

===Original===
By Newton
Mark 5:18﻿–﻿19.
I
Legion was my name by nature,
 

 Satan raged within my breast,
 

 Never misery was greater,
 

 Never sinner more possess’d:
 

 Mischievous to all around me,
 

 To myself the greatest foe;
 

 Thus I was when Jesus found me,
 

 Fill’d with madness, sin, and woe.
II
Yet in this forlorn condition,
 

 When he came to see me free,
 

 I replied to my Physician,
 

 “What have I to do with thee?”
 

 But he would not be prevented,
 

 Rescued me against my will:
 

 Had he stayed till I consented
 

 I had been a captive still.
III
“Satan, though thou fain wouldst have it,
 

 Know this soul is none of thine;
 

 I have shed my blood to save it,
 

 Now I challenge it for mine;10
 

 Though it long has thee resembled,
 

 Henceforth it shall me obey:
 

 Thus He spoke, while Satan trembled,
 

 Gnash’d his teeth, and fled away.
IV
Thus my frantic soul He heal’d,
 

 Bid my sins and sorrows cease;
 

 “Take,” said he, “my pardon sealed,
 

 I have saved thee﻿—go in peace.”
 

 Rather take me, Lord, to heaven,
 

 Now thy love and grace I know;
 

 Since thou hast my sins forgiven,
 

 Why should I remain below?
V
“Love,” he said, “will sweeten labours;
 

 Thou hast something yet to do;
 

 Go and tell your friends and neighbours
 

 What my love has done for you;
 

 Live to manifest my glory,
 

 Wait for heaven a little space:
 

 Sinners, when they hear thy story,
 

 Will repent and seek my face.”
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
马可福音 5:18–19。
一
群鬼按本性是我的名，
撒但在我胸中狂怒，
从无悲惨比这更大，
从无罪人被附更甚：
对四围的人尽是危害，
对自己是最大的仇敌；
耶稣寻见我时我正是这样，
充满疯狂、罪与祸。
二
然而在这孤苦的境况，
当他来释放我，
我回答我的医生：
「我与你有什么相干？」
但他不容拦阻，
违背我的意愿拯救了我：
他若等到我同意，
我必仍是俘虏。
三
「撒但，虽然你巴不得如此，
要知道这灵魂不属于你；
我已流我的血拯救它，
如今我认它为我的；10
虽然它长久像你，
从今它必顺从我：
他这样说时，撒但战兢，
咬牙切齿，逃遁而去。
四
这样他医治了我狂乱的灵魂，
吩咐我的罪与忧伤止息；
「拿去，」他说，「我盖印的赦免，
我救了你——平平安安地去吧。」
主啊，倒不如接我上天，
如今我认识你的爱与恩典；
你既赦免了我的罪，
我为何还留在下面？
五
「爱，」他说，「会甘化劳苦；
你还有当作的事；
去告诉你的朋友邻舍
我的爱为你作了什么；
活着彰显我的荣耀，
稍稍等候天家一时：
罪人们听见你的故事，
必悔改寻求我的面。」

## 93 The Ruler’s Daughter Raised / 93 睚鲁的女儿复活

===Original===
By Newton
Mark 5:39﻿–﻿42.
I
Could the creatures help or ease us
 

 Seldom should we think of prayer;
 

 Few, if any, come to Jesus
 

 Till reduced to self-despair:
 

 Long we either slight or doubt him;
 

 But when all the means we try
 

 Prove we cannot do without him,
 

 Then at last to him we cry.
II
Thus the ruler, when his daughter
 

 Suffer’d much, though Christ was nigh,
 

 Still deferr’d it till he thought her
 

 At the very point to die:
 

 Though he mourn’d for her condition,
 

 He did not entreat the Lord
 

 Till he found that no physician
 

 But himself could help afford.
III
Jesus did not once upbraid him,
 

 That he had no sooner come;
 

 But a gracious answer made him,
 

 And went straightway with him home:
 

 Yet his faith was put to trial
 

 When his servants came, and said,
 

 Though he gave thee no denial,
 

 ’Tis too late﻿—the child is dead.”
IV
Jesus, to prevent his grieving,
 

 Kindly spoke, and eased his pain;
 

 “Be not fearful, but believing﻿—
 

 Thou shalt see her live again.”
 

 When he found the people weeping,
 

 “Cease,” he said, “no longer mourn;
 

 For she is not dead, but sleeping:”
 

 Then they laughed him to scorn.
V
O thou meek and lowly Saviour,
 

 How determined is thy love!
 

 Not this rude unkind behaviour
 

 Could thy gracious purpose move.
 

 Soon as he the room had enter’d,
 

 Spoke, and took her by the hand,
 

 Death at once his prey surrender’d,
 

 And she lived at his command.
VI
Fear not then, distress’d believer,
 

 Venture on his mighty name;
 

 He is able to deliver,
 

 And his love is still the same:
 

 Can his pity or his power
 

 Suffer thee to pray in vain?
 

 Wait but his appointed hour,
 

 And thy suit thou shalt obtain.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
马可福音 5:39–42。
一
受造之物若能帮助安慰我们，
我们就很少想到祷告；
少有人（若有）来就耶稣，
直到被降到绝望：
我们长久轻看或怀疑他；
但当我们试尽一切方法
证明我们不能没有他，
那时才向他呼求。
二
这样那管会堂的，当他的女儿
病重之时，虽然基督在近旁，
仍一直耽延，直到他以为她
正处在死的边缘：
他虽为她的病情哀恸，
却未恳求主，
直到他发现除他以外
没有医生能施帮助。
三
耶稣没有一次责备他，
怪他不早来；
而是给他恩慈的回应，
立时随他回家：
但他的信心受了试验，
当他的仆人来，说：
「虽然他没有拒绝你，
但太迟了——孩子死了。」
四
耶稣为免他忧伤，
恩慈地说话，减轻他的痛：
「不要怕，只要信——
你必再见她活过来。」
当他见人们在哀哭，
「止住吧，」他说，「不要再哀恸；
她不是死了，是睡着了：」
那时他们耻笑他。
五
哦，你这柔和谦卑的救主，
你的爱何等坚定！
这样粗鲁不仁的行为
不能动摇你恩慈的旨意。
他一进入房间，
说话，拉着她的手，
死亡立时交出猎物，
她因他的命令活了。
六
受苦的信徒，那就不要惧怕，
放胆靠他大能的名；
他能施行拯救，
他的爱仍是一样：
他的怜悯或他的大能
岂容你祷告徒然？
只要等候他所定的时辰，
你必得着你的所求。

## 94 But One Loaf.11 / 94 只有一个饼.11

===Original===
By Newton
Mark 8:14.
I
When the disciples cross’d the lake
 

 With but one loaf on board,
 

 How strangely did their hearts mistake
 

 The caution of their Lord!
II
“The leaven of the Pharisees
 

 Beware,” the Saviour said;
 

 They thought, It is because he sees
 

 We have forgotten bread.
III
It seems they had forgotten too
 

 What their own eyes had view’d﻿—
 

 How, with what scarce sufficed for few,
 

 He fed a multitude.
IV
If five small loaves, by his command,
 

 Could many thousands serve,
 

 Might they not trust his gracious hand.
 

 That they should never starve?
V
They oft his power and love had known,
 

 And doubtless were to blame;
 

 But we have reason good to own
 

 That we are just the same.
VI
How often has he brought relief,
 

 And every want supplied!
 

 Yet soon, again, our unbelief
 

 Says, “Can the Lord provide?”
VII
Be thankful for one loaf to-day,
 

 Though that be all your store:
 

 To-morrow, if you trust and pray,
 

 Shall timely bring you more.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
马可福音 8:14。
一
当门徒渡过那湖，
船上只有一个饼，
他们的心何等奇怪地误解
他们主的提醒！
二
「要防备
法利赛人的酵，」救主说；
他们想：这是因为他看见
我们忘了带饼。
三
他们似乎也忘了
他们亲眼所见的——
他如何用仅够少数人的食物，
喂饱了众人。
四
五个小饼若奉他的命令
能服事几千人，
他们岂不能信靠他恩慈的手，
相信必不至于挨饿？
五
他们屡次经历他的大能与爱，
无疑当受责备；
但我们有充分理由承认
我们正和他们一样。
六
他曾何等多次带来解救，
供备每一需用！
然而不久，我们的不信
又说：「主能供备吗？」
七
今天要为这一个饼感谢，
虽然那是你们全部的储存：
明天，你们若信靠祷告，
必及时带给你们更多。

## 95 Bartimeus / 95 巴底买

===Original===
By Newton
Mark 10:47﻿–﻿48.
I
“Mercy, thou son of David!”
 

 Thus blind Bartimeus pray’d;
 

 “Others by thy word are saved,
 

 Now to me afford thine aid.”
 

 Many for his crying chid him,
 

 But he call’d the louder still,
 

 Till the gracious Saviour bid him
 

 “Come, and ask me what you will.”
II
Money was not what he wanted,
 

 Though by begging used to live;
 

 But he ask’d, and Jesus granted
 

 Alms which none but he could give:
 

 “Lord, remove this grievous blindness,
 

 Let my eyes behold the day;”
 

 Straight he saw and, won by kindness,
 

 Follow’d Jesus in the way.
III
Oh! methinks I hear him praising,
 

 Publishing to all around,
 

 “Friends, is not my case amazing?
 

 What a Saviour I have found!
 

 Oh! that all the blind but knew him,
 

 And would be advised by me;
 

 Surely they would hasten to him,
 

 He would cause them all to see.”
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
马可福音 10:47–48。
一
「大卫的子孙，可怜我吧！」
瞎眼的巴底买这样祷告；
「别人借你的话得救，
现在求你施助给我。」
多人因他喊叫责备他，
但他喊得更大声，
直到恩慈的救主吩咐他
「来，随你求什么。」
二
金钱不是他所要的，
虽然他惯于乞讨度日；
但他求了，耶稣赐了
惟独他能给的施舍：
「主啊，除去这沉重的瞎眼，
让我的眼得见白日；」
他立时看见，并被恩慈赢得，
在路上跟从了耶稣。
三
哦！我想我听见他赞美，
向四围的人传扬：
「朋友们，我的案例岂不奇妙？
我寻见了何等的救主！
哦，愿一切瞎眼的人认识他，
并肯听我的劝；
他们必速速就了他去，
他必使他们全都看见。」

## 96 The House of Prayer / 96 祷告的殿

===Original===
By Cowper
Mark 11:17.
I
Thy mansion is the Christian’s heart,
 

 O Lord, thy dwelling-place secure!
 

 Bid the unruly throng depart,
 

 And leave the consecrated door.
II
Devoted as it is to thee,
 

 A thievish swarm frequents the place;
 

 They steal away my joys from me,
 

 And rob my Saviour of his praise.
III
There too a sharp designing trade,
 

 Sin, Satan, and the world maintain;
 

 Nor cease to press me, and persuade,
 

 To part with ease and purchase pain.
IV
I know them, and I hate their din,
 

 Am weary of the bustling crowd;
 

 But while their voice is heard within
 

 I cannot serve thee as I would.
V
Oh! for the joy thy presence gives﻿—
 

 What peace shall reign when thou art here!
 

 Thy presence makes this den of thieves
 

 A calm delightful house of prayer.
VI
And if thou make thy temple shine,
 

 Yet, self-abased, will I adore;
 

 The gold and silver are not mine﻿—
 

 I give thee what was thine before.
===Chinese===
库珀 作
马可福音 11:17。
一
你的宅邸是基督徒的心，
主啊，你安稳的居所！
求你吩咐那不守规矩的人群离去，
离开那分别为圣的门。
二
它既献给你，
一群贼却常来此地；
他们偷去我的喜乐，
并抢夺我救主的赞美。
三
那里也有精明的买卖，
由罪、撒但与世界经营；
不住地压我、劝我，
出卖安逸购买痛苦。
四
我认识他们，恨他们的喧哗，
厌烦那攘扰的人群；
但他们的声音既在里面被听见，
我就不能照所愿的事奉你。
五
哦！为你同在所给的喜乐——
你在这里时必有何等的平安！
你的同在使这贼窝
成为安静可悦的祷告之殿。
六
你若使你的殿发光，
我仍必自卑地敬拜；
金银都不是我的——
我把原属你的归还你。

## 97 The Blasted Fig-Tree / 97 枯萎的无花果树

===Original===
By Newton
Mark 11:20.
I
One awful word which Jesus spoke
 

 Against the tree which bore no fruit,
 

 More piercing than the lightning’s stroke,
 

 Blasted and dried it to the root.
II
But could a tree the Lord offend,
 

 To make him show his anger thus!
 

 He surely had, a further end﻿—
 

 To be a warning word to us.
III
The fig-tree by its leaves was known;
 

 But having not a fig to show,
 

 It brought a heavy sentence down﻿—
 

 “Let none hereafter on thee grow.”
IV
Too many, who the gospel hear,
 

 Whom Satan blinds and sin deceives
 

 We to this fig-tree may compare,
 

 They yield no fruit, but only leaves
V
Knowledge and zeal and gifts and talk.
 

 Unless combined with faith and love,
 

 And witnessed by a gospel-walk,
 

 Will not a true profession prove.
VI
Without the fruit the Lord expects,
 

 Knowledge will make our state the worse;
 

 The barren trees he still rejects,
 

 And soon will blast them with his curse.
VII
O Lord, unite our hearts in prayer!
 

 On each of us thy Spirit send,
 

 That we the fruits of grace may bear,
 

 And find acceptance in the end.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
马可福音 11:20。
一
耶稣所说一句可怕的话，
对着那不结果子的树，
比闪电的打击更穿透，
枯干它直到根。
二
但一棵树岂能触犯主，
使他这样显出怒气！
他确有更远的目的——
作对我们的一句警言。
三
无花果树凭叶子被认出；
但拿不出一颗无花果，
便招来沉重的判语——
「愿此后无人再从你收取。」
四
太多听见福音的人，
被撒但弄瞎、被罪欺骗，
我们可比这无花果树，
不出果子，只有叶子。
五
知识、热心、恩赐与谈论，
若不与信心和爱结合，
并有福音的行事为证，
不能证明真实的表白。
六
没有主所期望的果子，
知识必使我们的光景更糟；
他仍弃绝不结果子的树，
不久必以咒诅枯干它们。
七
主啊，愿联合我们的心祷告！
将你的圣灵赐给我们每个人，
使我们能结出恩典的果子，
并在最终蒙悦纳。

## 98 The Two Debtors / 98 两个欠债的人

===Original===
By Newton
Luke 7:47.
I
Once a woman silent stood
 

 While Jesus sat at meat;
 

 From her eyes she pour’d a flood,
 

 To wash his sacred feet:
 

 Shame and wonder, joy and love,
 

 All at once possess’d her mind,
 

 That she e’er so vile could prove,
 

 Yet now forgiveness find.
II
“How came this vile woman here?
 

 Will Jesus notice such?
 

 Sure, if he a prophet were,
 

 He would disdain her touch!”
 

 Simon thus, with scornful heart,
 

 Slighted one whom Jesus loved;
 

 But her Saviour took her part,
 

 And thus his pride reproved:
III
“If two men in debt were bound,
 

 One less, the other more,
 

 Fifty, or five hundred pound,
 

 And both alike were poor;
 

 Should the lender both forgive
 

 When he saw them both distress’d,
 

 Which of them would you believe
 

 Engaged to love him best?
IV
“Surely he who most did owe,”
 

 The Pharisee replied:
 

 Then our Lord, “By judging so,
 

 Thou dost for her decide:
 

 Simon, if like her you knew
 

 How much you forgiveness need,
 

 You like her had acted too,
 

 And welcomed me indeed.
V
“When the load of sin is felt,
 

 And much forgiveness known
 

 Then the heart of course will melt,
 

 Though hard before as stone:
 

 Blame not then her love and tears,
 

 Greatly she in debt has been;
 

 But I have removed her fears,
 

 And pardon’d all her sin.”
VI
When I read this woman’s case,
 

 Her love and humble zeal,
 

 I confess, with shame of face,
 

 My heart is made of steel.
 

 Much has been forgiven to me,
 

 Jesus paid my heavy score;
 

 What a creature must I be,
 

 That I can love no more!
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
路加福音 7:47。
一
曾有一个妇人静默站立，
当耶稣坐席时；
她眼中倾出泪水如洪，
洗他圣洁的脚：
羞愧、惊奇、喜乐与爱，
一时全占有她的心，
她曾证明如此卑劣，
如今却寻得赦免。
二
「这卑劣妇人怎会来此？
耶稣会注意这样的人吗？
确实，他若是先知，
必藐视她的触摸！」
西门就这样怀着轻蔑的心，
轻看耶稣所爱的；
但她的救主站在她一边，
这样责备了他的骄傲：
三
「若有两个人欠债被绑，
一个少些，另一个多些，
五十或五百镑，
两人同样的贫穷；
债主若见他们都困苦，
便赦免他们两个，
你相信他们哪一个
会更爱他？」
四
「确然是那欠更多的，」
法利赛人回答：
我们的主便说：「你既这样判断，
便是为她定了案：
西门，你若像她那样知道
你需要多少赦免，
你也会像她那样行，
并真实地欢迎我。
五
「当罪的重担被感受，
并知深得赦免，
心自然便熔化，
虽从前硬如石头：
那就不要责备她的爱与眼泪，
她曾欠下大债；
但我已除去她的恐惧，
并赦免她一切的罪。」
六
当我读到这妇人的案例，
她的爱与谦卑的热忱，
我满脸羞愧地承认，
我的心是钢做的。
我蒙了很多赦免，
耶稣付了我沉重的账；
我必是何等的受造者，
竟能爱得不再更多！

## 99 The Good Samaritan / 99 好撒玛利亚人

===Original===
By Newton
Luke 10:33﻿–﻿35.
I
How kind the good Samaritan
 

 To him who fell among the thieves!
 

 Thus Jesus pities fallen man,
 

 And heals the wounds the soul receives.
II
Oh! I remember well the day
 

 When, sorely wounded, nearly slain,
 

 Like that poor man, I bleeding lay,
 

 And groan’d for help, but groan’d in vain.
III
Men saw me in this helpless case,
 

 And pass’d without compassion by;
 

 Each neighbour turn’d away his face,
 

 Unmoved by my mournful cry.
IV
But He whose name had been my scorn
 

 (As Jews Samaritans despise)
 

 Came, when he saw me thus forlorn,
 

 With love and pity in his eyes.
V
Gently he raised me from the ground,
 

 Press’d me to lean upon his arm,
 

 And into every gaping wound
 

 He pour’d his own all-healing balm.
VI
Unto his church my steps he led,
 

 The house prepared for sinners lost,
 

 Gave charge I should be clothed and fed,
 

 And took upon him all the cost.
VII
Thus saved from death, from want secured,
 

 I wait till he again shall come
 

 (When I shall be completely cured,)
 

 And take me to his heavenly home.
VIII
There, through eternal, boundless days,
 

 When Nature’s wheel no longer rolls,
 

 How shall I love, adore, and praise
 

 This good Samaritan to souls!
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
路加福音 10:33–35。
一
好撒玛利亚人何等仁慈，
对那落在强盗手中的！
耶稣就这样怜恤堕落的人，
医治灵魂所受的伤。
二
哦！我清楚记得那日，
当我重伤、几乎丧命，
像那可怜的人，我流血躺着，
呻吟求救，却徒然呻吟。
三
人见我在这无助的境况，
毫无怜悯地经过；
每个邻人转脸不顾，
不为我的哀声所动。
四
但那位名曾被我看轻的
（如犹太人藐视撒玛利亚人）
来，当他见我这样孤苦，
眼中带着爱与怜恤。
五
他温柔地把我从地上扶起，
让我倚靠他的膀臂，
向每个裂开的伤口，
倾倒他自己全能的膏油。
六
他领我的脚步到他的教会，
那为失丧罪人预备的家，
吩咐给我衣穿、给我饭吃，
并承担一切费用。
七
这样从死亡得救、脱离匮乏，
我等候他再来
（那时我必完全得医治），
并接我到他天上的家。
八
在那里，经过永恒无尽的岁月，
当自然之轮不再转动，
我何等要爱、敬拜并赞美
这位灵魂的好撒玛利亚人！

## 100 Martha and Mary / 100 马大与马利亚

===Original===
By Newton
Luke 10:38﻿–﻿42.
I
Martha her love and joy express’d
 

 By care to entertain her guest;
 

 While Mary sat to hear her Lord,
 

 And could not bear to lose a word.
II
The principle, in both the same,
 

 Produced in each a different aim;
 

 The one to feast the Lord was led,
 

 The other waited to be fed.
III
But Mary chose the better part,
 

 Her Saviour’s words refresh’d her heart,
 

 While busy Martha angry grew,
 

 And lost her time and temper too.
IV
With warmth she to her sister spoke,
 

 But brought upon herself rebuke:
 

 “One thing is needful, and but one,
 

 Why do thy thoughts on many run?”
V
How oft are we like Martha vex’d,
 

 Encumber’d, hurried, and perplex’d,
 

 While trifles so engross our thought
 

 The one thing needful is forgot!
VI
Lord, teach us this one thing to choose,
 

 Which they who gain can never lose﻿—
 

 Sufficient in itself alone,
 

 And needful, were the world our own.
VII
Let grov’ling hearts the world admire﻿—
 

 Thy love is all that I require!
 

 Gladly I may the rest resign,
 

 If the one needful thing be mine!
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
路加福音 10:38–42。
一
马大借着殷勤款待宾客
表达她的爱与喜乐；
马利亚却坐着听她主，
不舍漏掉一个字。
二
那原则，在两人里面相同，
在各自身上产生不同的目标；
一位受引导要设宴款待主，
另一位等候得着喂养。
三
但马利亚选择了那上好的福分，
她救主的话复苏她的心，
忙碌的马大却动了怒，
失了她的时间，也失了脾气。
四
她激动地向她妹妹说话，
却给自己招来责备：
「不可少的只有一件，
你的思虑为何奔向许多？」
五
我们何等常像马大烦恼，
负重、匆忙又困惑，
琐事如此占据思想，
那惟一需要的事被忘记！
六
主啊，教我们选择这一件事，
得着的人永不能失——
它本身独自足够，
即使世界是我们的也属需要。
七
让卑下的心去羡慕世界——
你的爱是我所要的一切！
我甘愿舍弃其余，
若那惟一需要的事属我！

## 101 The Heart Taken / 101 被夺取的心

===Original===
By Newton
Luke 11:21﻿–﻿22.
I
The castle of the human heart,
 

 Strong in its native sin,
 

 Is guarded well in every part
 

 By him who dwells within.
II
For Satan there in arms resides,
 

 And calls the place his own﻿—
 

 With care against assaults provides,
 

 And rules as on a throne.
III
Each traitor thought on him, as chief,
 

 In blind obedience waits;
 

 And pride, self-will, and unbelief,
 

 Are posted at the gates.
IV
Thus Satan for a season reigns,
 

 And keeps his goods in peace;
 

 The soul is pleased to wear his chains,
 

 Nor wishes a release.
V
But Jesus, stronger far than he,
 

 In his appointed hour
 

 Appears, to set his people free
 

 From the usurper’s power.
VI
“This heart I bought with blood,” he says,
 

 “And now it shall be mine;”
 

 His voice the strong one arm’d dismays﻿—
 

 He knows he must resign.
VII
In spite of unbelief and pride,
 

 And self, and Satan’s art,
 

 The gates of brass fly open wide,
 

 And Jesus wins the heart.
VIII
The rebel soul that once withstood
 

 The Saviour’s kindest call,
 

 Rejoices now, by grace subdued,
 

 To serve him with her all.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
路加福音 11:21–22。
一
人心的城堡，
在其本性的罪中坚固，
每个部分都被严密把守，
由住在里面的那位。
二
撒但武装居住在那里，
称那地方为他自己的——
小心防备攻击，
如坐宝座般统治。
三
每个悖逆的思想，以他为首领，
盲目顺服地等候；
骄傲、任性与不信，
被派驻在城门。
四
这样撒但掌权一时，
保守他的财物平安；
灵魂乐于戴他的锁链，
也不愿求得释放。
五
但耶稣比他强得多，
在他所定的时刻
显现，释放他的子民
脱离那篡位者的权势。
六
「这颗心我用血买来，」他说，
「如今它必属我；」
他的声音使那武装的壮士惊惶——
他知道他必须交出。
七
不顾不信与骄傲、
己意与撒但的诡计，
铜门大大敞开，
耶稣赢得了这心。
八
那曾抵挡救主
最恩慈呼召的悖逆灵魂，
如今欢喜，被恩典制伏，
以她的一切事奉他。

## 102 The Worldling / 102 世界之子

===Original===
By Newton
Luke 8:16﻿–﻿21.
I
“My barns are full, my stores increase,
 

 And now, for many years,
 

 Soul, eat and drink, and take thine ease,
 

 Secure from wants and fears.”
II
Thus while a worldling boasted once,
 

 As many now presume,
 

 He heard the Lord himself pronounce
 

 His sudden awful doom.
III
“This night, vain fool, thy soul must pass
 

 Into a world unknown;
 

 And who shall then the stores possess
 

 Which thou hast call’d thine own?”
IV
Thus blinded mortals fondly scheme
 

 For happiness below,
 

 Till death disturbs the pleasing dream,
 

 And they awake to woe.
V
Ah! who can speak the vast dismay
 

 That fills the sinner’s mind,
 

 When, torn by Death’s strong hand away,
 

 He leaves his all behind.
VI
Wretches, who cleave to earthly things,
 

 But are not rich to God,
 

 Their dying hour is full of stings,
 

 And hell their dark abode.
VII
Dear Saviour, make us timely wise
 

 Thy gospel to attend,
 

 That we may live above the skies
 

 When this poor life shall end.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
路加福音 8:16–21。
一
「我的仓房满盈，我的储存加增，
如今，为着许多年，
灵魂啊，吃吧喝吧，安享自在，
稳脱缺乏与恐惧。」
二
当一个世界之子这样夸口，
如今许多人也这样放胆，
他听见主亲自宣告
他突临的可怖厄运。
三
「今夜，愚妄的人，你的灵魂必过去，
进入一个未知的世界；
那时谁将拥有
你所称为你自己的储存？」
四
这样，瞎眼的必死者为
下面的幸福天真筹划，
直到死亡搅扰那美梦，
他们便醒入祸患。
五
啊！谁能述说那充满
罪人心中的巨大惊惶，
当他被死亡的大手撕去，
撇下他的一切。
六
那些紧抓属地之物、
却不富足归神的可怜人，
他们的临终之刻满刺，
地狱是他们的黑暗住处。
七
亲爱的救主，求你使我们及时智慧，
留意你的福音，
使我们在诸天之上活着，
当这可怜的生命终结。

## 103 The Barren Fig-Tree / 103 不结果的无花果树

===Original===
By Newton
Luke 13:6﻿–﻿9.
I
The church a garden is,
 

 In which believers stand
 

 Like ornamental trees
 

 Planted by God’s own hand!
 

 His Spirit waters all their roots,
 

 And every branch abounds with fruits.
II
But other trees there are
 

 In this enclosure grow,
 

 Which, though they promise fair,
 

 Have only leaves to show:
 

 No fruits of grace are on them found,
 

 They stand but cumberers of the ground.
III
The under gardener grieves,
 

 In vain his strength he spends,
 

 For heaps of useless leaves
 

 Afford him small amends:
 

 He hears the Lord his will make known,
 

 To cut the barren fig-trees down.
IV
How difficult his post,
 

 What pangs his bowels move,
 

 To find his wishes cross’d,
 

 His labours useless prove!
 

 His last relief is earnest prayer,
 

 “Lord, spare them yet another year.
V
“Spare them, and let me try
 

 What further means may do;
 

 I’ll fresh manure apply,
 

 My digging I’ll renew:
 

 Who knows but yet they fruit may yield?
 

 If not﻿—’tis just, they must be fell’d.”
VI
If under means of grace
 

 No gracious fruits appear,
 

 It is a dreadful case,
 

 Though God may long forbear:
 

 At length he’ll strike the threaten’d blow,12
 

 And lay the barren fig-tree low.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
路加福音 13:6–9。
一
教会是一个园子，
信徒在其中站立
如装饰的树木，
由神亲手栽种！
他的圣灵浇灌它们所有的根，
每条枝子果实丰盛。
二
但在这围子里
还长着别的树，
虽然它们前景美好，
却只有叶子可显示：
其上寻不见恩典的果子，
它们只是白占地土。
三
园丁忧伤，
徒然花费他的力量，
因为成堆无用的叶子
给他的补偿微少：
他听见主表明他的旨意，
要砍下不结果的无花果树。
四
他的岗位何等艰难，
他的肺腑何等疼痛，
发现他的愿望受挫，
他的劳苦归于无用！
他最后的安慰是恳切的祷告：
「主啊，今年且留着它们。
五
「且留着它们，让我试试
更多的方法能作什么；
我要施上新肥，
我要重新掘土：
谁知它们或许还会结果？
若不——那公义要求它们被砍倒。」
六
若在蒙恩之道下
不显出恩典的果子，
那是可怕的境况，
虽然神可能长久宽容：
最终他必挥出那威吓的一击，12
放倒那不结果的无花果树。

## 104 The Prodigal Son / 104 浪子

===Original===
By Newton
Luke 15:11﻿–﻿24.
I
Afflictions, though they seem severe,
 

 In mercy oft are sent;
 

 They stopp’d the prodigal’s career,
 

 And forced him to repent.
II
Although he no relentings felt
 

 Till he had spent his store,
 

 His stubborn heart began to melt
 

 When famine pinch’d him sore.
III
“What have I gain’d by sin,” he said,
 

 “But hunger, shame, and fear?
 

 My father’s house abounds with bread
 

 While I am starving here.
IV
I’ll go and tell him all I’ve done,
 

 And fall before his face;
 

 Unworthy to be call’d his son,
 

 I’ll seek a servant’s place.”
V
His father saw him coming back,
 

 He saw and ran and smiled,
 

 And threw his arms around the neck
 

 Of his rebellious child.
VI
“Father, I’ve sinn’d﻿—but O forgive!”
 

 “I’ve heard enough,” he said;
 

 “Rejoice, my house﻿—my son’s alive
 

 For whom I mourn’d as dead.
VII
“Now let the fatted calf be slain,
 

 And spread the news around:
 

 My son was dead, but lives again﻿—
 

 Was lost, but now is found.
VIII
’Tis thus the Lord his love reveals
 

 To call poor sinners home;
 

 More than a father’s love he feels.
 

 And welcomes all that come.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
路加福音 15:11–24。
一
患难虽然看似严厉，
却常在怜悯中差来；
它们止住了浪子的奔途，
强制他悔改。
二
虽然他不感悔恨，
直到花尽了储存，
他那顽固的心开始熔化，
当饥荒重压着他。
三
「我从罪得了什么，」他说，
「除了饥饿、羞耻与恐惧？
我父家粮食丰盈，
我却在这里挨饿。
四
我要回去向他承认一切所作，
俯伏在他面前；
不配称为他的儿子，
我要寻求仆人的位置。」
五
他父亲见他回来，
他看见，便跑去微笑，
把双臂环绕
那悖逆孩子的颈项。
六
「父亲，我犯了罪——哦，赦免！」
「我听够了，」他说；
「欢乐吧，我的家——我的儿子活了，
我为他哀恸如死。
七
「现在宰杀肥牛犊，
把消息传遍四围：
我儿子死了，却又活了——
失丧了，如今寻回了。」
八
主就这样启示他的爱，
呼召可怜的罪人回家；
他感受的过于父亲的爱，
并欢迎一切来的人。

## 105 The Rich Man and Lazarus / 105 财主与拉撒路

===Original===
By Newton
Luke 16:19﻿–﻿25.
I
A worlding spent each day
 

 In luxury and state,
 

 While a believer lay
 

 A beggar at his gate.
 

 Think not the Lord’s appointment strange,
 

 Death made a great and lasting change.
II
Death brought the saint release
 

 From want, disease, and scorn;
 

 And to the land of peace
 

 His soul, by angels borne,
 

 In Abraham’s bosom safely placed,
 

 Enjoys an everlasting feast.
III
The rich man also died,
 

 And in a moment fell
 

 From all his pomp and pride
 

 Into the flames of hell:
 

 The beggar’s bliss, from far beheld,
 

 His soul with double anguish fill’d.
IV
“O, Abra’m, send,” he cries,
 

 (But his request was vain,)
 

 “The beggar from the skies
 

 To mitigate my pain!
 

 One drop of water I entreat,
 

 To sooth my tongue’s tormenting heat.”
V
Let all who worldly pelf
 

 And worldly spirits have
 

 Observe, each for himself,
 

 The answer Abra’m gave:
 

 “Remember thou wast fill’d with good,
 

 While the poor beggar pined for good.
VI
“Neglected at thy door,
 

 With tears he begg’d his bread;
 

 But now he weeps no more﻿—
 

 His griefs and pains are fled:
 

 His joys eternally will flow,
 

 While thine expire in endless woe.”
VII
Lord, make us truly wise,
 

 To choose thy people’s lot,
 

 And earthly joys despise,
 

 Which soon will be forgot:
 

 The greatest evil we can fear,
 

 Is to possess our portion here!
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
路加福音 16:19–25。
一
一个世界之子天天
度奢华尊贵的生活，
而一个信徒躺着，
作他门前的乞丐。
不要以为主的安排奇怪，
死亡带来了巨大持久的改变。
二
死亡带给圣徒释放，
脱离匮乏、疾病与轻蔑；
他的灵魂被天使接去，
到平安之地，
安然安置在亚伯拉罕的怀里，
享受永远的筵席。
三
那财主也死了，
霎时从他一切的
浮华与骄傲
坠入地狱的火焰：
远远望见乞丐的福乐，
使他的灵魂充满双重的苦楚。
四
「哦，亚伯拉罕，求你差，」他喊，
（但他的请求徒然，）
「从天上差那乞丐
来减轻我的痛！
我求一滴水，
舒缓我舌燃烧的苦。」
五
愿一切拥有属世钱财
与属世之灵的人，
各为自己留心
亚伯拉罕的回答：
「要记念你从前享美物，
而那可怜的乞丐渴慕美物。
六
「他被撇在你门口，
流泪乞讨他的饼；
但如今他不再哭泣——
他的忧伤与疼痛已逃遁：
他的喜乐必永远涌流，
而你的在无尽的祸患中断绝。」
七
主啊，使我们真有智慧，
选择你百姓的份，
并轻看属世的宴乐，
它们不久必被忘却：
我们所能惧怕的最大邪恶，
是在此地拥有我们的份！

## 106 The Importunate Widow.13 / 106 切求的寡妇.13

===Original===
By Newton
Luke 18:1﻿–﻿7.
I
Our Lord, who knows full well
 

 The heart of every saint,
 

 Invites us, by a parable.
 

 To pray and never faint.
II
He bows his gracious ear,
 

 We never plead in vain;
 

 Yet we must wait till he appear,
 

 And pray, and pray again.
III
Though unbelief suggest,
 

 Why should we longer wait?
 

 He bids us never give him rest,
 

 But be importunate.
IV
’Twas thus a widow poor,
 

 Without support or friend,
 

 Beset the unjust judge’s door,
 

 And gain’d, at last, her end.
V
For her he little cared,
 

 As little for the laws;
 

 Nor god nor man did he regard,
 

 Yet he espoused her cause.
VI
She urged him day and night,
 

 Would no denial take;
 

 At length he said, “I’ll do her right,
 

 For my own quiet’s sake.”
VII
And shall not Jesus hear
 

 His chosen when they cry?
 

 Yes, though he may a while forbear,
 

 He’ll help them from on high.
VIII
His nature, truth, and love,
 

 Engage him on their side;
 

 When they are grieved, his bowels move,
 

 And can they be denied?
IX
Then let us earnest be,
 

 And never faint in prayer;
 

 He loves our importunity,
 

 And makes our cause his care.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
路加福音 18:1–7。
一
我们的主，他全然知道
每个圣徒的心，
借一个比喻邀请我们，
祷告而永不懈怠。
二
他垂下恩慈的耳，
我们从未恳求徒然；
但我们必等候直到他显现，
并祷告，再祷告。
三
虽然不信暗示，
为何还要再等候？
他吩咐我们决不容他安息，
而要切求不舍。
四
就这样一个穷寡妇，
无依靠无朋友，
缠着那不义法官的门，
最终达到了她的目的。
五
他对她少有关怀，
对法律也少有尊重；
他既不敬神也不顾人，
却站在她的事业一边。
六
她日夜催促他，
不接受拒绝；
最后他说：「我要为她伸冤，
为我自己的安宁。」
七
耶稣岂不垂听
他选民的呼求吗？
是的，他虽可能一时宽容延迟，
必从高处帮助他们。
八
他的性情、信实与爱，
都站在他们一边；
当他们忧伤，他的肺腑被激动，
他们岂会被拒绝？
九
那么让我们恳切，
在祷告中永不懈怠；
他爱我们的切求，
并以我们的事为念。

## 107 Zaccheus / 107 撒该

===Original===
By Newton
Luke 19:1﻿–﻿6.
I
Zaccheus climb’d the tree,
 

 And thought himself unknown;
 

 But how surprised was he
 

 When Jesus call’d him down!
 

 The Lord beheld him, though conceal’d,
 

 And by a word his power reveal’d.
II
Wonder and joy at once
 

 Were painted in his face;
 

 “Does he my name pronounce,
 

 And does he know my case?
 

 Will Jesus deign with me to dine?
 

 Lord, I, with all I have, are thine.”
III
Thus, where the gospel’s preach’d,
 

 And sinners come to hear,
 

 The hearts of some are reach’d
 

 Before they are aware:
 

 The word directly speaks to them,
 

 And seems to point them out by name.
IV
’Tis curiosity
 

 Oft brings them in the way,
 

 Only the man to see
 

 And hear what he can say;
 

 But how the sinner starts to find
 

 The preacher knows his inmost mind!
V
His long-forgotten faults
 

 Are brought again in view,
 

 And all his secrets thoughts
 

 Reveal’d in public too.
 

 Though compass’d with a crowd about,
 

 The searching word has found him out.
VI
While thus distressing pain
 

 And sorrow fill his heart,
 

 He hears a voice again,
 

 That bids his fears depart,
 

 Then like Zaccheus he is blest,
 

 And Jesus deigns to be his guest.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
路加福音 19:1–6。
一
撒该爬上树，
以为自己不被知道；
但当他被耶稣唤下时，
他是何等惊奇！
主看见他，虽然他隐藏，
借一句话启示了他的大能。
二
惊奇与喜乐一时
绘在他的脸上；
「他竟说出我的名，
他知道我的案情吗？
耶稣竟屈尊与我同席？
主啊，我和我一切所有的都是你的。」
三
这样，在传讲福音之处，
罪人们来听道，
有些人的心被摸着，
在他们未察觉之前：
这道直接对他们说话，
似按名把他们指出来。
四
是好奇心
常把他们带进这路，
只为见那个人
并听他能说什么；
但罪人何等惊起，
发现传道者知道他内心最深处！
五
他久被遗忘的过犯
再被摆在眼前，
他一切隐秘的思想
也在公众面前显露。
虽然被人群四围，
那鉴察的道已把他寻出。
六
当这样伤痛
与忧愁充满他的心，
他再听见一个声音，
吩咐他的恐惧离去，
那时他像撒该一样蒙福，
耶稣屈尊作他的客人。

## 108 The Believer’s Danger, Safety, and Duty / 108 信徒的危险、安全与本分

===Original===
By Newton
Luke 22:31﻿–﻿32.
I
“Simon beware,” the Saviour said﻿—
