 When the great water-floods prevail,
 

 Leave not my trembling heart to fail!
II
Friend of the friendless and the faint,
 

 Where should I lodge my deep complaint?
 

 Where but with thee, whose open door
 

 Invites the helpless and the poor?
III
Did ever mourner plead with thee,
 

 And thou refuse that mourner’s plea?
 

 Does not the word still fix’d remain
 

 That none shall seek thy face in vain?
IV
That were a grief I could not bear,
 

 Didst thou not hear and answer pray’r;
 

 But a pray’r-hearing, answ’ring God,
 

 Supports me under every load.
V
Fair is the lot that’s cast for me,
 

 I have an Advocate with thee:
 

 They whom the world caresses most
 

 Have no such privilege to boast.
VI
Poor though I am, despised, forgot,
 

 Yet God, my God, forgets me not;
 

 And he is safe and must succeed,
 

 For whom the Lord vouchsafes to plead.
===Chinese===
库珀 作
一
我生命之神啊，我向你呼求，
受苦中俯伏你脚前；
当大水泛滥之时，
求你不要任我战兢的心失败！
二
无友者与软弱者的朋友，
我深沉的苦情向谁倾诉？
除了向你，你敞开的门
正邀请无助者与贫穷人？
三
曾有哀恸的人向你恳求，
你却拒绝那哀恸者的恳求吗？
你的话岂不仍然坚立：
寻求你面的必不落空？
四
你若不垂听不应允祷告，
那是我承受不起的忧伤；
但一位垂听祷告、
应允祷告的神，扶持我担各重担。
五
我所得的份何等美好，
我在你那里有一位中保：
世上最受宠爱的人
也没有这样的特权可夸。
六
我虽贫穷、被藐视、被遗忘，
但神，我的神，不忘记我；
主屈尊为之申辩的人，
必蒙保守、必得成功。

## 20 The Valley of the Shadow of Death / 20 死荫幽谷

===Original===
By Cowper
I
My soul is sad and much dismay’d;
 

 See, Lord, what legions of my foes,
 

 With fierce Apollyon at their head,
 

 My heavenly pilgrimage oppose!
II
See from the ever-burning lake,
 

 How like a smoky cloud they rise!
 

 With horrid blasts my soul they shake,
 

 With storms of blasphemies and lies.
III
Their fiery arrows reach the mark,
 

 My throbbing heart with anguish tear:
 

 Each lights upon a kindred spark,
 

 And finds abundant fuel there.
IV
I hate the thought that wrongs the Lord;
 

 Ah! I would drive it from my breast,
 

 With thy own sharp two-edged sword,
 

 Far as the east is from the west.
V
Come then and chase the cruel host,
 

 Heal the deep wounds I have received!
 

 Nor let the pow’rs of darkness boast
 

 That I am foil’d and thou art grieved!
===Chinese===
库珀 作
一
我的灵魂忧伤、大大惊惶；
主啊，看哪，多少支仇敌的军旅，
由凶猛的亚玻伦率领，
拦阻我天路的旅程！
二
看哪，从那永远燃烧的火湖，
他们如烟云般涌起！
以可怖的狂风摇撼我的灵魂，
以亵渎和谎言的风暴。
三
他们的火箭射中目标，
撕扯我悸动的心：
每一支都落在同类的火星上，
在那里寻得充足的燃料。
四
我恨恶那亏负主的意念；
啊！我要以你锋利的两刃剑，
将它逐出胸怀，
远如东离西。
五
求你来驱逐这残忍的军旅，
医治我已受的重伤！
不要让黑暗的权势夸口
说我已被挫败、你已忧伤！

## 21 The Storm Is Hushed / 21 风暴平息

===Original===
By Newton
I
’Tis past﻿—the dreadful stormy night
 

 Is gone, with all its fears!
 

 And now I see returning light﻿—
 

 The Lord, my Sun, appears.
II
The tempter, who but lately said
 

 I soon should be his prey,
 

 Has heard my Saviour’s voice, and fled
 

 With shame and grief away.
III
Ah! Lord, since thou didst hide thy face.
 

 What has my soul endured?
 

 But now ’tis past, I feel thy grace,
 

 And all my wounds are cured!
IV
O wondrous change! but just before
 

 Despair beset me round;
 

 I heard the lion’s horrid roar,
 

 And trembled at the sound:
V
Before corruption, guilt, and fear,
 

 My comforts blasted fell;
 

 And unbelief discover’d near
 

 The dreadful depths of hell:
VI
But Jesus pitied my distress,
 

 He heard my feeble cry,
 

 Reveal’d his blood and righteousness,
 

 And brought salvation nigh.
VII
Beneath the banner of his love
 

 I now secure remain;
 

 The tempter frets, but dares not move
 

 To break my peace again.
VIII
Lord, since thou thus hast broke my bands,
 

 And set the captive free,
 

 I would devote my tongue, my hands,
 

 My heart, my all to thee.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
过去了——那可怕的暴风夜
连同它一切的惊恐已消散！
如今我看见归回的光——
主，我的太阳，显现。
二
那试探者，前不久还夸言
我快要做他的掠物，
如今听见我救主的声音，
便羞愧忧闷地逃遁。
三
啊！主啊，自从你掩面，
我的灵魂受了何等的苦！
但如今过去了，我感到你的恩典，
我一切的伤都痊愈了！
四
哦奇妙的变化！就在片刻前
绝望还四面围困我；
我听见狮子恐怖的吼叫，
因那声音战兢：
五
在腐败、罪咎和惧怕面前，
我的安慰尽都枯萎凋零；
不信又使我窥见近处
地狱可怕的深渊：
六
但耶稣怜悯我的困苦，
他垂听我微弱的呼求，
显明他的宝血和义，
将救恩带到近前。
七
在他爱的旗帜之下，
我如今安稳居住；
试探者恼怒，却不敢再动
来破坏我的平安。
八
主啊，你既这样断开我的锁链，
释放了被囚的，
我愿将我的口、我的手、
我的心、我的一切献给你。

## 22 Help in the Time of Need / 22 患难中随时的帮助

===Original===
By Newton
I
Unless the Lord had been my stay,
 

 (With trembling joy my soul may say,)
 

 My cruel foe had gain’d his end:
 

 But he appear’d for my relief,
 

 And Satan sees, with shame and grief,
 

 That I have an almighty Friend.
II
Oh! ’twas a dark and trying hour,
 

 When harass’d by the tempter’s power,
 

 I felt my strongest hopes decline!
 

 You only who have known his arts,
 

 You only who have felt his darts,
 

 Can pity such a case as mine.
III
Loud in my ears a charge he read,
 

 (My conscience witness’d all he said,)
 

 My long black list of outward sin:
 

 Then bringing forth my heart to view,
 

 Too well what’s hidden there he knew,
 

 He show’d me ten times worse within.
IV
’Tis all too true, my soul replied,
 

 But I remember Jesus died,
 

 And now he fills a throne of grace;
 

 I’ll go, as I have done before,
 

 His mercy I may still implore;
 

 I have his promise﻿—“Seek my face.”
V
But as when sudden fogs arise
 

 The trees and hills, the sun and skies,
 

 Are all at once conceal’d from view;
 

 So clouds of horror, black as night,
 

 By Satan raised, hid from my sight
 

 The throne of grace and promise too.
VI
Then, while beset with guilt and fear.
 

 He tried to urge me to despair,
 

 He tried and he almost prevail’d:
 

 But Jesus by a heavenly ray
 

 Drove clouds and guilt and fear away,
 

 And all the tempter’s malice fail’d.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
若不是主作我的扶持
（我的灵魂能以战兢的喜乐说，），
我残忍的仇敌早已如愿：
但他显现搭救我，
撒但便羞愧忧恼地看见，
我有一位全能的朋友。
二
哦！那是黑暗煎熬的时辰，
被试探者的权势缠磨，
我感到最强固的盼望都在消退！
惟有识透他诡计的人，
惟有感受过他毒箭的人，
才能怜悯我这样的处境。
三
他向我耳中高声宣读
（我的良心见证他所说的，）
我外在罪的长而黑的清单：
又将我的心陈列眼前，
他太熟悉其中隐藏的事，
指给我看内里还要坏十倍。
四
「全是真的，」我的灵魂回答，
但我记得耶稣死了，
如今他坐在施恩宝座上；
我要去，像从前一样，
仍可恳求他的怜悯；
我有他的应许——「寻求我的面。」
五
但正如骤起的浓雾
使树木山岭、太阳天空
霎时全从眼前隐没；
撒但掀起的恐怖黑云
也如此遮蔽我的眼目，
连施恩座与应许也不见。
六
那时，我被罪咎惧怕围困，
他竭力逼我绝望，
他极力尝试，几乎得逞：
但耶稣以一缕属天的光芒
驱散乌云、罪咎与惧怕，
试探者的恶谋尽归徒然。

## 23 Peace After a Storm / 23 风暴后的平安

===Original===
By Cowper
I
When darkness long has veil’d my mind.
 

 And smiling day once more appears,
 

 Then, my Redeemer, then I find
 

 The folly of my doubts and fears.
II
Straight I upbraid my wand’ring heart,
 

 And blush that I should ever be
 

 Thus prone to act so base a part,
 

 Or harbour one hard thought of thee.
III
Oh! let me then at length be taught
 

 What I am still so slow to learn:
 

 That God is love and changes not,
 

 Nor knows the shadow of a turn.
IV
Sweet truth, and easy to repeat!
 

 But when my faith is sharply tried,
 

 I find myself a learner yet,
 

 Unskilful, weak, and apt to slide.
V
But, my Lord, one look from thee
 

 Subdues the disobedient will;
 

 Drives doubt and discontent away,
 

 And thy rebellious worm is still.
VI
Thou art as ready to forgive,
 

 As I am ready to repine;
 

 Thou, therefore, all the praise receive;
 

 Be shame and self-abhorrence mine.
===Chinese===
库珀 作
一
当黑暗久久蒙蔽我心，
欢笑的白昼再度出现，
那时，我的救赎主，我便发现
我的疑惑与惧怕何等愚昧。
二
我随即责备我游荡的心，
并羞愧自己竟会
如此轻易演这卑劣的角色，
或对你怀一丝刚硬的念头。
三
哦！愿我终于学会
我一直迟慢不肯学的功课：
神就是爱，永不改变，
连转动的影儿也没有。
四
甘甜的真理，复述何等容易！
但当我的信心受严厉试炼，
我发现自己仍是初学的，
笨拙、软弱、易于滑跌。
五
但主啊，你的一瞥
便降服悖逆的意志；
驱散疑惑与不满，
你悖逆的虫便安静。
六
你乐于赦免，
正如我易于抱怨；
所以愿一切颂赞归于你；
愿羞愧和自恨归于我。

## 24 Mourning and Longing / 24 哀恸与渴慕

===Original===
By Cowper
I
The Saviour hides his face!
 

 My spirit thirsts to prove
 

 Renew’d supplies of pard’ning grace,
 

 And never-fading love.
II
The favour’d souls who know
 

 What glories shine in him
 

 Pant for his presence, as the roe
 

 Pants for the living stream.
III
What trifles tease me now!
 

 They swarm like summer flies,
 

 They cleave to every thing I do,
 

 And swim before my eyes.
IV
How dull the Sabbath-day,
 

 Without the Sabbath’s Lord!
 

 How toilsome then to sing and pray
 

 And wait upon the word?
V
Of all the truths I hear,
 

 How few delight my taste!
 

 I glean a berry here and there,
 

 But mourn the vintage past.
VI
Yet let me (as I ought)
 

 Still hope to be supplied;
 

 No pleasure else is worth a thought,
 

 Nor shall I be denied.
VII
Though I am but a worm,
 

 Unworthy of his care,
 

 The Lord will my desire perform,
 

 And grant me all my pray’r.
===Chinese===
库珀 作
一
救主掩面了！
我的灵渴慕经历
赦罪之恩更新的供给，
和永不凋残的爱。
二
那蒙恩认识
他里面荣耀的灵魂，
切慕他的同在，如鹿
切慕活水的溪流。
三
如今何等琐事烦扰我！
它们如夏日的蝇群，
缠附我手所作的每件事，
又漂浮在我眼前。
四
没有了安息日的主，
安息日何等沉闷！
歌唱祷告、
等候圣言，又是何等劳累！
五
在我所听的一切真理中，
能悦我味觉的何其少！
我东一处西一处拾取浆果，
却哀叹采摘的季节已过。
六
然而愿我（如所当行的）
仍盼望得着供给；
别的快乐不值得一想，
我也必不遭拒绝。
七
我虽只是一条虫，
不配他的眷顾，
主必成全我的心愿，
应允我一切的祷告。

## 25 Rejoice the Soul of Thy Servant / 25 求你使你仆人心里欢喜

===Original===
By Newton
I
When my pray’rs are a burden and task.
 

 No wonder I little receive;
 

 Lord, make me willing to ask,
 

 Since thou art so ready to give:
 

 Although I am bought with thy blood,
 

 And all thy salvation is mine,
 

 At a distance from thee, my chief good.
 

 I wander and languish and pine.
II
Of thy goodness of old, when I read,
 

 To those who were sinners like me,
 

 Why may I not wrestle and plead,
 

 With them a partaker to be?
 

 Thine arm is not shorten’d since then,
 

 And those who believe in thy name
 

 Ever find thou art Yea and Amen,
 

 Through all generations the same.
III
While my spirit within me is press’d
 

 With sorrow, temptation, and fear,
 

 Like John I would flee to thy breast,
 

 And pour my complaints in thine ear:
 

 How happy and favour’d was he
 

 Who could on thy bosom repose!
 

 Might this favour be granted to me,
 

 I’d smile at the rage of my foes.
IV
I have heard of thy wonderful name,
 

 How great and exalted thou art;
 

 But ah! I confess to my shame,
 

 It faintly impresses my heart:
 

 The beams of thy glory display,
 

 As Peter once saw thee appear;
 

 That, transported like him, I might say,
 

 “It is good for my soul to be here.”
V
What a sorrow and weight didst thou feel,
 

 When nail’d, for my sake, to the tree!
 

 My heart sure is harder than steel,
 

 To feel no more sorrow for thee:
 

 Oh! let me with Thomas descry
 

 The wounds in thy hands and thy side,
 

 And have feelings like his when I cry,
 

 “My God and my Saviour has died!”
VI
But if thou hast appointed me still
 

 To wrestle and suiter and fight;
 

 make me resign’d to thy will,
 

 For all thy appointments are right:
 

 This mercy, at least I entreat,
 

 That, knowing how vile I have been,
 

 I, with Mary, may wait at thy feet,
 

 And weep o’er the pardon of sin.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
当祷告成了重担与苦差，
难怪我得着的如此少；
主啊，你既这样乐于赐予，
求你使我甘愿祈求：
我虽用你的血买来，
你全部的救恩都属于我，
但远离你这至宝，
我便流离、衰残、憔悴。
二
当我读到古时你的良善，
如何临到像我一样的罪人，
我为何不能角力恳求，
与他们同作蒙恩的人？
你的膀臂自那时并未缩短，
凡信靠你名的人
总发现你是那「是的阿们」，
世世代代永不改变。
三
当我里面的灵被
忧伤、试探和惧怕压制，
我愿像约翰逃向你胸怀，
将苦情倾入你耳中：
那能靠你胸膛
安息的人何等有福蒙恩！
这恩若也赐给我，
我便笑对我的仇敌的愤怒。
四
我已听闻你奇妙的名，
你是何等伟大崇高；
但啊！我羞愧地承认，
它在我心上留下的印何等浅淡：
求你显出你荣耀的光辉，
像彼得当年见你显圣；
使我像他那样出神地说：
「我的灵魂在这里真好。」
五
你为我被钉在树上时，
感受了何等的忧伤与重负！
我的心必是比铁还硬，
竟不为你更多忧伤：
哦！愿我像多马那样
探看你手和你肋旁的伤痕，
带着他那样的感情呼喊：
「我的神、我的救主已经死了！」
六
但你若仍派定我
去角力、受苦、争战；
求你使我顺服你的旨意，
因你一切的安排都是好的：
至少求你赐下这恩典——
既知道我曾何等卑劣，
我便能与马利亚同候你脚前，
为罪的赦免而流泪。
## 26 Self-Acquaintance / 26 自我省察

===Original===
By Cowper
I
Dear Lord! accept a sinful heart,
 

 Which of itself complains,
 

 And mourns, with much and frequent smart,
 

 The evil it contains.
II
There fiery seeds of anger lurk,
 

 Which often hurt my frame;
 

 And wait but for the tempter’s work,
 

 To fan them to a flame.
III
Legality holds out a bribe
 

 To purchase life from thee;
 

 And discontent would fain prescribe
 

 How thou shalt deal with me.
IV
While unbelief withstands thy grace,
 

 And puts the mercy by,
 

 Presumption, with a brow of brass,
 

 Says, “Give me, or I die.”
V
How eager are my thoughts to roam
 

 In quest of what they love!
 

 But ah! when duty calls them home,
 

 How heavily they move!
VI
Oh, cleanse me in a Saviour’s blood,
 

 Transform me by thy power,
 

 And make me thy beloved abode,
 

 And let me rove no more!
===Chinese===
库珀 作
一
亲爱的主啊！求你悦纳一颗有罪的心，
它为自己哀叹，
并常带着频繁的伤痛，
为里面所含的邪恶忧伤。
二
那里潜伏着怒气的火种，
常常伤害我的身心；
只等试探者一动手，
便将它们扇成火焰。
三
律法主义递上贿赂，
要向你买生命；
不满则一心想规定
你该怎样待我。
四
不信拦阻你的恩典，
把怜悯推开一旁；
放肆则板着黄铜的脸，
说：「给我，不然我死。」
五
我的思绪何等急切地游荡，
追寻它们所爱的！
但啊！本分召它们回家时，
它们又何等迟重！
六
哦，用救主的血洁净我，
以你的大能改变我，
使我作你挚爱的居所，
叫我不再流荡！

## 27 Bitter and Sweet / 27 苦与甜

===Original===
By Newton
I
Kindle, Saviour, in my heart
 

 A flame of love divine;
 

 Hear, for mine I trust thou art,
 

 And sure I would be thine:
 

 If my soul has felt thy grace,
 

 If to me thy name is known;
 

 Why should trifles fill the place
 

 Due to thyself alone?
II
’Tis a strange mysterious life
 

 I live from day to day;
 

 Light and darkness, peace and strife,
 

 Bear an alternate sway:
 

 When I think the battle won,
 

 I have to fight it o’er again;
 

 When I say I’m overthrown,
 

 Relief I soon obtain.
III
Often at the mercy-seat,
 

 While calling on thy name;
 

 Swarms of evil thoughts I meet,
 

 Which fill my soul with shame.
 

 Agitated in my mind,
 

 Like a feather in the air,
 

 Can I thus a blessing find?
 

 My soul, can this be pray’r?
IV
But when Christ, my Lord and Friend,
 

 Is pleased to show his power,
 

 All at once my troubles end,
 

 And I’ve a golden hour:
 

 Then I see his smiling face,
 

 Feel the pledge of joys to come,
 

 Often, Lord, repeat this grace
 

 Till thou shalt call me home.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
救主啊，在我心里
点燃神圣爱火的焰；
垂听，我信你是我的，
我也定意属于你：
我的灵魂若尝过你的恩典，
你的名我若已经认识；
为何琐事竟占据了
惟独属于你的位置？
二
这是一种奇异奥秘的生命，
我天天如此度过；
光明与黑暗、平安与争斗，
轮流掌权作主：
当我想战役已获胜，
却不得不重新再战；
当我说我已被击倒，
不久却得着解救。
三
常在施恩座前，
正呼求你名的时候；
成群的恶念向我袭来，
使我的灵魂满心羞愧。
心思这样纷扰，
像空中的羽毛，
我这样能寻得祝福吗？
我的灵魂啊，这能算祷告吗？
四
但当基督——我的主、我的朋友——
乐意彰显他的大能，
我的患难霎时止息，
我便有一段黄金时光：
那时我见他笑脸，
感到将来喜乐的凭据，
主啊，愿你常常重赐这恩典，
直到你召我回家。

## 28 Prayer for Patience / 28 求忍耐的祷告

===Original===
By Cowper
I
Lord, who hast suffer’d all for me,
 

 My peace and pardon to procure,
 

 The lighter cross I bear for thee
 

 Help me with patience to endure.
II
The storm of loud repining hush,
 

 I would in humble silence mourn;
 

 Why should th unburnt though burning bush
 

 Be angry as the crackling thorn?
III
Man should not faint at thy rebuke,
 

 Like Joshua falling on his face,
 

 When the cursed thing that Achan took
 

 Brought Isr’el into just disgrace.
IV
Perhaps some golden wedge suppress’d,
 

 Some secret sin, offends my God;
 

 Perhaps that Babylonish vest,
 

 Self-righteousness, provokes the rod.
V
Ah! were I buffeted all day,
 

 Mock’d, crown’d with thorns, and spit upon,
 

 I yet should have no right to say,
 

 My great distress is mine alone.
VI
Let me not angrily declare
 

 No pain was ever sharp like mine;
 

 Nor murmur at the cross I bear,
 

 But rather weep, rememb’ring thine.
===Chinese===
库珀 作
一
主啊，你为我受了一切，
为要为我成就平安与赦免，
求你帮助我以忍耐
承受我为你背的较轻的十架。
二
平息大声抱怨的风暴吧，
我愿在谦卑的沉默中哀伤；
那燃烧却未烧毁的荆棘，
为何要像爆响的荆棘一样发怒？
三
人不该在你的责备下沮丧，
像约书亚俯伏在地，
当亚干取了当灭之物，
使以色列公然蒙羞。
四
或许有藏起的金子，
隐秘的罪，得罪了我的神；
或许那件巴比伦衣裳——
自义——招来了刑杖。
五
啊！我纵然整日被殴打、
被戏弄、被戴荆棘冠、被吐唾，
我仍无权说，
我这大苦惟我独有。
六
愿我不要忿忿宣称
没有人的疼痛像我的这样尖锐；
也不抱怨我所背的十架，
而要宁愿流泪，记念你的十架。

## 29 Submission / 29 顺服

===Original===
By Cowper
I
O Lord, my best desire fulfill,
 

 And help me to resign
 

 Life, health, and comfort to thy will,
 

 And make thy pleasure mine.
II
Why should I shrink at thy command,
 

 Whose love forbids my fear’s
 

 Or tremble at the gracious hand
 

 That wipes away my tears?
III
No, rather let me freely yield
 

 What most I prize to thee,
 

 Who never hast a good withheld,
 

 Nor wilt withhold from me.
IV
Thy favour all my journey through,
 

 Thou art engaged to grant;
 

 What else I want, or think I do,
 

 ’Tis better still to want.
V
Wisdom and mercy guide my way,
 

 Shall I resist them both?
 

 A poor blind creature of a day,
 

 And crush’d before the moth!
VI
But ah! my inward spirit cries,
 

 Still bind me to thy sway;
 

 Else the next cloud that veils the skies,
 

 Drives all these thoughts away.
===Chinese===
库珀 作
一
主啊，成全我最深的愿望，
帮助我把
生命、健康、安乐顺服你的旨意，
使你的喜悦成为我的。
二
我为何在你命令前退缩，
你的爱不容我惧怕？
或在那擦去我眼泪的
恩手面前战兢？
三
不，我宁愿甘心
把我最珍爱的献给你，
你从未留下一样好处不给，
也永不会从我扣留。
四
你已应许在我旅程全程
赐下你的恩眷；
别的我所缺的、或自以为缺的，
仍是缺着更好。
五
智慧和怜悯引导我的路，
我还要二者都抗拒吗？
我这穷乏、眼瞎、只活一日、
在虫子之前被压碎的受造物！
六
但啊！我里面的灵呼求，
仍要捆我于你的权下；
不然下一片遮天的乌云，
便把这些意念全驱散。

## 30 Why Should I Complain? / 30 我为何抱怨？

===Original===
By Newton
I
When my Saviour, my Shepherd, is near,
 

 How quickly my sorrows depart?
 

 New beauties around me appear,
 

 New spirits enliven my heart:
 

 His presence gives peace to my soul,
 

 And Satan assaults me in vain;
 

 While my Shepherd his power controls
 

 I think I no more shall complain.
II
But, alas! what a change do I find,
 

 When my Shepherd withdraws from my sight!
 

 My fears all return to my mind,
 

 My day is soon changed into night;
 

 Then Satan his efforts renews
 

 To vex and ensnare me again;
 

 All my pleasing enjoyments I lose,
 

 And can only lament and complain.
III
By these changes I often pass through
 

 I am taught my own weakness to know;
 

 I am taught what my Shepherd can do,
 

 And how much to his mercy I owe:
 

 It is he that supports me through all;
 

 When I faint he revives me again;
 

 He attends to my pray’r when I call,
 

 And bids me no longer complain.
IV
Wherefore then should I murmur and grieve,
 

 Since my Shepherd is always the same,
 

 And has promised he never will leave
 

 The soul that confides in his name?
 

 To relieve me from all that I fear,
 

 He was buffeted, tempted, and slain;
 

 And at length he will surely appear,
 

 Though he leaves me awhile to complain.
V
While I dwell in an enemy’s land,
 

 Can I hope to be always in peace?
 

 ’Tis enough that my Shepherd’s at hand,
 

 And that shortly this warfare will cease:
 

 For ere long he will bid me remove
 

 From this region of sorrow and pain,
 

 To abide in his presence above,
 

 And then I no more shall complain.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
当我的救主、我的牧者临近，
我的忧伤何等快消散？
新的美景在我四周显现，
新的活力苏醒我心：
他的同在给我灵魂平安，
撒但攻击我也徒然；
我的牧者既掌管他的大能，
我想我不再抱怨。
二
但唉！我发现何等的改变，
当我的牧者从我眼前退去！
惧怕都回到我心中，
白昼很快变成黑夜；
于是撒但重新努力，
再来搅扰、网罗我；
一切可喜的享受尽失，
我只有悲叹和抱怨。
三
藉着我常经历的这些变迁，
我学会认识自己的软弱；
我学会知道我的牧者能做什么，
并知道我亏欠他的怜悯何等多：
是他在一切中扶持我；
我昏厥时他再苏醒我；
我呼求时他垂听我的祷告，
吩咐我不要再抱怨。
四
那我为何还发怨言忧伤，
既然我的牧者永不改变，
又应许永不撇弃
那信靠他名的灵魂？
为救我脱离一切所惧怕的，
他被击打、受试探、被杀；
他终必再显现，
虽然他暂容我抱怨一时。
五
我既住在仇敌之地，
还能指望常有平安吗？
我的牧者就在身旁，
这争战不久必止息，已经足够：
因为他不久必吩咐我迁移，
离开这忧伤痛苦之地，
住在他上面的同在中，
那时我必不再抱怨。

## 31 Return, O Lord, How Long? / 31 耶和华啊，你几时转回？

===Original===
By Newton
I
Return to bless my waiting eyes,
 

 And cheer my mourning heart, Lord!
 

 Without thee all beneath the skies
 

 No real pleasure can afford.
II
When thy loved presence meets my sight
 

 It softens care and sweetens toil,
 

 The sun shines forth with double light,
 

 The whole creation wears a smile.
III
Upon thine arm of love I rest,
 

 Thy gracious voice forbids my fear;
 

 No storms disturb my peaceful breast,
 

 No foes assault when thou art near.
IV
But, ah! since thou hast been away,
 

 Nothing but trouble have I known;
 

 And Satan marks me for his prey,
 

 Because he sees me left alone.
V
My sun is hid, my comforts lost,
 

 My graces droop, my sins revive;
 

 Distress’d, dismay’d, and tempest-toss’d,
 

 My soul is only just alive!
VI
Lord, hear my cry, and come again!
 

 Put all mine enemies to shame;
 

 And let them see ’tis not in vain
 

 That I have trusted in thy name.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
求你转回，赐福我等候的眼，
安慰我哀恸的心，主啊！
没有你，天底下的一切
都给不出真实的快乐。
二
当你所爱的同在映入我眼，
重担便得柔化、劳苦得甘甜，
太阳加倍明亮地照耀，
全受造界都带着笑容。
三
我安息在你爱的膀臂上，
你恩惠的声音不容我惧怕；
没有风暴扰乱我平静的心怀，
你临近时没有仇敌来袭。
四
但啊！自从你离开，
我只经历了患难；
撒但把我标记为掠物，
因它见我被单独撇下。
五
我的太阳隐藏，安慰尽失，
恩赐枯萎，罪又复苏；
困苦、惊惶、被风浪颠簸，
我的灵魂仅存一息！
六
主啊，垂听我的呼求，再回来吧！
使我一切的仇敌蒙羞；
让他们看见我信靠你的名
并不是徒然。

## 32 Cast Down, but Not Destroyed / 32 打倒了，却不至死亡

===Original===
By Newton
I
Though sore beset with guilt and fear,
 

 I cannot, dare not, quite despair;
 

 If I must perish, would the Lord
 

 Have taught my heart to love his word:
 

 Would he have given me eyes to see
 

 My danger and my remedy;
 

 Reveal’d his name, and bid me pray,
 

 Had he resolved to say me nay?
II
No though cast down, I am not slain!
 

 I fall, but I shall rise again;
 

 The present, Satan, is thy hour,
 

 But Jesus shall control thy pow’r:
 

 His love will plead for my relief,
 

 He hears my groans, he sees my grief;
 

 Nor will he suffer thee to boast
 

 A soul that sought his help was lost,
III
’Tis true, I have unfaithful been,
 

 And grieved his Spirit by my sin;
 

 Yet still his mercy he’ll reveal,
 

 And all my wounds and follies heal:
 

 Abounding sin I must confess,
 

 But more abounding is his grace:
 

 He once vouchsafed for me to bleed,
 

 And now he lives my cause to plead.
IV
I’ll cast myself before his feet,
 

 I see him on his mercy-seat,
 

 (’Tis sprinkled with atoning blood);
 

 There sinners find access to God:
 

 Ye burden’d souls, approach with me,
 

 And make the Saviour’s name your plea:
 

 Jesus will pardon all who come,
 

 And strike your fierce accuser dumb.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
我虽被罪咎惧怕重重围困，
却不能、也不敢全然绝望；
我若必灭亡，主还会
教导我的心爱他的话吗？
还会赐我眼看见
我的危险与救法；
启示他的名，又吩咐我祷告，
若他已定意拒绝我吗？
二
不，我虽被打倒，却未丧命！
我跌倒，还必再起；
撒但，现在是你的时候，
但耶稣必辖制你的权势：
他的爱必为我申辩救助，
他听见我的呻吟，看见我的忧伤；
他必不容你夸口说
那寻求他帮助的灵魂灭亡了，
三
不错，我一直不忠，
以罪使他的圣灵担忧；
但他仍要显明他的怜悯，
医治我一切的伤与愚妄：
罪显多，我必须承认，
但他的恩典更显多：
他曾屈尊为我流血，
如今活着为我申辩。
四
我要俯伏在他脚前，
我见他坐在施恩座上，
（洒着赎罪的血）；
罪人在那里寻得亲近神之路：
负重的灵魂啊，与我同来，
以救主的名作你的恳求：
耶稣必赦免一切来的人，
使你凶猛的控告者哑口。

## 33 The Benighted Traveler / 33 夜行迷途的旅人

===Original===
By Newton
I
Forest beasts that live by prey,
 

 Seldom show themselves by day;
 

 But when day-light is withdrawn,
 

 Then they rove and roar till dawn.
II
Who can tell the trav’ler’s fears
 

 When their horrid yells he hears?
 

 Terror almost stops his breath,
 

 While each step he looks for death.
III
Thus, when Jesus is in view,
 

 Cheerful I my way pursue;
 

 Walking by my Saviour’s light
 

 Nothing can my soul affright.
IV
But when he forbears to shine,
 

 Soon the trav’ler’s case is mine;
 

 Lost, benighted, struck with dread,
 

 What a painful path I tread!
V
Then my soul with terror hears,
 

 Worse than lions, wolves, or bears,
 

 Roaring loud in every part,
 

 Through the forest of my heart.
VI
Wrath, impatience, envy, pride,
 

 Satan and his host beside,
 

 Press around me to devour;
 

 How can I escape their power?
VII
Gracious Lord, afford me light,
 

 Put these beasts of prey to flight;
 

 Let thy pow’r of love be shown;
 

 Save me, for I am thine own!
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
林中靠捕猎为生的野兽，
白日很少露面；
但当日光退去，
它们便游荡咆哮到天明。
二
谁能述说行路人的恐惧，
当他听见它们恐怖的嚎叫？
惊骇几乎使他窒息，
每一步都料想死亡临到。
三
同样，当耶稣在望，
我欢然赶路；
行在我救主的光中，
没有能使我灵魂惊惶的。
四
但当他暂时不照耀，
行路人的处境便成了我的；
迷失、陷入黑夜、大为惊恐，
我走的是何等痛苦的路！
五
那时我的灵魂惊恐地听见，
比狮子、狼或熊更甚的，
在我心的森林里，
四处大声咆哮。
六
忿怒、急躁、嫉妒、骄傲，
加上撒但和它的军旅，
四面围迫要吞吃我；
我怎能逃脱它们的权势？
七
恩慈的主啊，赐我光，
使这些猛兽逃遁；
愿你爱的大能彰显；
拯救我，因我是属你的！

## 34 The Prisoner / 34 囚徒

===Original===
By Newton
I
When the poor pris’ner, through a grate,
 

 Sees others walk at large,
 

 How does he mourn his lonely state,
 

 And long for a discharge!
II
Thus I, confined in unbelief,
 

 My loss of freedom mourn;
 

 And spend my hours in fruitless grief
 

 Until my Lord return.
III
The beam of day, which pierces through
 

 The gloom in which I dwell,
 

 Only discloses to my view
 

 The horrors of my cell.
IV
Ah! how my pensive spirit faints
 

 To think of former days!
 

 When I could triumph with the saints,
 

 And join their songs of praise!
V
But now my joys are all cut off,
 

 In prison I am cast;
 

 And Satan, with a cruel scoff,
 

 Says, “Where’s your God at last?”
VI
Dear Saviour, for thy mercy’s sake,
 

 My strong, my only plea,
 

 These gates and bars in pieces break,
 

 And set the pris’ner free!
VII
Surely my soul shall sing to thee,
 

 For liberty restored;
 

 And all thy saints admire to see
 

 The mercies of the Lord.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
当可怜的囚徒透过栅栏，
看见别人自由行走，
他何等为自己的孤寂哀叹，
渴望获释！
二
我也这样，被囚在不信中，
为失去自由哀叹；
在徒然的忧苦里消磨时日，
直到我主回来。
三
那穿透我居住之幽暗的
一线日光，
只是向我显明
我牢房的可怖。
四
啊！想到往日，
我忧伤的灵何等昏厥！
那时我能与圣徒夸胜，
同唱他们的赞美歌！
五
但如今我的喜乐尽都截断，
我被投在监里；
撒但残忍地讥诮说：
「你的神如今在哪里？」
六
亲爱的救主，为你怜悯的缘故——
我坚强、我惟一的恳求——
求你粉碎这些门闩栅栏，
释放那囚徒！
七
我的灵魂必向你歌唱，
为着恢复的自由；
你众圣徒也必惊叹
看见主的怜悯。

## 35 Perplexity Relieved / 35 困惑得解

===Original===
By Newton
I
Uncertain how the way to find
 

 Which to salvation led,
 

 I listen’d long, with anxious mind,
 

 To hear what others said.
II
When some of joys and comforts told,
 

 I fear’d that I was wrong;
 

 For I was stupid, dead, and cold﻿—
 

 Had neither joy nor song.
III
The Lord my lab’ring heart relieved,
 

 And made my burden light;
 

 Then for a moment I believed,
 

 Supposing all was right.
IV
Of fierce temptations others talk’d,
 

 Of anguish, and dismay﻿—
 

 Through what distresses they had walk’d
 

 Before they found the way.
V
Ah! then I thought my hopes were vain,
 

 For I had lived at ease;
 

 I wish’d for all my fears again,
 

 To make me more like these.
VI
I had my wish: the Lord disclosed
 

 The evils of my heart,
 

 And left my naked soul exposed
 

 To Satan’s fiery dart.
VII
Alas! “I now must give it up,”
 

 I cried in deep despair;
 

 How could I dream of drawing hope
 

 From what I cannot bear?
VIII
Again my Saviour brought me aid,
 

 And when he set me free,
 

 “Trust simply on my word,” he said,
 

 “And leave the rest to me.”
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
不知怎样寻得
那条通往救恩的路，
我焦灼地久听
别人怎样说。
二
有人述说喜乐安慰时，
我便害怕自己错了；
因为我麻木、死沉、冰冷——
既无喜乐也无歌唱。
三
主使我劳苦的心得舒解，
使我的担子轻省；
那一刻我信了，
以为一切都对了。
四
别人谈到猛烈的试探、
痛苦与惊惶——
他们在寻见正路之前
曾走过怎样的困苦。
五
啊！那时我想我的盼望是空的，
因我一直活得安逸；
我竟愿一切惧怕重来，
使我更像这些人。
六
我如愿了：主显明了
我心里的邪恶，
使我赤裸的灵魂暴露在
撒但的火箭之下。
七
唉！「我如今只能放弃了，」
我在深深的绝望中呼喊；
我怎能妄想从
自己受不了的事中汲出盼望？
八
我的救主又来救助，
当他释放我时，说：
「单纯信靠我的话，
其余的交托给我。」

## 36 Prayer Answered by Crosses / 36 蒙十字架应允的祷告

===Original===
By Newton
I
I ask’d the Lord, that I might grow
 

 In faith and love and every grace,
 

 Might more of his salvation know,
 

 And seek more earnestly his face.
II
’Twas he who taught me thus to pray,
 

 And he, I trust, has answer’d pray’r;
 

 But it has been in such a way
 

 As almost drove me to despair.
III
I hoped that in some favour’d hour
 

 At once he’d answer my request;
 

 And by his love’s constraining power
 

 Subdue my sins and give me rest.
IV
Instead of this, he made me feel
 

 The hidden evils of my heart;
 

 And let the angry pow’rs of hell
 

 Assault my soul in every part.
V
Yea more, with his own hand he seem’d
 

 Intent to aggravate my woe;
 

 Cross’d all the fair designs I schemed,
 

 Blasted my gourds, and laid me low.
VI
Lord, why is this, I trembling cried,
 

 Wilt them pursue thy worm to death?
 

 “ ’Tis in this way,” the Lord replied,
 

 “I answer pray’r for grace and faith.
VII
“These inward trials I employ,
 

 From self and pride to set thee free;
 

 And break thy schemes of earthly joy.
 

 That thou may’st seek thy all in me.”
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
我求主使我
在信心、爱心和各样恩典上长进，
更认识他的救恩，
更恳切寻求他的面。
二
是他教我这样祷告，
我也信他已应允祷告；
但应允的方式
几乎把我逼到绝望。
三
我盼望他在某个蒙恩的时刻
忽然应允我的恳求；
以他爱的激励大能
制伏我的罪，赐我安息。
四
岂知相反，他使我觉出
心中隐藏的恶；
又任凭地狱愤怒的权势
从四面攻击我的灵魂。
五
不但如此，他似乎亲手
决意加重我的苦楚；
挫败我谋算的一切美图，
枯萎我的蓖麻，使我低伏。
六
主啊，为何这样？我战兢呼喊，
你要追赶你的虫直到死地吗？
「正是以这方式，」主回答说，
「我应允求得恩典信心的祷告。
七
「我使用这些内心的试炼，
使你脱离自恃和骄傲；
破碎你属世快乐的图谋，
使你在我里面寻求你的一切。」」

## 37 I Will Trust and Not Be Afraid / 37 我要倚靠，并不惧怕

===Original===
By Newton
I
Begone, unbelief,
 

 My Saviour is near,
 

 And for my relief
 

 Will surely appear:
 

 By pray’r let me wrestle,
 

 And he will perform;
 

 With Christ in the vessel,
 

 I smile at the storm.
II
Though dark be my way,
 

 Since he is my guide,
 

 ’Tis mine to obey,
 

 ’Tis his to provide;
 

 Though cisterns be broken,
 

 And creatures all fail,
 

 The word he has spoken
 

 Will surely prevail.
III
His love in time past
 

 Forbids me to think
 

 He’ll leave me at last
 

 In trouble to sink;
 

 Each sweet Ebenezer
 

 I have in review
 

 Confirms his good pleasure
 

 To help me quite through.
IV
Determined to save,
 

 He watch’d o’er my path,
 

 When, Satan’s blind slave,
 

 I sported with death;
 

 And can he have taught me
 

 To trust in his name,
 

 And thus far have brought me,
 

 To put me to shame?
V
Why should I complain
 

 Of want or distress,
 

 Temptation or pain?
 

 He told me no less:
 

 The heirs of salvation,
 

 I know from his word,
 

 Through much tribulation
 

 Must follow their Lord.
VI
How bitter that cup,
 

 No heart can conceive,
 

 Which he drank quite up,
 

 That sinners might live!
 

 His way was much rougher
 

 And darker than mine;
 

 Did Jesus thus suffer,
 

 And shall I repine?
VII
Since all that I meet
 

 Shall work for my good,
 

 The bitter is sweet,
 

 The med’cine is food;
 

 Though painful at present,
 

 Twill cease before long,
 

 And then, O! how pleasant
 

 The conqueror’s song!
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
退去吧，不信，
我的救主临近，
他必显现
来解救我：
让我以祷告角力，
他必成就；
基督既在船上，
我笑对风暴。
二
我的路纵然幽暗，
他既是我的引路者，
我只管顺服，
供应是他的事；
池子纵然破裂，
受造之物尽都失效，
他所说出的言语
必永远坚立。
三
他以往的爱
不容我想
他终会撇下我
沉溺于患难；
我回顾每一个
甘甜的以便以谢，
都证实他乐意
助我完全经过。
四
他定意拯救，
曾看顾我的路途，
当我还是撒但盲目的奴仆，
在死亡边缘嬉戏；
他既已教导我
信靠他的名，
又一路带我到这里，
岂会再使我羞愧？
五
我为何抱怨
贫乏或困苦、
试探或疼痛？
他早告诉我必如此：
救恩的承受者，
我从他的话知道，
必须经过许多患难
才能跟从他们的主。
六
那杯何等苦，
没有心能测透，
他竟完全喝尽，
为使罪人得活！
他的路比我
更崎岖更幽暗；
耶稣既这样受苦，
我岂可埋怨？
七
既然我所遇的一切
都必互相效力使我得益，
苦变为甜，
良药成了食物；
现今虽疼痛，
不久必止息，
那时，哦！得胜者的歌
何等甘甜！

## 38 Questions to Unbelief / 38 对不信的诘问

===Original===
By Newton
I
If to Jesus for relief
 

 My soul has fled by pray’r,
 

 Why should I give way to grief,
 

 Or heart-consuming care?
 

 Are not all things in his hand?
 

 Has he not his promise pass’d?
 

 Will he then regardless stand,
 

 And let me sink at last?
II
While I know his providence
 

 Disposes each event,
 

 Shall I judge by feeble sense,
 

 And yield to discontent?
 

 If he worms and sparrows feed,
 

 Clothe the grass in rich array,
 

 Can he see a child in need,
 

 And turn his eye away?
III
When his name was quite unknown,
 

 And sin my life employ’d,
 

 Then he watch’d me as his own,
 

 Or I had been destroy’d!
 

 Now his mercy-seat I know.
 

 Now by grace am reconciled;
 

 Would he spare me while a foe,
 

 To leave me when a child?
IV
If he all my wants supplied
 

 When I disdain’d to pray;
 

 Now his Spirit is my guide,
 

 How can he say me nay?
 

 If he would not give me up
 

 When my soul against him fought.
 

 Will he disappoint the hope
 

 Which he himself has wrought?
V
If he shed his precious blood
 

 To bring me to his fold,
 

 Can I think that meaner good
 

 He ever will withhold?
 

 Satan, vain is thy device!
 

 Here my hope rests assured,
 

 In that great redemption price
 

 I see the whole secured.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
我的灵魂既已
藉祷告逃向耶稣求救，
我为何还要向忧伤
或噬心的忧虑屈服？
万事不都在他手中吗？
他岂不曾赐下应许？
他岂会漠然站立，
任凭我终至沉没？
二
我既知道他的护理
安排每一事件，
岂可凭微弱的感官判断，
向不满屈服？
他既喂养飞鸟和虫，
为青草披上华饰，
他岂会看见儿女有缺，
就转眼不顾？
三
他的名我还全然不识、
罪占据我一生时，
他尚且看顾我如己有，
不然我早已灭亡！
如今我认识他的施恩座，
靠恩典已与他和好；
他岂会仇敌时尚宽容我，
作了儿女反倒撇下？
四
他既在我
不屑祷告时供给我一切；
如今他的灵引导我，
岂会对我说不？
我的灵魂与他为敌时
他尚不肯放弃我，
他岂会使
他自己所生的盼望落空？
五
他既流下宝血
要领我归他的羊圈，
我岂能想较小的美物
他反会扣留？
撒但，你的诡计徒然！
我的盼望安然而立，
在那重价的救赎里
我看见万有都已稳妥。

## 39 Good Effects by Weak Means / 39 藉微弱途径成就的美善果效

===Original===
By Newton
I
Unbelief the soul dismays,
 

 What objections will it raise!
 

 But true faith securely leans
 

 On the promise, in the means.
II
If to faith it once be known,
 

 God has said, “It shall be done,
 

 And in this appointed way,”
 

 Faith has then no more to say.
III
Moses road, by faith uprear’d,
 

 Through the sea a path prepared;
 

 Jericho’s devoted wall
 

 At the trumpet’s sound must fall.
IV
With a pitcher and a lamp
 

 Gideon overthrew a camp:
 

 And a stone, well aim’d by faith,
 

 Proved the arm’d Philistine’s death.
V
Thus the Lord is pleased to try
 

 Those who on his help rely;
 

 By the means he makes it known
 

 That the power is all his own.
VI
Yet the means are not in vain,
 

 If the end we would obtain;
 

 Though the breath of pray’r be weak,
 

 None shall find but they who seek.
VII
God alone the heart can reach,
 

 Yet the ministers must preach:
 

 ’Tis their part the seed to sow,
 

 And ’tis his to make it grow.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
不信使灵魂惊惶，
它会提出多少异议！
但真信心安稳倚靠
应许，并藉着蒙恩之道。
二
信心若一旦知道
神已说：「这事必成，
且循此指定之途，」
信心便再无别话可说。
三
摩西的路，凭信举起，
在海中预备道路；
耶利哥当灭的城墙
在号角声中必然倒塌。
四
凭一个瓶和一盏灯，
基甸倾覆了敌营；
一块凭信瞄准的石头，
成了武装的非利士人之死。
五
主就这样乐意试炼
那些倚靠他帮助的人；
他藉着这些途径显明
大能全属于他自己。
六
然而若要得着目标，
这些途径并非徒然；
祷告的气息虽然微弱，
惟寻找的人才能寻见。
七
惟独神能触及人心，
但传道人仍须传讲：
撒种是他们的本分，
使之生长是他的。

## 40 Why Art Thou Cast Down? / 40 你为何忧闷？

===Original===
By Newton
I
Be still, my heart! these anxious cares
 

 To thee are burdens, thorns, and snares;
 

 They cast dishonour on thy Lord,
 

 And contradict his gracious word.
II
Brought safely by his hand thus far,
 

 Why wilt thou now give place to fear?
 

 How canst thou want if he provide,
 

 Or lose thy way with such a guide?
III
When first before his mercy-seat
 

 Thou didst to him thy all commit,
 

 He gave the warrant from that hour,
 

 To trust his wisdom, love, and power.
IV
Did ever trouble yet befall,
 

 And he refuse to hear thy call?
 

 And has he not his promise pass’d,
 

 That thou shalt overcome at last?
V
Like David thou may’st comfort draw,
 

 Saved from the bear’s and lion’s paw;
 

 Goliath’s rage I may defy,
 

 For God, my Saviour, still is nigh.
VI
He who has help’d me hitherto,
 

 Will help me all my journey through,
 

 And give me daily cause to raise
 

 New Ebenezers to his praise.
VII
Though rough and thorny be the road,
 

 It leads thee home apace to God;
 

 Then count thy present trials small,
 

 For heav’n will make amends for all.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
安静吧，我的心！这些忧虑
对你是重担、荆棘与网罗；
它们使你的主蒙羞，
并与他恩惠的话相违。
二
他的手既一路平安领你到此，
你如今为何还要容让惧怕？
他既供给，你怎会匮乏？
有这样的引路者，你怎会迷路？
三
当初你在他施恩座前
向他交托你的一切，
从那一刻他给了你凭据，
可信托他的智慧、慈爱和大能。
四
可曾有患难临到，
他竟拒绝垂听你的呼求？
他岂不曾赐下应许，
说你终必得胜？
五
你可以像大卫那样取安慰，
曾从熊掌狮爪中被救；
我可以无惧歌利亚的怒吼，
因为神，我的救主，仍在我旁。
六
那帮助我到今日的，
必助我走完全程，
并天天给我理由
竖起新的以便以谢赞美他。
七
道路虽然崎岖多刺，
它正引你快快归家到神那里；
那么看你现在的试炼为小吧，
天堂必补偿这一切。

## 41 The Way of Access / 41 亲近神之路

===Original===
By Newton
I
One glance of thine, eternal Lord,
 

 Pierces all nature through;
 

 Nor heaven nor earth nor hell afford
 

 A shelter from thy view!
II
The mighty whole, each smaller part,
 

 At once before thee lies;
 

 And every thought of every heart
 

 Is open to thine eyes.
III
Though greatly from myself conceal’d,
 

 Thou seest my inward frame;
 

 To thee I always stand reveal’d
 

 Exactly as I am.
IV
Since, therefore, I can hardly bear
 

 What in myself I see,
 

 How vile and black must I appear
 

 Most holy God, to thee!
V
But since my Saviour stands between,
 

 In garments dyed in blood,
 

 ’Tis he, instead of me, is seen,
 

 When I approach to God.
VI
Thus, though a sinner, I am safe;
 

 He pleads before the throne
 

 ’Tis life and death in my behalf,
 

 And calls my sins his own.
VII
What wondrous love, what mysteries,
 

 In this appointment shine!
 

 My breaches of the law are his,
 

 And his obedience mine.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
永恒的主啊，你一瞥眼，
便穿透整个自然；
天、地、阴间都无处
可藏身躲你的眼目！
二
浩大的整体、每一细微部分，
都同时陈列你面前；
每一颗心中的每个意念
都向你眼目敞开。
三
我虽大大向自己隐藏，
你却看见我内里的构造；
我在你面前总是赤露，
恰如我的本相。
四
既然我几乎无法忍受
在自己里面所见的，
最圣洁的神啊，我在你眼中
必显得何等污秽黑暗！
五
但我的救主既站在中间，
穿着染血的衣服，
当我亲近神时，
被看见的是他，而不是我。
六
这样，我虽是罪人，却得安稳；
他在宝座前申辩，
以他的生与死替我代求，
并称我的罪为他自己的。
七
何等奇妙的爱，何等奥秘，
在这安排中闪耀！
我违背律法之罪归他，
他的顺服归我。

## 42 The Pilgrim’s Song / 42 天路客之歌

===Original===
By Newton
I
From Egypt lately freed
 

 By the Redeemer’s grace,
 

 A rough and thorny path we tread,
 

 In hopes to see his face.
II
The flesh dislikes the way,
 

 But faith approves it well!
 

 This only leads to endless day,
 

 All others lead to hell.
III
The promised land of peace
 

 Faith keeps in constant view;
 

 How diff’rent from the wilderness
 

 We are now passing through!
IV
Here often from our eyes
 

 Clouds hide the light divine:
 

 There we shall have unclouded skies,
 

 Our sun will always shine.
V
Here griefs and cares and pains
 

 And fears distress us sore!
 

 But there eternal pleasure reigns,
 

 And we shall weep no more.
VI
Lord, pardon our complaints,
 

 We follow at thy call;
 

 The joy prepared for suff’ring saints
 

 Will make amends for all.29
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
蒙救赎主的恩典，
我们新近脱离埃及，
走在崎岖多刺的路上，
盼望见他一面。
二
肉体不喜悦这路，
信心却完全称许！
惟这条路通往无尽的白昼，
别的路都通向地狱。
三
那应许的平安之地，
信心时时放在眼前；
它与我们现在
穿行的旷野何等不同！
四
在这里，乌云常
遮蔽我们眼中的神圣之光：
在那里天空永无云翳，
我们的太阳永远照耀。
五
在这里忧伤、忧虑、疼痛
和惧怕苦害我们！
但在那里永恒的喜乐作王，
我们必不再哭泣。
六
主啊，赦免我们的抱怨，
我们随你呼召而行；
为受苦圣徒预备的喜乐
必补偿这一切。29

## IV Comfort / 四 安慰

## 43 Faith a New and Comprehensive Sense / 43 信心——一种全新而完备的感知

===Original===
By Newton
I
Sight, hearing, feeling, taste, and smell.
 

 Are gifts we highly prize;
 

 But faith does singly each excel,
 

 And all the five comprise.
II
More piercing than the eagle’s sight,
 

 It views the world unknown,
 

 Surveys the glorious realms of light,
 

 And Jesus on the throne.
III
It hears the mighty voice of God,
 

 And ponders what he saith.
 

 His word and works, his gifts and rod,
 

 Have each a voice to faith.
IV
It feels the touch of heav’nly power,
 

 And from that boundless source
 

 Derives fresh vigour every hour
 

 To run its daily course.
V
The truth and goodness of the Lord
 

 Are suited to its taste;
 

 Mean is the worldling’s pamper’d board,
 

 To faith’s perpetual feast.
VI
It smells the dear Redeemer’s name
 

 Like ointment poured forth;
 

 Faith only knows, or can proclaim,
 

 Its savour or its worth.
VII
Till saving faith possess the mind,
 

 In vain of sense we boast;
 

 We are but senseless, tasteless, blind,
 

 And deaf and dead and lost.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
视觉、听觉、触觉、味觉和嗅觉，
都是我们珍视的恩赐；
但信心单独超越每一种，
又总括全部五样。
二
比鹰的眼目更敏锐，
它眺望未知的世界，
览视荣耀的光明之境，
和宝座上的耶稣。
三
它听见神的大声音，
并思量他的话。
他的言与行、他的恩赐和刑杖，
对信心都各有一番声音。
四
它感受天来大能的一触，
并从那无限泉源
时时汲取新的力量，
奔走每日的行程。
五
主的真理与良善
正合它的口味；
世俗之徒奢华的宴席，
在信心永恒的筵席前何等寒微。
六
它嗅到亲爱的救赎主之名，
如倒出来的膏油；
惟有信心知道、并能传扬
它的芬芳与价值。
七
直到救人的信心占有心思，
我们夸感官都是徒然；
我们不过是麻木无味、
又瞎又聋又死又失丧的。

## 44 The Happy Change / 44 可喜的改变

===Original===
By Cowper
I
How blest thy creature is, God,
 

 When, with a single eye,
 

 He views the lustre of thy word﻿—
 

 The day-spring from on high!
II
Through all the storms that veil the skies,
 

 And frown on earthly things,
 

 The Sun of Righteousness he eyes,
 

 With healing on his wings.
III
Struck by that light, the human heart,
 

 A barren soil no more,
 

 Sends the sweet smell of grace abroad,
 

 Where serpents lurk’d before.
IV
The soul, a dreary province once
 

 Of Satan’s dark domain,
 

 Feels a new empire form’d within,
 

 And owns a heav’nly reign.
V
The glorious orb, whose golden beams
 

 The fruitful year control,
 

 Since first, obedient to thy word,
 

 He started from the goal,
VI
Has cheer’d the nations with the joys
 

 His orient rays impart:
 

 But, Jesus, ’tis thy light alone
 

 Can shine upon the heart.
===Chinese===
库珀 作
一
神啊，你的受造物何等有福，
当他以专一的眼，
看你话语的光辉——
那从高天而来的晨光！
二
透过那遮蔽天空、
对属地之事皱眉的一切风暴，
他仰望公义的太阳，
翅膀带着医治。
三
人心被那光击中，
不再是荒瘠之地，
在从前毒蛇潜伏之处，
散发恩典的芳香。
四
灵魂，从前不过是撒但
黑暗疆域里一片荒凉的行省，
如今感觉里面建立了新的国度，
承认一个属天的统治。
五
那荣耀的日球，它金色的光芒
掌管结果实的年岁，
自从它起初遵从你的话，
从起跑点出发，
六
它以东方之光所赐的喜乐
振奋了万国：
但耶稣，惟有你的光
能照亮人心。

## 45 Retirement / 45 退隐

===Original===
By Cowper
I
Far from the world, O Lord, I flee.
 

 From strife and tumult far!
 

 From scenes where Satan wages still
 

 His most successful war.
II
The calm retreat, the silent shade,
 

 With pray’r and praise agree;
 

 And seem by thy sweet bounty made
 

 For those who follow thee.
III
There, if thy Spirit touch the soul,
 

 And grace her mean abode,
 

 Oh with what peace and joy and love
 

 She communes with her God!
IV
There like the nightingale she pours
 

 Her solitary lays;
 

 Nor asks a witness of her song,
 

 Nor thirsts for human praise.
V
Author and Guardian of my life,
 

 Sweet source of light divine,
 

 And (all harmonious names in one)
 

 My Saviour, thou art mine!
VI
What thanks I owe thee, and what love,
 

 A boundless, endless store,
 

 Shall echo through the realms above
 

 When time shall be no more.
===Chinese===
库珀 作
一
主啊，我逃离世界，
远离争竞与喧嚣！
远离撒但至今
打得最得胜的战场。
二
那宁静的退隐处、无声的树荫，
与祷告赞美相合；
仿佛出于你甘甜的恩赐，
为跟从你的人而造。
三
在那里，你的灵若触及灵魂，
恩待她卑微的居所，
哦，她便以何等的平安、
喜乐与爱与她的神相交！
四
在那里她如夜莺倾吐
她独自的歌吟；
不为歌唱寻求见证人，
也不渴求人的称赞。
五
我生命的创造者与守护者，
神圣之光的甘甜泉源，
还有（一切和谐之名集于一身）
我的救主，你是我的！
六
我当向你献上何等的感谢、何等的爱——
那无边无尽的宝藏——
将在时间不再有之时
回响于诸天之上。

## 46 Jesus My All / 46 耶稣我的一切

===Original===
By Newton
I
Why should I fear the darkest hour,
 

 Or tremble at the tempter’s power!
 

 Jesus vouchsafes to be my tower.
II
Though hot the fight, why quit the field,
 

 Why must I either flee or yield,
 

 Since Jesus is my mighty shield?
III
When creature-comforts fade and die,
 

 Worldlings may weep, but why should I,
 

 Jesus still lives, and still is nigh?
IV
Though all the flocks and herds were dead,
 

 My soul a famine need not dread,
 

 For Jesus is my living bread.
V
I know not what may soon betide,
 

 Or how my wants shall be supplied;
 

 But Jesus knows, and will provide.
VI
Though sin would fill me with distress,
 

 The throne of grace I dare address,
 

 For Jesus is my righteousness.
VII
Though faint my pray rs, and cold my love,
 

 My steadfast hope shall not remove
 

 While Jesus intercedes above.
VIII
Against me earth and hell combine,
 

 But on my side is power divine;
 

 Jesus is all, and he is mine.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
我为何惧怕最黑暗的时辰，
或在那试探者的权势前战兢！
耶稣屈尊作我的保障。
二
争战虽激烈，为何退出战场，
我为何必须逃跑或降服，
既然耶稣是我大能的盾牌？
三
受造之物的安慰凋零消逝时，
世俗之人可以哭泣，我为何要哭？
耶稣仍活着，仍在身旁。
四
纵然牛羊尽都死亡，
我的灵魂也不必惧怕饥荒，
因为耶稣是我生命的粮。
五
我不知道不久将有何事，
也不知我的缺乏将如何补足；
但耶稣知道，他必预备。
六
罪虽要使我充满愁苦，
我仍敢就近施恩宝座，
因为耶稣是我的义。
七
我的祷告虽微弱，爱虽冷淡，
我坚定的盼望却必不动摇，
只要耶稣在上方代求。
八
地和地狱联合抵挡我，
但属神的大能站在我一边；
耶稣是一切，而他是我的。

## 47 The Hidden Life / 47 隐藏的生命

===Original===
By Cowper
I
To tell the Saviour all my wants,
 

 How pleasing is the task!
 

 Nor less to praise him when he grants
 

 Beyond what I can ask.
II
My lab’ring spirit vainly seeks
 

 To tell but half the joy;
 

 With how much tenderness he speaks,
 

 And helps me to reply.
III
Nor were it wise, nor should I choose,
 

 Such secrets to declare;
 

 Like precious wines, their taste they lose
 

 Exposed to open air.
IV
But this with boldness I proclaim,
 

 Nor care if thousands hear,
 

 Sweet is the ointment of his name,
 

 Not life is half so dear.
V
And can you frown, my former friends.
 

 Who knew what once I was;
 

 And blame the song that thus commends
 

 The Man who bore the cross?
VI
Trust me, I draw the likeness true,
 

 And not as fancy paints;
 

 Such honour may he give to you,
 

 For such have all his saints.
===Chinese===
库珀 作
一
向救主述说我一切所需，
这差事何等可悦！
而当他赐下超过
我所求的，赞美也不稍减。
二
我劳顿的心灵徒然想要
述说那喜乐的一半；
他说话何等温柔，
又帮助我回应。
三
那样倾诉这些隐秘
既不明智，我也不会选择；
它们像珍贵的酒，
暴露在空气中便失去滋味。
四
但这一件我放胆传扬，
也不在乎千万人听见：
他名的膏油何等甘甜，
生命也不及它一半宝贵。
五
而我从前的朋友们，
你们知道我从前怎样，
竟要皱眉，怪这首歌
称颂那背十架的人吗？
六
信我，我所画的像千真万确，
并非凭想象描摹；
愿他也赐你这样的尊荣，
因为他众圣徒都曾如此。

## 48 Joy and Peace in Believing / 48 因信而有的喜乐与平安

===Original===
By Cowper
I
Sometimes a light surprises
 

 The Christian while he sings;
 

 It is the Lord who rises
 

 With healing in his wings.
 

 When comforts are declining,
 

 He grants the soul again
 

 A season of clear shining,
 

 To cheer it after rain.
II
In holy contemplation,
 

 We sweetly then pursue
 

 The theme of God’s salvation,
 

 And find it ever new:
 

 Set free from present sorrow,
 

 We cheerfully can say,
 

 E’en let th’ unknown to-morrow
 

 Bring with it what it may.
III
It can bring with it nothing
 

 But he will bear us through;
 

 Who gives the lilies clothing
 

 Will clothe his people too:
 

 Beneath the spreading heavens
 

 No creature but is fed;
 

 And He who feeds the ravens
 

 Will give his children bread.
IV
Though vine nor fig-tree neither
 

 Their wonted fruit shall bear,
 

 Though all the field should wither,
 

 Nor flocks nor herds be there:
 

 Yet God the same abiding,
 

 His praise shall tune my voice;
 

 For, while in him confiding,
 

 I cannot but rejoice.
===Chinese===
库珀 作
一
有时一道光令
歌唱中的基督徒惊异；
那是主正升起，
翅膀带着医治。
当安慰渐渐消退，
他再赐灵魂
一段晴朗闪耀的时节，
在雨后鼓舞它。
二
在圣洁的沉思中，
我们甘甜地追想
神救恩的主题，
发觉它永远常新：
既已脱离眼前的忧愁，
我们便能欢然说：
就让未知的明天
带来它的一切吧。
三
它带来的无论什么，
他必带我们经过；
他既给百合花衣裳，
也必给他百姓穿上衣裳：
在广张的诸天之下，
受造之物无不得食；
那喂养乌鸦的
必给他儿女饼。
四
葡萄树、无花果树纵然
不结惯常的果子，
田野纵然都枯干，
羊圈牛棚都没有了：
神仍永不改变，
我要以赞美调准声音；
因为既信靠他，
我便不能不喜乐。

## 49 True Pleasure / 49 真正的喜乐

===Original===
By Cowper
I
Lord, my soul with pleasure springs
 

 When Jesus name I hear,
 

 And when God the Spirit brings
 

 The word of promise near:
 

 Beauties too, in holiness,
 

 Still delighted I perceive;
 

 Nor have words that can express
 

 The joys thy precepts give.
II
Clothed in sanctity and grace,
 

 How sweet it is to see
 

 Those who love thee, as they pass,
 

 Or when they wait on thee!
 

 Pleasant too, to sit and tell
 

 What we owe to love divine;
 

 Till our bosoms grateful swell
 

 And eyes begin to shine.
III
Those the comforts I possess,
 

 Which God shall still increase:
 

 All his ways are pleasantness,
 

 And all his paths are peace.
 

 Nothing Jesus did or spoke,
 

 Henceforth let me ever slight;
 

 For I love his easy yoke,
 

 And find his burden light.
===Chinese===
库珀 作
一
主啊，当我听见
耶稣的名，我的灵魂欢跃，
当神的圣灵把
应许的话带近：
在圣洁中我也不断
欣喜地看见美；
也没有言语能表达
你训词所赐的喜乐。
二
披上圣洁与恩典，
看见那些爱你的人走过、
或等候事奉你，
是何等甘甜！
坐下述说我们亏欠
神圣之爱的，也同样甘甜；
直到我们心中感恩满溢，
眼睛开始发亮。
三
这些是我所拥有的安慰，
神还要继续加增：
他的一切道路尽是可喜悦，
他的一切路径尽是平安。
耶稣所行所说的，
愿我从今再不轻看；
因为我爱他容易的轭，
并觉得他的担子轻省。

## 50 The Christian / 50 基督徒

===Original===
By Cowper
I
Honour and happiness unite
 

 To make the Christian’s name a praise;
 

 How fair the scene, how clear the light,
 

 That fill the remnant of his days!
II
A kingly character he bears,
 

 No change his priestly office knows;
 

 Unfading is the crown he wears,
 

 His joys can never reach a close.
III
Adorn’d with glory from on high,
 

 Salvation shines upon his face;
 

 His robe is of th’ethereal dye,
 

 His steps are dignity and grace.
IV
Inferior honours he disdains,
 

 Nor stoops to take applause from earth:
 

 The King of kings himself maintains
 

 Th expenses of his heavenly birth.
V
The noblest creature seen below,
 

 Ordain’d to fill a throne above;
 

 God gives him all he can bestow
 

 His kingdom of eternal love!
VI
My soul is ravish’d at the thought!
 

 Methinks from earth I see him rise!
 

 Angels congratulate his lot,
 

 And shout him welcome to the skies!
===Chinese===
库珀 作
一
尊荣与福乐联合，
使基督徒之名成为可称赞的；
那充满他余生的景色
何等美好、光何等明亮！
二
他带着君王的身份，
他祭司的职分没有更迭；
他戴的冠冕永不褪色，
他的喜乐永无终止。
三
以从高天而来的荣耀为饰，
救恩在他脸上发光；
他的袍子染着天上的颜色，
他的步履是尊贵与优雅。
四
他藐视次等的尊荣，
也不俯就领受地上的掌声：
万王之王亲自供养
他属天出生的开销。
五
他是地上所见最高贵的受造物，
被派定要坐上面的宝座；
神把他所能赐的一切都给他——
他永恒爱的国度！
六
我的灵魂一想到这便狂喜！
我仿佛见他离地升上高天！
众天使庆贺他的福分，
欢呼迎接他进入诸天！

## 51 Lively Hope and Gracious Fear / 51 活泼的盼望与恩典的敬畏

===Original===
By Cowper
I
I was a grov’ling creature once,
 

 And basely cleaved to earth;
 

 I wanted spirit to renounce
 

 The clod that gave me birth.
II
But God has breathed upon a worm,
 

 And sent me from above
 

 Wings, such as clothe an angel’s form,
 

 The wings of joy and love.
III
With these to Pisgah’s top I fly,
 

 And there delighted stand,
 

 To view, beneath a shining sky,
 

 The spacious promised land.
IV
The Lord of all the vast domain
 

 Has promised it to me;
 

 The length and breadth of all the plain,
 

 As far as faith can see.
V
How glorious is my privilege!
 

 To thee for help I call;
 

 I stand upon a mountain’s edge,
 

 Oh, save me, lest I fall!
VI
Though much exalted in the Lord,
 

 My strength is not my own;
 

 Then let me tremble at his word,
 

 And none shall cast me down.
===Chinese===
库珀 作
一
我原是一个爬行的受造物，
卑贱地贴着泥土；
我缺少志气弃绝
那生我的土块。
二
但神向一条虫吹气，
从上面赐给我
翅膀，如披在天使身上的，
那喜乐与爱的翅膀。
三
凭这翅膀我飞上毗斯迦山顶，
在那里欢然站立，
在闪耀的天空下眺望
那辽阔的应许之地。
四
那广大疆域的主
已把它应许给我；
整个平原的长与宽，
直到信心所能见的远方。
五
我的特权何等荣耀！
我向你呼求帮助；
我站在山崖边上，
哦，拯救我，免得我坠落！
六
我虽在主里大大升高，
我的力量却非自己所有；
那么愿我因他的话战兢，
就无人能将我推倒。

## 52 Confidence / 52 确信

===Original===
By Newton
I
Yes! since God himself has said it,
 

 On the promise I rely;
 

 His good word demands my credit,
 

 What can unbelief reply;
 

 He is strong, and can fulfill;
 

 He is truth, and therefore will.
II
As to all the doubts and questions
 

 Which my spirit often grieve,
 

 These are Satan’s sly suggestions,
 

 And I need no answer give.
 

 He would fain destroy my hope,
 

 But the promise bears it up.
III
Sure the Lord thus far has brought me
 

 By his watchful tender care;
 

 Sure ’tis he himself has taught me
 

 How to seek his face by pray’r:
 

 After so much mercy past,
 

 Will he give me up at last?
IV
True, I’ve been a foolish creature,
 

 And have sinn’d against his grace!
 

 But forgiveness is his nature,
 

 Though he justly hides his face;
 

 Ere he call’d me, well he knew
 

 What a heart like mine would do.
V
In my Saviour’s intercession
 

 Therefore I will still confide;
 

 Lord, accept my free confession,
 

 I have sinn’d but thou hast died:
 

 This is all I have to plead,
 

 This is all the plea I need.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
是的！神既亲自说了，
我就倚靠那应许；
他的善言配得我的信，
不信还能回答什么；
他有大能，能够成就；
他是真理，必定成就。
二
至于那些疑惑和诘问，
常使我灵忧伤，
这些是撒但狡猾的暗示，
我不必回答。
它巴不得毁掉我的盼望，
但应许把它托住。
三
主既以他警醒温柔的看顾
领我到今日；
既是他亲自教导我
藉祷告寻求他的面：
施了这么多怜悯之后，
他岂会至终放弃我？
四
不错，我一直是愚昧的受造物，
屡屡得罪他的恩典！
但赦免是他的性情，
纵然他公义地掩面；
他召我以先，早已知道
像我这样的心会怎样。
五
在我救主的代求里，
我仍要安然信靠；
主啊，悦纳我坦白的认罪，
我犯了罪，但你已死了：
这是我惟一可恳求的，
这也是我惟一需要的恳求。

## 53 Peace Restored / 53 平安复得

===Original===
By Newton
I
Oh! speak that gracious word again,
 

 And cheer my drooping heart;
 

 No voice but thine can soothe my pain,
 

 Or bid my fears depart.
II
And canst thou still vouchsafe to own
 

 A wretch so vile as I?
 

 And may I still approach thy throne,
 

 And Abba, Father, cry?
III
Oh then let saints and angels join,
 

 And help me to proclaim
 

 The grace that heal’d a breach like mine,
 

 And put my foes to shame!
IV
How oft did Satan’s cruel boast
 

 My troubled soul affright!
 

 He told me I was surely lost,
 

 And God had left me quite.
V
Guilt made me fear, lest all were true
 

 The lying tempter said;
 

 But now the Lord appears in view﻿—
 

 My enemy is fled.
VI
My Saviour, by his powerful word,
 

 Has turn’d my night to day;
 

 And his salvation’s joys restored
 

 Which I had sinn’d away.
VII
Dear Lord, I wonder and adore,
 

 Thy grace is all divine;
 

 Oh! keep me that I sin no more
 

 Against such love as thine!
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
哦！求你再说那句恩言，
鼓舞我消沉的心；
惟有你的声音能抚平我的痛，
或吩咐我的惧怕离开。
二
你还能屈尊承认
像我这样卑劣的可怜人吗？
我还能就近你的宝座，
呼叫阿爸、父吗？
三
哦，愿圣徒和天使联合，
帮助我传扬
那医好我这样破裂的恩典，
并使我的仇敌蒙羞！
四
撒但残忍的夸口
何等屡次惊吓我烦扰的灵魂！
它说我必定灭亡了，
神已完全离弃我。
五
罪咎使我怕那说谎的
试探者所讲的全是真的；
但如今主显现眼前——
我的仇敌逃跑了。
六
我的救主以他大能的话，
使我的黑夜转为白昼；
并恢复了救恩的喜乐，
那被我犯罪失去的。
七
亲爱的主，我惊叹敬拜，
你的恩典全然属天；
哦！保守我不再犯罪，
得罪这样的大爱！

## 54 Hear What He Has Done for My Soul! / 54 你们要听他为我灵魂所行的事！

===Original===
By Newton
I
Saved by blood, I live to tell
 

 What the love of Christ hath done;
 

 He redeem’d my soul from hell,
 

 Of a rebel made a son:
 

 Oh! I tremble still to think
 

 How secure I lived in sin;
 

 Sporting on destruction shrink,
 

 Yet preserved from falling in.
II
In his own appointed hour,
 

 To my heart the Saviour spoke;
 

 Touch’d me by his Spirit’s power,
 

 And my dang’rous slumber broke.
 

 Then I saw and own’d my guilt:
 

 Soon my gracious Lord replied,
 

 “Fear not, I my blood have spilt,
 

 ’Twas for such as thee I died.”
III
Shame and wonder, joy and love,
 

 All at once possess’d my heart;
 

 Can I hope thy grace to prove
 

 After acting such a part?
 

 “Thou hast greatly sinn’d,” he said,
 

 “But I freely all forgive;
 

 I myself thy debt have paid,
 

 Now I bid thee rise and live.”
IV
Come, my fellow-sinners, try,
 

 Jesus heart is full of love!
 

 Oh that you, as well as I,
 

 May his wondrous mercy prove!
 

 He has sent me to declare,
 

 All is ready, all is free:
 

 Why should any soul despair,
 

 When he saved a wretch like me?
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
蒙宝血拯救，我活着述说
基督的爱所成就的；
他把我的灵魂从地狱赎回，
使叛徒作了儿子：
哦！我一想仍战兢，
我在罪中活得何等自恃平安；
在灭亡的边缘嬉戏，
却蒙保守未曾坠落。
二
在他所定的时刻，
救主对我心说话；
以他圣灵的大能触摸我，
打破我危险的沉睡。
那时我看见并认了自己的罪：
恩慈的主随即回答说：
「不要怕，我已流了我的血，
正是为你这样的人我死了。」
三
羞愧与惊奇、喜乐与爱，
一时充满我心；
演了这样一个角色之后，
我还能盼望经历你的恩典吗？
「你犯了大罪，」他说，
「但我白白赦免一切；
我自己已付清你的债，
如今我吩咐你起来存活。」
四
来吧，与我同罪的罪人们，试试，
耶稣的心充满爱！
哦，愿你们像我一样，
经历他奇妙的怜悯！
他差我去宣告：
一切都预备了，一切都白白赐下：
既有他救了像我这样的可怜人，
为何还有灵魂绝望？

## 55 Freedom from Care / 55 脱离忧虑

===Original===
By Newton
I
While I lived without the Lord,
 

 (If I might be said to live,)
 

 Nothing could relief afford,
 

 Nothing satisfaction give.
II
Empty hopes and groundless fear
 

 Moved by turn my anxious mind;
 

 Like a feather in the air,
 

 Made the sport of every wind.
III
Now I see, whate’er betide,
 

 All is well, if Christ be mine;
 

 He has promised to provide,
 

 I have only to resign.
IV
When a sense of sin and thrall
 

 Forced me to the sinner’s Friend,
 

 He engaged to manage all,
 

 By the way, and to the end.
V
“Cast,” he said, “on me thy care,
 

 ’Tis enough that I am nigh;
 

 I will all thy burdens bear,
 

 I will all thy wants supply.
VI
“Simply follow as I lead,
 

 Do not reason, but believe;
 

 Call on me in time of need,
 

 Thou shalt surely help receive.”
VII
Lord, I would, I do submit,
 

 Gladly yield my all to thee;
 

 What thy wisdom sees most fit,
 

 Must be surely best for me.
VIII
Only, when the way is rough,
 

 And the coward flesh would start,
 

 Let thy promise and thy love
 

 Cheer and animate my heart.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
我还没有主的时候
（若那也算活着的话，），
没有任何事物能带来解救，
没有任何事物能给我满足。
二
虚空的盼望和无根据的惧怕
轮流搅动我焦灼的心；
像空中的羽毛，
被每一阵风戏弄。
三
如今我看见，无论何事临到，
只要基督是我的，一切都好；
他已应许供给，
我只需顺服交托。
四
当罪与捆绑的意识
迫我投奔罪人之友，
他承诺一路
管理一切，直到末了。
五
「把你的忧虑，」他说，「卸给我，
有我在近处就够了；
我必背负你一切的重担，
我必补足你一切的缺乏。
六
「只管单纯跟从我引领，
不要穷究推理，只要信；
在需要之时呼求我，
你必确实得着帮助。」
七
主啊，我愿意、也实在顺服，
甘心把我一切献给你；
你的智慧看为最合宜的，
对我必是最好。
八
只是，当道路崎岖、
怯懦的肉体想要却步时，
愿你的应许和你的爱
鼓舞激励我心。

## 56 Humiliation and Praise / 56 谦卑与赞美

===Original===
By Newton
I
When the wounded spirit hears
 

 The voice of Jesus blood,
 

 How the message stops the tears
 

 Which else in vain had flow’d!
 

 Pardon, grace, and peace proclaim’d,
 

 And the sinner call’d a child;
 

 Then the stubborn heart is tamed,
 

 Renewed and reconciled.
II
Oh! ’twas grace indeed to spare
 

 And save a wretch like me!
 

 Men or angels could not bear
 

 What I have offer’d thee!
 

 Were thy bolts at their command,
 

 Hell ere now had been my place;
 

 Thou alone couldst silent stand
 

 And wait to show thy grace.
III
If, in one created mind,
 

 The tenderness and love
 

 Of thy saints on earth were join’d
 

 With all the hosts above;
 

 Still that love were weak and poor,
 

 If compared, my Lord, with thine;
 

 Far too scanty to endure
 

 A heart so vile as mine.
IV
Wondrous mercy I have found,
 

 But, ah, how faint my praise!
 

 Must I be a cumber-ground,
 

 Unfruitful all my days?
 

 Do I in thy garden grow,
 

 Yet produce thee only leaves?
 

 Lord, forbid it should be so!
 

 The thought my spirit grieves.
V
Heavy charges Satan brings
 

 To fill me with distress;
 

 Let me hide beneath thy wings,
 

 And plead thy righteousness:
 

 Lord, to thee for help I call;
 

 ’Tis thy promise bids me come;
 

 Tell him thou hast paid for all,
 

 And that shall strike him dumb.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
受伤的灵听见
耶稣宝血的声音，
那信息怎样止住
本要徒然流淌的眼泪！
赦免、恩典与平安宣告，
罪人被称为儿女；
那时顽固的心被驯服，
得更新、得和好。
二
哦！存留并拯救
我这样的可怜人，实在是恩典！
人或天使都受不了
我所献上给你的这一切！
若雷霆由他们支配，
地狱早成了我的住处；
惟独你能静默站立，
等候显明你的恩典。
三
若把地上你众圣徒的
温柔与爱，
和天上众军旅的
都聚在一个受造的心思里；
那爱比起你的，主啊，
仍属软弱贫乏；
远不足以承受
像我这样卑劣的心。
四
我已寻得奇妙的怜悯，
但啊，我的赞美何等微弱！
难道我是一块白占地土，
终生不结果实？
难道我在你园中生长，
却只给你叶子？
主啊，断不容这样！
这意念使我的灵忧伤。
五
撒但提出沉重的控状，
要使我充满愁苦；
愿我藏在你翅膀之下，
以你的义为恳求：
主啊，我向你呼求帮助；
是你的应许召我来；
告诉它你已付清一切，
那必使它哑口无言。

## 57 For the Poor / 57 为穷人

===Original===
By Cowper
I
When Hagar found the bottle spent,
 

 And wept o’er Ishmael,
 

 A message from the Lord was sent
 

 To guide her to a well.
II
Should not Elijah’s cake and cruse
 

 Convince us at this day,
 

 A gracious God will not refuse
 

 Provisions by the way?
III
His saints and servants shall be fed,
 

 The promise is secure:
 

 “Bread shall be given them,” as he said,
 

 “Their water shall be sure.”
IV
Repasts far richer they shall prove,
 

 Than all earth’s dainties are;
 

 ’Tis sweet to taste a Saviour’s love,
 

 Though in the meanest fare.
V
To Jesus then your trouble bring,
 

 Nor murmur at your lot;
 

 While you are poor, and he is King,
 

 You shall not be forgot.
===Chinese===
库珀 作
一
夏甲发现皮袋的水用尽了，
伏在以实玛利身上哭，
有从主来的信息
指引她到一口井。
二
以利亚的饼和瓶岂不
今日也能使我们确信，
恩慈的神不会拒绝
一路的供给？
三
他的圣徒和仆人必得食物，
这应许是可靠的：
「饼必赐给他们，」如他所说，
「他们的水必不断绝。」
四
他们必经历远比
地上一切珍馐更丰盛的筵席；
尝到救主的爱何等甘甜，
纵然吃的是最粗淡的饭食。
五
那么把你的难处带到耶稣那里，
也不要抱怨你的命运；
你们虽然贫穷，他却是王，
你们必不被忘记。

## 58 Home in View / 58 天家在望

===Original===
By Newton
I
As when the weary trav’ler gains
 

 The height of some o’erlooking hill,
 

 His heart revives if, cross the plains,
 

 He eyes his home, though distant still.
II
While he surveys the much-loved spot,
 

 He slights the space that lies between;
 

 His past fatigues are now forgot,
 

 Because his journey’s end is seen.
III
Thus when the Christian pilgrim views
 

 By faith his mansion in the skies,
 

 The sight his fainting strength renews,
 

 And wings his speed to reach the prize.
IV
The thought of home his spirit cheers,
 

 No more he grieves for troubles past,
 

 Nor any future trial fears,
 

 So he may safe arrive at last.
V
’Tis there, he says, I am to dwell
 

 With Jesus in the realms of day;
 

 Then I shall bid my cares farewell,
 

 And he shall wipe my tears away.
VI
Jesus, on thee our hope depends,
 

 To lead us on to thine abode:
 

 Assured our home will make amends
 

 For all our toil while on the road.30
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
正如疲惫的行路人
登上某座俯瞰的山冈，
若越过平原望见家——虽仍遥远——
他的心便重新振作。
二
当他凝望那挚爱的地方，
便轻看中间相隔的路程；
已忘却过去的疲惫，
因为旅程的终点已在望。
三
同样，当基督徒天路客
凭信望见他在天上的宅邸，
这景象使他昏厥之力更新，
如添翼加速去得奖赏。
四
家的意念鼓舞他的灵，
他不再为过去的患难忧伤，
也不惧怕将来的试炼，
只要他能最终平安抵达。
五
「就是在那里，」他说，「我要
与耶稣同住在光明之境；
那时我要向忧虑道别，
他要擦去我的眼泪。」
六
耶稣，我们的盼望靠你，
求你领我们到你居所：
我们确信天家必补偿
我们一路的劳苦。30

## V Dedication and Surrender / 五 奉献与降服

## 59 Old Things Are Passed Away / 59 旧事已过

===Original===
By Newton
I
Let worldly minds the world pursue
 

 It has no charms for me;
 

 Once I admired its trifles too,
 

 But grace has set me free.
II
Its pleasures now no longer please,
 

 No more content afford;
 

 Far from my heart be joys like these,
 

 Now I have seen the Lord.
III
As by the light of op’ning day
 

 The stars are all conceal’d;
 

 So earthly pleasures fade away
 

 When Jesus is reveal’d.
IV
Creatures no more divide my choice,
 

 I bid them all depart;
 

 His name and love and gracious voice
 

 Have fix’d my roving heart.
V
Now, Lord, I would be thine alone,
 

 And wholly live to thee;
 

 But may I hope that thou wilt own
 

 A worthless worm like me?
VI
Yes! though of sinners I’m the worst,
 

 I cannot doubt thy will;
 

 For if thou hadst not loved me first,
 

 I had refused thee still.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
让世俗之心追逐世界吧，
它对我已无魅力；
我曾一度欣赏它的琐屑，
但恩典释放了我。
二
它的欢乐如今不再使我喜悦，
不再给我满足；
愿这样的快乐远离我心，
如今我已见了主。
三
正如破晓的光
把众星全都隐没；
耶稣一显明，
属世的欢乐便消褪。
四
受造之物不再分夺我的选择，
我吩咐它们全都退去；
他的名、他的爱和恩惠的声音
已固定我游荡的心。
五
主啊，如今我愿单单属你，
完全为你而活；
但我可盼望你肯承认
我这无用的虫？
六
可以！我虽是罪人中最坏的，
也不能怀疑你的旨意；
因为你若不曾先爱我，
我必仍拒绝你。

## 60 The Power of Grace / 60 恩典的大能

===Original===
By Newton
I
Happy the birth where grace presides,
 

 To form the future life!
 

 In wisdom’s path the soul she guides,
 

 Remote from noise and strife.
II
Since I have known the Saviour’s name,
 

 And what for me he bore;
 

 No more I toil for empty fame,
 

 I thirst for gold no more.
III
Placed by his hand in this retreat,
 

 I make his love my theme,
 

 And see that all the world calls great
 

 Is but a waking dream.
IV
Since he has rank’d my worthless name
 

 Amongst his favour’d few,
 

 Let the mad world, who scoff at them,
 

 Revile and hate me too.
V
O thou whose voice the dead can raise,
 

 And soften hearts of stone,
 

 And teach the dumb to sing thy praise,
 

 This work is all thine own!
VI
Thy wond’ring saints rejoice to see
 

 A wretch like me restored,
 

 And point and say﻿—“How changed is he
 

 Who once defied the Lord!”
VII
Grace bid me live, and taught my tongue
 

 To aim at notes divine;
 

 And grace accepts my feeble song﻿—
 

 The glory, Lord, be thine!
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
恩典掌管的出生何等有福，
她塑造未来的一生！
她领灵魂走智慧之路，
远离喧嚣与争竞。
二
自从我认识救主的名，
并知他为我承受的一切；
我不再为虚名劳碌，
也不再渴慕黄金。
三
他亲手把我安置在这隐退处，
我以他的爱为题，
并看见世人所谓伟大的一切
不过是一场醒着的梦。
四
他既把我无用的名
列在他蒙恩的少数人中，
任凭狂妄的世界——它既讥笑他们——
也辱骂恨恶我吧。
五
哦，你的声音能叫死人复活，
能软化石头之心，
能教哑巴歌唱赞美你，
这工作全是你的！
六
你惊奇的圣徒们喜见
我这样的可怜人得恢复，
指着我惊叹——「他的改变何等大，
那曾公然藐视主的人！」
七
恩典吩咐我活，又教导我的口
以求发出神圣的音符；
恩典悦纳我微弱的歌——
主啊，愿荣耀归于你！

## 61 My Soul Thirsteth for God / 61 我的心渴想神

===Original===
By Cowper
I
I thirst, but not as once I did,
 

 The vain delights of earth to share;
 

 Thy wounds, Emmanuel, all forbid
 

 That I should seek my pleasures there.
II
It was the sight of thy dear cross
 

 First wean’d my soul from earthly things,
 

 And taught me to esteem as dross
 

 The mirth of fools and pomp of kings.
III
I want that grace that springs from thee,
 

 That quickens all things where it flows,
 

 And makes a wretched thorn like me
 

 Bloom as the myrtle or the rose.
IV
Dear fountain of delight unknown!
 

 No longer sink below the brim;
 

 But overflow, and pour me down
 

 A living and life-giving stream!
V
For sure, of all the plants that share
 

 The notice of thy Father’s eye,
 

 None proves less grateful to his care,
 

 Or yields him meaner fruit than I.
===Chinese===
库珀 作
一
我渴，但不像从前那样，
去分享属地虚空的欢乐；
你的伤痕，以马内利，全都禁止
我在那里寻求快乐。
二
正是望见你亲爱的十架，
初次使我的灵魂离弃世事，
并教我看君王的排场、
愚人的欢笑如粪土。
三
我渴望那从你而出的恩典，
它流到哪里就苏醒万物，
并使我这样可怜的荆棘
如番石榴或玫瑰开放。
四
亲爱的、无人识得的喜乐泉！
不要再沉到泉缘以下；
要满溢，向我倾注
一条活而赐生命的溪流！
五
因为确实，在一切分享
你父之眼眷顾的植物中，
没有一棵比我对他的眷顾
更不知感恩，结的果子更劣。

## 62 Love Constraining to Obedience / 62 爱激励人顺服

===Original===
By Cowper
I
No strength of nature can suffice
 

 To serve the Lord aright;
 

 And what she has she misapplies,
 

 For want of clearer light.
II
How long beneath the law I lay
 

 In bondage and distress!
 

 I toil’d the precept to obey,
 

 But toil’d without success.
III
Then to abstain from outward sin
 

 Was more than I could do:
 

 Now, if I feel its power within,
 

 I feel I hate it too.
IV
Then all my servile works were done
 

 A righteousness to raise;
 

 Now freely chosen in the Son,
 

 I freely choose his ways.
V
What shall I do, was then the word.
 

 That I may worthier grow?
 

 What shall I render to the Lord?
 

 Is my inquiry now.
VI
To see the law by Christ fulfill’d,
 

 And hear his pard’ning voice,
 

 Changes a slave into a child,
 

 And duty into choice.
===Chinese===
库珀 作
一
天然的力量不足以
正确地事奉主；
她所有的也都被误用，
只因缺少更亮的光。
二
我在律法下躺卧多久，
受捆绑、受痛苦！
我劳苦要遵守诫命，
却劳苦而无成效。
三
那时仅要禁戒外面的罪，
已超过我所能；
如今我若感到罪的权势在内，
也感到我恨恶它。
四
那时我一切的奴仆之工
都是为立自己的义；
如今在爱子里白白领受，
我也甘愿选择他的道路。
五
「我当怎样行，」是那时的问话，
「才配得更深？」
「我当怎样报答主？」
是我如今的求问。
六
看见律法被基督成全，
并听见他赦免的声音，
便把奴仆变为儿女，
把本分变为选择。

## 63 The Heart Healed and Changed by Mercy / 63 心因怜悯得医治与更新

===Original===
By Cowper
I
Sin enslaved me many years,
 

 And led me bound and blind,
 

 Till at length a thousand fears
 

 Came swarming o’er my mind.
 

 Where, said I, in deep distress,
 

 Will these sinful pleasures end?
 

 How shall I secure my peace,
 

 And make the Lord my friend?
II
Friends and ministers said much,
 

 The gospel to enforce;
 

 But my blindness still was such
 

 I chose a legal course:
 

 Much I fasted, watch’d, and strove,
 

 Scarce would show my face abroad,
 

 Fear’d almost to speak or move,
 

 A stranger still to God.
III
Thus afraid to trust his grace,
 

 Long time did I rebel,
 

 Till, despairing of my case,
 

 Down at his feet I fell:
 

 Then my stubborn heart he broke,
 

 And subdued me to his sway,
 

 By a simple word he spoke,
 

 “Thy sins are done away.”
===Chinese===
库珀 作
一
罪奴役了我许多年，
领我盲目又被捆绑，
直到终于千百种惧怕
蜂拥爬上我的心头。
我在深苦中说，
这些有罪的欢乐何处是尽头？
我怎能保住平安，
使主成为我的朋友？
二
朋友们和传道人说了许多，
要劝服我信福音；
但我的盲目仍那样深，
竟选择了一条律法之路：
我多多禁食、警醒、较力，
几乎不敢在外面露面，
惧怕得几乎不敢说话行动，
对神仍是陌生。
三
这样惧怕信靠他的恩典，
我悖逆了许久，
直到对自己的境况绝望，
俯伏在他脚前：
那时他破碎我顽固的心，
制伏我归他掌管，
只藉他说的一句简简单单的话：
「你的罪已经除去。」

## 64 Hatred of Sin / 64 恨恶罪恶

===Original===
By Cowper
I
Holy Lord God! I love thy truth,
 

 Nor dare thy least commandments slight;
 

 Yet pierced by sin, the serpent’s tooth,
 

 I mourn the anguish of the bite.
II
But though the poison lurks within,
 

 Hope bids me still with patience wait;
 

 Till death shall set me free from sin,
 

 Free from the only thing I hate.
III
Had I a throne above the rest,
 

 Where angels and archangels dwell,
 

 One sin, unslain, within my breast,
 

 Would make that heav’n as dark as hell.
IV
The pris’ner, sent to breathe fresh air,
 

 And bless’d with liberty again,
 

 Would mourn were he condemn’d to wear
 

 One link of all his former chain.
V
But oh! no foe invades the bliss
 

 When glory crowns the Christian’s head;
 

 One view of Jesus as he is
 

 Will strike all sin for ever dead.
===Chinese===
库珀 作
一
圣洁的主神！我爱你的真理，
也不敢轻看你最小的诫命；
却被罪——那蛇的毒牙——刺透，
我为咬伤的痛苦哀恸。
二
但毒虽潜伏体内，
盼望仍叫我存忍耐等候；
直到死使我脱离罪，
脱离这惟一我所恨的东西。
三
我纵有高出众人的宝座，
在天使和天使长居住之处，
一件未除的罪在我胸中，
也会使那天堂黑如地狱。
四
被放出去呼吸新鲜空气、
重新蒙福得自由的囚徒，
若被判要戴上
旧锁链中的一环，也要哀恸。
五
但哦！当荣耀加冕基督徒的头，
没有仇敌能侵扰那福乐；
一见耶稣的本相，
就要把一切的罪永远击死。

## 65 The Child / 65 孩子

===Original===
By Newton
I
Quiet, Lord, my froward heart,
 

 Make me teachable and mild,
 

 Upright, simple, free from art,
 

 Make me as a weaned child:
 

 From distrust and envy free,
 

 Pleased with all that pleases thee.
II
What thou shalt to-day provide,
 

 Let me as a child receive;
 

 What to-morrow may betide,
 

 Calmly to thy wisdom leave:
 

 ’Tis enough that thou wilt care,
 

 Why should I the burden bear?
III
As a little child relies
 

 On a care beyond his own;
 

 Knows he’s neither strong nor wise,
 

 Fears to stir a step alone:
 

 Let me thus with thee abide,
 

 As my Father, Guard, and Guide,
IV
Thus preserved from Satan’s wiles,
 

 Safe from dangers, free from fears,
 

 May I live upon thy smiles,
 

 Till the promised hour appears,
 

 When the sons of God shall prove
 

 All their Father’s boundless love.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
主啊，使我任性顽梗的心安静，
使我受教、温良，
正直、单纯、不施巧诈，
使我像断了奶的孩子：
脱离不信与嫉妒，
以一切讨你喜悦的为喜悦。
二
你今天所要预备的，
愿我像孩子一样领受；
明天或有何事，
都安然交托你的智慧：
有你眷顾已经足够，
我何必背负重担？
三
正如小孩子倚靠
超出自己能力的看护；
知道自己不强壮也不聪明，
害怕独自行一步：
愿我这样与你同住，
以你为我的父、我的保障、我的引导。
四
这样蒙保守脱离撒但的诡计，
远离危险、没有惧怕，
愿我靠你的笑容而活，
直到所应许的时刻显现，
那时神的众子要经历
他们父浩瀚的爱。

## 66 True Happiness / 66 真正的福乐

===Original===
By Newton
I
Fix my heart and eyes on thine!
 

 What are other objects worth?
 

 But to see thy glory shine
 

 Is a heaven begun on earth:
 

 Trifles can no longer move﻿—
 

 Oh, I tread on all beside
 

 When I feel my Saviour’s love,
 

 And remember how he died!
II
Now my search is at an end,
 

 Now my wishes rove no more!
 

 Thus my moments I would spend,
 

 Love and wonder and adore:
 

 Jesus, source of excellence!
 

 All thy glorious love reveal;
 

 Kingdoms shall not bribe me hence
 

 While this happiness I feel.
III
Take my heart, ’tis all thine own,
 

 To thy will my spirit frame;
 

 Thou shalt reign, and thou alone,
 

 Over all I have or am:
 

 If a foolish thought shall dare
 

 To rebel against thy word,
 

 Slay it, Lord, and do not spare,
 

 Let it feel thy Spirit’s sword.
IV
Making thus the Lord my choice,
 

 I have nothing more to choose
 

 But to listen to thy voice,
 

 And my will in thine to lose:
 

 Thus, whatever may betide,
 

 I shall safe and happy be;
 

 Still content and satisfied,
 

 Having all in having thee.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
使我的心和眼定睛于你！
别的目标还有什么价值？
惟见你的荣耀闪耀，
是已在地上开始的天堂：
琐屑不能再使我动——
哦，当我感到我救主的爱，
并记念他怎样受死，
我便践踏一切身外之物！
二
如今我的搜寻已经到头，
如今我的愿望不再游荡！
我愿这样度过每一刻，
爱慕、惊奇并敬拜：
耶稣，一切卓越的源头！
愿你显明你一切的荣耀之爱；
国度也不能贿赂我离开
这幸福的感觉。
三
拿去我的心，它全然属你，
照你旨意塑造我的灵；
你要作王，且单单作王，
管理我所有的、我所是的：
若有愚妄的意念竟敢
叛逆你的话，
主啊，杀死它，不要留情，
让它感受你圣灵的剑。
四
既以主为我的选择，
我便再无可选，
只愿聆听你的声音，
让我的旨意消失在你的里：
这样，无论何事临到，
我都必安全而快乐；
仍然知足而满足，
拥有了一切，因拥有了你。

## 67 The Happy Debtor / 67 有福的欠债者

===Original===
By Newton
I
Ten thousand talents once I owed,
 

 And nothing had to pay;
 

 But Jesus freed me from the load,
 

 And wash’d my debt away.
II
Yet since the Lord forgave my sin,
 

 And blotted out my score,
 

 Much more indebted I have been
 

 Than e’er I was before.
III
My guilt is cancel’d quite, I know.
 

 And satisfaction made;
 

 But the vast debt of love I owe
 

 Can never be repaid.
IV
The love I owe for sin forgiven,
 

 For power to believe,
 

 For present peace and promised heaven,
 

 No angel can conceive.
V
That love of thine, thou sinner’s Friend!
 

 Witness thy bleeding heart!
 

 My little all can ne’er extend
 

 To pay a thousandth part.
VI
Nay, more; the poor returns I make,
 

 I first from thee obtain;
 

 And ’tis of grace that thou wilt take
 

 Such poor returns again.
VII
’Tis well﻿—it shall my glory be,
 

 (Let who will boast their store,)
 

 In time and to eternity,
 

 To owe thee more and more.31
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
我曾欠一万他连得，
却无分文可还；
但耶稣使我脱离重担，
把我的债洗除净尽。
二
然而主既赦免了我的罪，
涂抹了我的账目，
我反倒比从前
亏欠得更多更深。
三
我的罪债已完全取消，我知道，
赔偿也已作成；
但我所欠爱的巨债，
永远无法偿还。
四
为罪得赦所欠的爱，
为得着信心的能力，
为现在的平安、应许的天堂——
天使也无法测度。
五
你这罪人之友的爱！
你流血的心为证！
我微薄的一切永不能
偿付千分之一。
六
不，还有；我献上的
微薄回报，也是先从你领受；
你肯再收这样的
微薄回报，全是恩典。
七
这样甚好——愿这是我的荣耀，
（任凭谁夸自己的丰盛，）
在时间之内、直到永恒，
都越欠你越多。31

## VI Cautions / 六 警戒

## 68 The New Convert / 68 初信的人

===Original===
By Cowper
I
The new-born child of gospel grace,
 

 Like some fair tree when summer’s nigh,
 

 Beneath Emmanuel’s shining face,
 

 Lifts up his blooming branch on high.
II
No fears he feels, he sees no foes.
 

 No conflict yet his faith employs,
 

 Nor has he learn’d to whom he owes
 

 The strength and peace his soul enjoys.
III
But sin soon darts its cruel sting,
 

 And comforts sinking day by day;
 

 What seem’d his own, a self-fed spring,
 

 Proves but a brook that glides away.
IV
When Gideon arm’d his num’rous host,
 

 The Lord soon made his numbers less;
 

 And said, “Lest Isr’el vainly boast,
 

 My arm procured me this success.”
V
Thus will he bring our spirits down,
 

 And draw our ebbing comforts low,
 

 That, saved by grace, but not our own,
 

 We may not claim the praise we owe.
===Chinese===
库珀 作
一
福音恩典里新生的孩子，
像夏日将近的佳美树木，
在以马内利光照的脸下，
把他繁花的枝条高高扬起。
二
他不感到惧怕，也看不见仇敌，
他的信心尚未经历争战，
他还不晓得他所享受的
力量与平安当归给谁。
三
但罪很快射出残忍的毒刺，
安慰日渐消沉；
那貌似自涌的泉源，
不过是流逝的小溪。
四
基甸招聚大军的时候，
主很快削减他的数目；
并说：「免得以色列妄夸，
是我的膀臂为我成就了这胜利。」
五
他也要这样降卑我们的灵，
使我们消退的安慰落低，
使我们知道既靠恩典得救、非靠己力，
就不敢夺取当归于他的赞美。

## 69 True and False Comforts / 69 真安慰与假安慰

===Original===
By Cowper
I
God, whose favourable eye
 

 The sin-sick soul revives,
 

 Holy and heav’nly is the joy
 

 Thy shining presence gives.
II
Not such as hypocrites suppose,
 

 Who, with a graceless heart,
 

 Taste not of thee, but drink a dose
 

 Prepared by Satan’s art.
III
Intoxicating joys are theirs,
 

 Who, while they boast their light,
 

 And seem to soar above the stars,
 

 Are plunging into night.
IV
Lull’d in a soft and fatal sleep,
 

 They sin and yet rejoice;
 

 Were they indeed the Saviour’s sheep.
 

 Would they not hear his voice?
V
Be mine the comforts that reclaim
 

 The soul from Satan’s pow’r;
 

 That make me blush for what I am,
 

 And hate my sin the more.
VI
’Tis joy enough, my All in All,
 

 At thy dear feet to lie;
 

 Thou wilt not let me lower fall,
 

 And none can higher fly.
===Chinese===
库珀 作
一
神啊，你恩眷的眼
使患罪病的灵魂苏醒，
你容光同在所赐的
喜乐，圣洁而属天。
二
不像假冒为善者所以为的，
他们怀着无恩典的心，
尝的不是你，而是饮下一剂
撒但诡计所调的药。
三
令他们迷醉的喜乐属于他们，
他们一面夸口自己的光，
似乎翱翔在众星之上，
却正坠入黑夜。
四
被哄入柔软致命的沉睡，
他们犯罪却仍欢喜；
他们若真是救主的羊，
岂会不听他的声音？
五
愿我所蒙的安慰是那使人回转、
救灵魂脱离撒但权势的；
是那使我为我如今的样子羞愧、
使我更恨恶我的罪的。
六
这喜乐已经足够，我的一切中之一切，
就是躺卧在你亲爱的脚前；
你必不容我坠得更低，
也没有人能飞得更高。

## 70 True and False Zeal / 70 真热心与假热心

===Original===
By Newton
I
Zeal is that pure and heav’nly flame
 

 The fire of love supplies;
 

 While that which often bears the name
 

 Is self in a disguise.
II
True zeal is merciful and mild,
 

 Can pity and forbear;
 

 The false is headstrong, fierce, and wild,
 

 And breathes revenge and war.
III
While zeal for truth the Christian warms,
 

 He knows the worth of peace;
 

 But self contends for names and forms,
 

 Its party to increase.
IV
Zeal has attained its highest aim,
 

 Its end is satisfied,
 

 If sinners love the Saviour’s name,
 

 Nor seeks it aught beside.
V
But self, however well employ’d,
 

 Has its own ends in view;
 

 And says, as boasting Jehu cried,
 

 “Come, see what I can do.”
VI
Self may its poor reward obtain,
 

 And be applauded here;
 

 But zeal the best applause will gain,
 

 When Jesus shall appear.
VII
Dear Lord! the idol self dethrone,
 

 And from our hearts remove;
 

 And let no zeal by us be shown
 

 But that which springs from love.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
热心是那纯净属天的火焰，
由爱火所供应；
而那常顶着热心之名的，
往往是变装的自恃。
二
真热心满有怜悯与温柔，
能怜恤也能宽容；
假热心却任性、凶猛、狂野，
吐出复仇与争战。
三
热心为真理温暖基督徒时，
他知道平安的价值；
但自恃却为名目和形式争竞，
只为壮大自己的党派。
四
热心若达到它至高的目的，
它的心愿便已满足，
只要罪人爱救主的名；
此外它别无所求。
五
但自恃无论怎样善用，
都有自己的目的；
并像自夸的耶户那样喊说：
「来看我能做什么。」
六
自恃或能得它微薄的报偿，
在地上受掌声；
但热心要得最好的赞美，
当耶稣显现之时。
七
亲爱的主！求你废去自恃这偶像，
把它从我们心中除掉；
愿我们不再显出别样的热心，
只显出从爱而发的。

## 71 A Living and a Dead Faith / 71 活的信心与死的信心

===Original===
By Cowper
I
The Lord receives his highest praise
 

 From humble minds and hearts sincere;
 

 While all the loud professor says
 

 Offends the righteous Judge’s ear.
II
To walk as children of the day,
 

 To mark the precept’s holy light,
 

 To wage the warfare, watch, and pray,
 

 Show who are pleasing in his sight.
III
Not words alone it cost the Lord
 

 To purchase pardon for his own;
 

 Nor will a soul by grace restored
 

 Return the Saviour words alone.
IV
With golden bells the priestly vest,
 

 And rich pomegranates border’d round,
 

 The need of holiness express’d,
 

 And call’d for fruit as well as sound.
V
Easy, indeed, it were to reach
 

 A mansion in the courts above,
 

 If swelling words and fluent speech
 

 Might serve instead of faith and love.
VI
But none shall gain the blissful place
 

 Or God’s unclouded glory see,
 

 Who talks of free and sov’reign grace,
 

 Unless that grace has made him free!
===Chinese===
库珀 作
一
主从谦卑的心思和真诚的心
领受他最高的赞美；
而高声的表白者所说的一切，
只冒犯公义审判者的耳。
二
像白昼之子那样行，
留意诫命圣洁的光，
打那美好的仗、警醒、祷告，
才显明谁在他眼中蒙喜悦。
三
主买赎他自己的子民，
并非单用言语；
蒙恩典恢复的灵魂，
也不会只用言语回报救主。
四
祭司的圣衣上金铃
和饱满的石榴环绕，
正表明圣洁的必要，
要求果子也要求声响。
五
若膨胀的言语和流利的讲说
可以代替信心与爱心，
那升到天上院宇得居一处，
实在轻而易举。
六
但没有人能得着那福地、
见神无云的荣耀，
若他谈论白白而主权的恩典，
那恩典却未曾使他得自由！

## 72 Abuse of the Gospel / 72 滥用福音

===Original===
By Cowper
I
Too many, Lord, abuse thy grace,
 

 In this licentious day;
 

 And while they boast they see thy face,
 

 They turn their own away.
II
Thy book displays a gracious light.
 

 That can the blind restore;
 

 But these are dazzled by the sight,
 

 And blinded still the more.
III
The pardon such presume upon,
 

 They do not beg but steal;
 

 And when they plead it at thy throne,
 

 Oh! where’s the Spirit’s seal?
IV
Was it for this, ye lawless tribe,
 

 The dear Redeemer bled?
 

 Is this the grace the saints imbibe
 

 From Christ the living head?
V
Ah, Lord! we know thy chosen few
 

 Are fed with heavenly fare;
 

 But these, the wretched husks they chew
 

 Proclaim them what they are.
VI
The liberty our hearts implore,
 

 Is not to live in sin;
 

 But still to wait at Wisdom’s door,
 

 ‘Till Mercy calls us in.
===Chinese===
库珀 作
一
主啊，太多人滥用你的恩典，
在这放纵的日子；
他们一面夸口见你的面，
一面转开自己的脸。
二
你的书展示恩惠之光，
能使瞎眼复原；
但这些人被光晃得目眩，
反而瞎得更深。
三
这样的人擅自假定的赦免，
不是求来而是偷来；
当他们在你宝座前这样恳求时，
哦，圣灵的印记在哪里？
四
无法无天的族类啊，
亲爱的救赎主流血，
难道是为这个？
圣徒从基督——这活的首——
所领受的恩典，难道就是这个？
五
啊，主啊！我们知道你拣选的少数人
以天粮喂养；
但这些人所咀嚼的可怜秕糠，
正表明他们的真相。
六
我们心所渴慕的自由，
不是要活在罪中；
而是仍等候在智慧门前，
直到怜悯召我们进去。

## 73 The Narrow Way / 73 窄路

===Original===
By Cowper
I
What thousands never knew the road!
 

 What thousands hate it when ’tis known!
 

 None but the chosen tribes of God
 

 Will seek or choose it for their own.
II
A thousand ways in ruin end,
 

 One only leads to joys on high;
 

 By that my willing steps ascend,
 

 Pleased with a journey to the sky.
III
No more I ask or hope to find
 

 Delight or happiness below;
 

 Sorrow may well possess the mind
 

 That feeds where thorns and thistles grow.
IV
The joy that fades is not for me,
 

 I seek immortal joys above;
 

 There glory without end shall be
 

 The bright reward of faith and love.
V
Cleave to the world, ye sordid worms,
 

 Contented lick your native dust;
 

 But God shall fight with all his storms,
 

 Against the idol of your trust.
===Chinese===
库珀 作
一
多少人从未认识这路！
多少人知道了却恨恶！
惟有神拣选的支派
才寻求或选它为己有。
二
千条路都以毁灭告终，
惟有一条通往高天之乐；
我甘心的脚步沿它攀升，
欣喜于这通往天空的旅程。
三
我不再求、也不再指望
在下面得着快乐或幸福；
忧愁尽可占据那
在荆棘蒺藜间觅食的心。
四
会消褪的欢乐不是为我，
我寻求上面不朽的喜乐；
在那里荣耀将无止境，
作信心与爱心光明的赏赐。
五
卑贱的虫啊，紧贴世界吧，
知足地舔你们的本土尘土；
但神必以他一切的风暴
攻击你们所信靠的偶像。

## 74 Dependence / 74 依靠

===Original===
By Cowper
I
To keep the lamp alive,
 

 With oil we fill the bowl;
 

 ’Tis water makes the willow thrive,
 

 And grace that feeds the soul.
II
The Lord’s unsparing hand
 

 Supplies the living stream;
 

 It is not at our own command,
 

 But still derived from him.
III
Beware of Peter’s word,
 

 Nor confidently say,
 

 “I never will deny thee, Lord,”
 

 But grant I never may.
IV
Man’s wisdom is to seek
 

 His strength in God alone;
 

 And even an angel would be weak
 

 Who trusted in his own.
V
Retreat beneath his wings,
 

 And in his grace confide;
 

 This more exalts the King of kings
 

 Than all your works beside.
VI
In Jesus is our store,
 

 Grace issues from his throne;
 

 Whoever says, “I want no more,”
 

 Confesses he has none.
===Chinese===
库珀 作
一
要使灯常明，
我们用油盛满灯盏；
使柳树茂盛的是水，
滋养灵魂的是恩典。
二
主不吝惜的手
供应活水的溪流；
这并不随我们自己的吩咐，
而仍是单单从他领受。
三
要当心彼得的话，
不要自信地说：
「我永不否认你，主，」
只求让我永不致如此。
四
人的智慧在于
单单在神里寻求力量；
连天使若信靠自己，
也会软弱。
五
退到他的翅膀之下，
安息在他的恩典里；
这比你们一切的作为
更尊崇万王之王。
六
我们的库房在耶稣里，
恩典从他宝座发出；
谁若说「我不再需要」，
便是承认自己一无所有。

## 75 Not of Works / 75 不是出于行为

===Original===
By Cowper
I
Grace, triumphant in the throne,
 

 Scorns a rival, reigns alone;
 

 Come and bow beneath her sway,
 

 Cast your idol-works away.
 

 Works of man, when made his plea,
 

 Never shall accepted be;
 

 Fruits of pride (vain-glorious worm)
 

 Are the best he can perform.
II
Self, the god his soul adores,
 

 Influences all his pow’rs;
 

 Jesus is a slighted name,
 

 Self-advancement all his aim:
 

 But when God the Judge shall come,
 

 To pronounce the final doom,
 

 Then for rocks and hills to hide
 

 All his works and all his pride!
III
Still the boasting heart replies,
 

 What, the worthy and the wise,
 

 Friends to temperance and peace,
 

 Have not these a righteousness?
 

 Banish every vain pretence
 

 Built on human excellence;
 

 Perish every thing in man,
 

 But the grace that never can.
===Chinese===
库珀 作
一
恩典在宝座上得胜，
藐视对手，独自作王；
来吧，俯伏在她权下，
抛去你们偶像的善工。
人的善工作为他的恳求，
永不会蒙悦纳；
骄傲的果子（虚妄的虫啊）
已是他所能行的至好。
二
自恃，他灵魂崇拜的神，
支配他一切的能力；
耶稣是被轻慢的名，
自求高升是他全部的目标：
但当神，那审判者来临时，
要宣告最后的判决，
那时只求山岩遮盖
他一切的工和他一切的骄傲！
三
自夸的心仍回答说，
什么，那配得的人、有智慧的人，
节制与和平之友，
难道他们没有义吗？
要驱逐一切建立在
人的卓越上的虚妄托辞；
愿人身上的一切都朽坏，
只有那不能朽坏的恩典存留。

## 76 Sin’s Deceit / 76 罪的欺骗

===Original===
By Newton
I
Sin, when view’d by Scripture light,
 

 Is a horrid, hateful sight;
 

 But when seen in Satan’s glass,
 

 Then it wears a pleasing face.
II
When the gospel-trumpet sounds,
 

 When I think how grace abounds,
 

 When I feel sweet peace within,
 

 Then I’d rather die than sin.
III
When the cross I view by faith,
 

 Sin is madness, poison, death:
 

 Tempt me not, ’tis all in vain,
 

 Sure I ne’er can yield again.
IV
Satan for a while debarr’d,
 

 When he finds me off my guard,
 

 Puts his glass before my eyes,
 

 Quickly other thoughts arise.
V
What before excited fears,
 

 Rather pleasing now appears;
 

 If I sin, it seems so small,
 

 Or perhaps no sin at all.
VI
Often thus through sin’s deceit,
 

 Grief and shame and loss I meet;
 

 Like a fish, my soul mistook,
 

 Saw the bait, but not the hook.
VII
O my Lord, what shall I say?
 

 How can I presume to pray?
 

 Not a word have I to plead,
 

 Sins like mine are black indeed.
VIII
Made by past experience wise,
 

 Let me learn thy word to prize;
 

 Taught by what I’ve felt before,
 

 Let me Satan’s glass abhor.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
罪，在圣经光照下看，
是一幅可怖可憎的景象；
但经撒但的镜子里看，
便戴着讨喜的面容。
二
当福音号角吹响，
当我想起恩典何等丰盛，
当我感到内里甘甜的平安，
我宁死也不肯犯罪。
三
当我凭信心仰望十架，
罪便是癫狂、是毒药、是死亡：
不要试探我，那是全然徒然，
我确实永不会再屈服。
四
撒但一时被拒于门外，
见我不加防备，
便把它的镜子举到我眼前，
霎时别的意念升起。
五
从前激起惧怕的，
如今倒显得可悦；
我若犯罪，似乎微不足道，
或者根本不算罪。
六
常常这样因罪的欺骗，
我遭遇忧愁羞愧与亏损；
我的灵魂像一条鱼认错了，
看见饵，却看不见钩。
七
哦我的主，我还有什么可说？
我怎敢再祈求？
我没有一言可恳求，
像我这样的罪确实黑。
八
既由过去的经历变聪明，
愿我学会珍视你的话；
既受教于从前所感受的，
愿我憎恶撒但的镜子。

## 77 Are There Few That Shall Be Saved? / 77 得救的人少吗？

===Original===
By Newton
I
Destruction’s dangerous road
 

 What multitudes pursue,
 

 While that which leads the soul to God
 

 Is known or sought by few.
II
Believers enter in
 

 By Christ, the living gate;
 

 But they who will not leave their sin,
 

 Complain it is too strait.
III
If self must be denied,
 

 And sin forsaken quite,
 

 They rather choose the way that’s wide,
